A put copies the text it puts, as before (5 ms for 10 MB). `core/tests/vi_15_mem.rs` holds the line: nine such deletes grow the process by under 30 MB (110 MB when a register holds its own copy).
## Performance (measured on a release build)

- **PERF-1** [P3][M1] `sim --plain +q` opens and closes a 10,000-line file within 5 ms (median of hyperfine) — B
  - **Decided** (P3): sim needs a terminal, so `scripts/bench.sh` runs hyperfine inside a private tmux when it is installed. Otherwise `scripts/pty_bench.py` spawns the release binary in a pty; the median it reports includes starting the process from Python, and the test (`perf_1_bench_startup_under_5ms`) asserts that raw median, so it is stricter than hyperfine's.
  - **Decided** (P3): `sim --plain +q` quits before it draws, so it never waits for the terminal's color (VIS-4): the first frame does, for at most 3 ms when the terminal never answers (the wait counts from the query, which goes out once the frame has been built; the rest of the setup overlaps it). The harness measures both: `+q` (the number the test asserts), and the time to the first frame with the pty answering the query like a terminal and with it staying silent. The harness waits for the process with a blocking waitpid, because Python's `Popen.wait(timeout=...)` polls with sleeps of 0.5, 1, 2, 4 ms... and rounds every time up to 3.5, 7.5 or 15.5 ms. Measured on the 10,000-line file (release, `/bin/true` 0.2 ms): `+q` 2.4 ms before M5 and 2.5 ms now; first frame 2.1 ms before M5, 2.4 ms with an answer and 4.9 ms with none. Since VIS-4 stopped `+q` from asking, and moved the frame's build before the query (it is known only then that the keys do not quit), the pty harness plays a terminal in order, answering both the OSC 11 query and the device attributes request, and measures the quit too: `:q` to the exit after the first frame. On the same file (release, `/bin/true` 0.2 ms): `+q` 2.0 ms; first frame 1.9 ms with the terminal answering everything, 4.7 ms with one that answers all but OSC 11, 5.6 ms with one that answers nothing (4.7 ms before, when the frame was built within the 3 ms); `:q` to the exit 0.5 ms with the terminal answering everything (the color is in, nothing to wait for) and with one that answers all but OSC 11 (a round trip), and 101 ms with one that answers nothing (the 100 ms bound; the quit used to take 0.5 ms and leave the answer for the shell). Run to run these move by a few tenths of a millisecond; the interleaved runs of the old and the new binary (`scripts/pty_bench.py startup`) differ by at most 0.5 ms.
  - **Decided** (P3): measured as `sim --plain +q` (2026-10-17, release build, AMD Ryzen 9 9950X, load average about 10): the median is 3.44 ms (0.30 ms for `/bin/true` the same way, so 3.13 ms is sim's), the first frame comes 4.37 ms after the start (3.82 ms with a terminal that answers nothing) and the quit after `:q` takes 1.18 ms (1.32 ms without OSC 11), or the 100 ms bound with a terminal that answers nothing. A quit that was never answered because nothing was asked (`+q`) waits for nothing: the shell waits for a terminal only when it has asked it for something (E2E `vis_4_a_quit_at_startup_...`, `vis_4_a_quit_at_startup_waits_for_no_terminal_it_did_not_ask`; the first version of the fold waited the 100 ms for it, and `+q` took 104 ms).
- **PERF-2** [P3][M1] On a 100,000-line file, handling one key (motion, insert, delete, undo) and building the Frame take at most 1 ms at p99 — B
  - **Decided** (P3): the time of a key is `App::update` plus `Term::draw` into memory, i.e. everything before the write to the terminal, measured on 80x24 and 240x60 screens.
  - **Decided** (P3): marks are shared with the undo steps, and an edit that moves them copies the list as counts and pointers, never as clones of their words (a mark that paints its whole line holds no words at all). Measured on 100,000 lines after a rewrite of every line (100,000 marks, release): an edit that moves the marks takes about 0.2 ms through `App::update` (2.5 ms when every mark was cloned), a key that does not about 0.1 ms. Every such edit copies the list, so a list of more than 1,024 marks is kept by the last 4 undo steps only (16 copies of 100,000 marks, 3 MB each, fall out of the cache and cost 160 µs against 70 µs for 4, and a session of edits would hold gigabytes); undo further back does not bring those marks back.
  - **Decided** (P3): a command of many edits copies the list of marks once, not for each edit: the list is copied only when an undo step holds it, and `:s` goes through its lines from the last to the first, so the marks of the lines still to do keep their places. Measured on 100,000 lines that all carry a mark (a rewrite by an AI), `:%s/beta/gamma/` took 3.8 s, now 0.12 s (release).
- **PERF-3** [P3][M1] After one character is inserted, only that line is sent to the terminal again — U (bytes written)
  - **Decided** (P2): plus the status line, whose `line:col` changed, and the cursor position.
  - **Decided** (P3): a frame that rewrites anything is wrapped in synchronized output (`CSI ? 2026 h` … `CSI ? 2026 l`), so a terminal that supports it never shows half of a frame; terminals that don't know it ignore it. A frame that only moves the cursor is sent as it is.
- **PERF-4** [P3][M1] On a 100,000-line file, a search that matches nothing (the worst case) finishes within 50 ms — B
  - **Decided** (P3): a line that lacks a literal the pattern requires (the longest plain run, e.g. `foo` in `foo\d+bar`) is skipped without running the regex, because regex-lite does about 20 ns a byte and a 100,000-line file is several MB.
  - **Decided** (P3): regex-lite stays, and the worst case is held by running it in parallel. A buffer of 512 KiB or more is searched in 8 chunks of lines (a fixed number, so the result never depends on the machine) on `std::thread::scope` threads that only read the text; the first chunk in search order that has a match wins, so the match is exactly the one a single thread finds. The first 256 lines run on one thread before any starts, so a match near the cursor costs no thread. 8 threads bring a regex with no useful literal on 7.65 MB from about 150 ms to about 24 ms, on a machine with 8 or more cores.
- **PERF-5** [P4][M1] The release binary is at most 5 MB (strip, LTO), the editor's syntax set included. It was at most 2 MB when the core was a program of its own, and that program was 1.2 MB (measured at 5dc7c46); the core is a library now, and the one binary is the editor's. With a 10 MB file open, RSS is at most 50 MB — B
  - **Decided** (P4): the test asserts the peak (VmHWM) after the first frame, which is never lower than the RSS at that moment. It runs on Linux only.
  - **Decided** (P4): measured as `sim --plain` with a 10.8 MB file open (2026-10-17, release build): VmRSS 17.2 MB and peak (VmHWM) 26.3 MB, under 50 MB; the release binary is 4.7 MB (4,712,560 bytes at the time of writing).
  - **Decided** (P4): registers (VI-15, 2026-10-18) took the release binary from 5,224,080 to 5,236,936 bytes, under 5,242,880 with 5,944 to spare. The first version stored them in a `BTreeMap` and sorted them for `:registers`: 5,249,608 bytes, 6,728 over. Keeping a list in the order of the names, and no sort, took 5.2 KB of the generic sort out; the rest is the registers' code and 779 bytes of the key table's words.

## Validation

- **VAL-1** [P2][M6] With tools/perception (not counted in src/), show 20 trials of a 60-line code screen in which exactly one word changed, and record the time to answer the number of the changed line and whether the answer was right. Condition A is calm, the core's display; condition B is syntax highlighting with a line-level diff background (close to the defaults of common editors). Output medians and 95% bootstrap confidence intervals — B

# Part two — the editor

## The tree panel

- **TREE-1** [P2][S1] `sim` without arguments, or `sim {dir}`, opens with the tree of that directory (the current one by default) in a panel on the left, focused. `sim {file}` (and `file:line[:col]`) opens the file with the panel closed. The panel is a third of the screen, at most 32 columns — I
  - **Decided** (P2): the panel is `min(32, w/3)` columns, followed by one blank column; narrower than 4 columns it is not drawn. A row is `indent 2 per depth`, then `▸ `/`▾ ` and `name/` for a directory, two spaces and `name` for a file, cut to the panel's width in columns (a wide char counts 2). The panel's cursor row is underlined while the panel has the focus (the terminal's cursor sits on it), and nothing else in the panel is styled. The status line is across the whole width: the core's App cuts its status line to its own width, so when that line could be cut, or the command line is being typed, sim has the App render once more at the full width and takes the last row (the App's scroll state is put back: the first row, the skipped rows, centering and size; the command line's own scroll, `vi.off`, is kept by sim apart, because every update of the App renders its narrower frame and moves it, so the line scrolls at the width of the screen, VI-12).
  - **Decided** (P2): `sim {file}` reads the tree of the current directory too, for Ctrl-n later. A file opened from the tree or by `:e` is named by its path relative to the current directory when it lies below it, else by its absolute path; that name is what the status line shows and what the App, its snapshots and its saves use.
  - **Decided** (P1): a file name can hold anything, so the panel never sends one raw (SAFE-8's rule for names): control chars, bidirectional controls, U+200B and U+FEFF are shown as U+FFFD.
  - **Decided** (P1): a name that is not UTF-8 cannot be opened, because every path in sim is text (the core's App, its saves and snapshots take a `String`): the listings show it with U+FFFD, and what was listed is not the file's name. Opening it from the panel, the search or `:e` as a new file would show an empty buffer for a file that has text, and a save would create a second file under the name with U+FFFD. The shell opens a name with U+FFFD that is no file as a read-only buffer that says `the name is not valid UTF-8, so the file cannot be opened` (a file that does have such a name in valid UTF-8 opens as any); on the command line the name is a usage error, exit 2 (`env::args` would panic). Nothing is written under another name, and nobody's text is touched (P1).
  - **Decided** (P2): with no file open the editor area shows five help lines (Ctrl-n tree, Enter open, Tab next file, q close, :qa quit all) in the secondary gray, and the status line is the selected path and `TREE` (the root when the panel has no focus). `q` in the text with no file open quits with 0; in the panel `q` closes the panel (TREE-4), so `q` twice leaves sim from the panel.
  - **Decided** (P4): the event loop runs the core's Apps, many of them, and needs crossterm (events, size), signal-hook (SIGTERM, SIGHUP) and unicode-width (columns) for it, at the versions and features the core's terminal code has, so the build gains no code. The snapshots go to the one state directory (STATE-1). The tree and the file are read on the main thread like the core does for `:e`; directory reads, saves and snapshots are threads. What the terminal sends goes through the core's `term::convert_all` (Alt+x is Esc, then x; VI-9), and Shift-Tab, which the core has no key for, is sim's.
  - **Decided** (P2): the status line keeps its state words, whatever the length of the path in front of them (a long `$TMPDIR`, a deep tree, an absolute path from `git commit`): the TREE line is the selected path (a directory's with its `/`) and `  TREE`, and the root shown alone when no file is open is made to fit the same way. A path is made to fit by the core's own `view::shorten` (made `pub` for this, nothing else in the core changed): `…/` and as many of its last components as fit, the file name at least; sim cuts a name that is wider than the room from the left (`layout::short`), and `layout::stated` keeps the state words whole, as the core's status line does for its path. A message that names a directory names it as the panel does, below the root (`cannot read gone: Permission denied`), and `io::read_dir` answers with the reason alone.
- **TREE-2** [P2][S1] Ctrl-n opens and focuses the panel, or closes it when it already has focus (Neo-tree's key in the author's nvim). Esc in the panel gives the focus back to the text and leaves the panel open — U
  - **Decided** (P2): Ctrl-n is sim's in every mode (Insert and the command line too) and never reaches the App. With the panel open but not focused it focuses it. Opening the panel reads the root and every open directory again, so files made meanwhile show; the cursor stays on its entry.
- **TREE-3** [P2][S1] The order never changes: directories first, then files, each by name (case-insensitive, runs of digits compared as numbers). `.git` is not shown; everything else is, dotfiles included. Expanding or collapsing never moves what is above it, and the expansion stays as you left it — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the order of names is a total order, so it never depends on the order a listing arrives in. A name is read as tokens: a run of ASCII digits is one token that sorts where `0` would, by its value (any length, leading zeros ignored), anything else is one char, lowercased. Names are compared token by token; names equal that way (`a01` and `a1`, `A` and `a`) are ordered by their bytes. `.git` is hidden by name, whether it is a directory or a file (a worktree has a `.git` file).
  - **Decided** (P4): every node keeps the number of rows of its visible subtree, so a window of rows is found by skipping whole subtrees: the work is the rows asked for plus the siblings along the way, never the size of the tree. Rows carry their path relative to the root; an open directory whose listing has not arrived shows nothing below it.
- **TREE-4** [P2][S1] Keys in the panel: `j k` and the arrows move; `l`, Right and Enter expand a directory or open a file (the focus moves to the text); `h` and Left collapse a directory, or go to its parent; `gg G Ctrl-d Ctrl-u PageUp PageDown` move as in the core; `-` collapses everything; `q` closes the panel — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the data side of the keys is in `tree.rs`: `row_of(path)` (none when a directory above it is closed), `parent_row(row)` (none at the top), `expand` (true when the listing is still to be read), `collapse`, `collapse_all` (the root stays open, listings that arrived are kept). The keys themselves come with the event loop.
  - **Decided** (P5): `l`, Right and Enter on an open directory do nothing. Ctrl-d and Ctrl-u move half the panel's height, PageUp and PageDown all of it; a `g` waits for a second one and any other key forgets it. `-` leaves the cursor on the top-level entry that held it. Tab and Shift-Tab (BUF-2) work from the panel too; Paste does nothing there.
- **TREE-5** [P4][S1] Directories are read in the background; entries appear when they arrive, without moving anything above them — I
  - **Decided** (P4): `insert_listing(dir, entries)` stores a listing whenever it arrives, whether or not `dir` is still expanded, and says false for a directory the tree does not know. A second listing for the same directory refreshes it: entries that stay keep their expansion and their own listing, a name that changes kind is a new entry. Reading a directory is `io::read_dir`: a symlink to a directory counts as a directory unless it leads to the directory itself or one above it (a loop); a link that cannot be followed is a file. Loops through two siblings are not detected: they cost one expansion each, never a walk. The background thread comes with the event loop.
  - **Decided** (P2): the cursor and the first row stay on their entries (found by path) whatever a listing puts above them. A directory that cannot be read stays open and empty, and the status line says why.
  - **Decided** (P2): the reads of directories run in threads of their own, so two reads of one directory (the panel opens twice in a row, a directory is expanded while it is read again) can end in the other order, and the older one would put a listing that is gone over the new one, to stay until the next read. A read takes a ticket when it starts (`io::Reads`), and its answer is sent only if no read of that directory that started later has sent its own; answers in order are all sent, and other directories are free.
  - **Decided** (P4): `io::read_dir` puts the listing in the order of the tree (`tree::sort`) in the thread that reads, and `insert_listing` sorts again, which costs one pass over what is already in order: sorting a directory of 100,000 entries on the main thread took 0.8 s (500,000: 4.3 s), measured on a release build, a key that waits for that; with the sort in the thread, the main thread's part is 46 ms (235 ms), once for each listing.

## Several files

- **BUF-1** [P1][S1] Each open file is a complete core App: its own undo, snapshots, watcher and saves. Opening a file that is already open switches to it — I
  - **Decided** (P1): sim's events and effects are the core's, tagged with the id of the file they belong to (ids are never reused, so a late answer for a closed file is dropped). There is one save thread and one journal thread for all files, a watcher for each, and `Sim::update` gives every file's App the events the core's loop would: ticks, signals, disk changes, save results. The shell starts watching before it reads, then looks for the snapshot a dead session left, and the first keys (`+cmd`, `file:line`) are typed after the recovery, as in the core. The shell does for each file what the core's main does for its one: the watcher's thread makes the hash of what it read (`Disk { hash }`, so no key waits for hashing, P3), and an App's `Effect::Watch` (asked for after every save's answer, DISK-1) replaces that file's watcher by one whose first read comes before the debounced ones.
  - **Decided** (P1): on the core's M5 every disk read is numbered (DISK-1), per file, as in the core's main: the number is taken from `file::seq()` when a read starts. A watcher's reads are numbered by `watch` itself (the first read of a new watcher, which follows every save's answer, included) and sim passes the number on in `Event::Disk`; `Effect::Open` (`:e!`, a reload) takes it before its read and carries it in `Event::Opened`; the save thread numbers each answer, `Saved` and `SaveConflict`, when the save is done. The first load of a file (`Opened`) is not numbered, as in the core. Each App keeps the newest number it took in and drops an older read, so a read that began before a save's answer cannot undo it, in any file.
  - **Decided** (P1): on the core's M4 the filters, the hook and the places hold per file. `Effect::Spawn` runs `sh -c` in its own process group, one running filter for each file at most (the App allows one), and its answer comes back as `Event::FilterDone` tagged with the file's id, so it finds its file wherever the user is; `Effect::Kill` stops that file's group; a closed file's group is stopped, and so are all groups at exit; an id that is not open drops the answer. `SI_ON_NORMAL` (VI-11) is given to every App (`app.hook`), and every App has the working directory (`app.cwd`). Where a file was closed (CLI-6) is looked up with the file (`Opened.place`) and given to the App as `Event::Place` unless keys were typed at startup (`+cmd`, `file:line`: for the first file only); `Effect::Open` of a reload asks the journal thread, whose answer is tagged with the id; `Effect::Remember` is a job of the journal thread, which reads the places again before it saves them. A signal, `:cq` and `:qa` remember the place of every file, as the quit of one file does. A tick that makes an App draw (the seconds of its running filter, FILT-1) draws the screen again; one that changes nothing draws nothing.
  - **Decided** (P1): `:e name` opens another file and never replaces this one, so it is not refused when this file is modified (`:e! name` is the same, and so is `:%e name`, `:1e name` or `:.,$e name`: the core reads a range before `:e` and ignores it, and would have replaced this file by the other, leaving the buffer with this file's journal key and the other's text); `:e` and `:e!` without a name reload this file by the core's rules. A directory named by `:e` is revealed in the panel (the panel opens, focused, with it expanded and under the cursor, reading what is not there yet); outside the tree's root it says so. A file is the same file when its journal key (its canonical path) is the same, whatever its name: opening a second name of an open file shows the first, because two buffers on one key would overwrite each other's snapshots.
  - **Decided** (P1): a reload (`Effect::Open`) silences the file's watcher before it takes the number of its read, as the core's shell does: a `Disk` carries no file name, and the old watcher's read of the file the reload leaves (of the old target, when a link moved), begun after that number was taken, would reach the App after the `Opened` and be merged into the buffer of the file it now has as news of the disk. The App asks for a watch of the file it has after every answer of a reload (the core's DISK-1), so the silence is only for what was on its way. Checked with a link moved from a file that is rewritten every 10 ms to another, and a reload (E2E).
  - **Decided** (P1): the shell starts watching a name before it reads it (so that no change slips in between), and cannot know yet that it is a directory: the answer for a directory, `SimEvent::Dir(id, path)`, names the id, and Sim answers it with `Close(id)`, so that the watcher (an inotify watch and a thread) does not stay for every directory opened by `:e`, Enter or a link that leads to one. A watcher or a filter of an id that is not open is a leak, which the model (`tests/buf_model.rs`) checks after every event.
  - **Decided** (P1): a file whose snapshot was recovered keeps that snapshot until its own is on disk, as in the core; the journal thread keeps one such snapshot for each file.
  - **Decided** (P1): the state directory of snapshots and recovery copies is the core's, by the core's own functions, so that the rule has one home: `journal::state_dir` (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/sim`, else `$HOME/.local/state/sim`, a relative path ignored, none otherwise, never `/tmp`), `journal::write` and `file::save` (they make it 0700 and use it only if it is a real directory, not a symlink, owned by the user, with no access for group or others) and `journal::find` (it looks only at a private directory, and only at files owned by the user, so a snapshot of someone else's is never read). With no directory, or one that is not private, snapshots are off: none is written, none is looked for, and a save that needs a recovery copy (SAFE-2's in-place save) fails instead of writing it anywhere else; the journal thread says why once, on the status line, when the first snapshot cannot be written (`cannot write snapshots: {why}`), as in the core. sim's own copies of these (`snapshot_dir`, `private`, `foreign`, a dead path for the save thread) are gone; the history, the look and the icons are in the same directory, by the same rule (MARK-2, STATE-1). Checked with threads on a private, an open and a linked directory, and on none; the owner test itself is the core's (a test cannot make a file for another user).
- **BUF-2** [P2][S1] Tab and Shift-Tab switch to the next and the previous open file (BufferLine's keys in the author's nvim). With more than one file open, the status line shows its position, e.g. `2/5` — U
  - **Decided** (P5): the keys wrap round. They are sim's when the current App is in Normal mode with nothing pending (a count, an operator, a prefix), also while the panel has the focus; in any other state Tab goes to the App (a tab in Insert mode, a space on the command line) and Shift-Tab, which the core has no key for, is dropped. The position is added after the status text or the message, but not on the command line.
  - **Decided** (P2): the position `2/5` is never cut: the status (the App's, a message, the TREE line, the search) is cut to what the position leaves of the width, and the App is asked to lay its status out at that width (the same second render as for the command line), so that its own state words are the ones kept.
- **BUF-3** [P1][S1] `:q`, `q`, `ZQ`, `:x`, `ZZ` and `:wq` close the current file with the core's rules; closing the last one exits. `:qa` exits when no file is modified and otherwise names the modified ones; `:qa!` discards them all; `:wa` saves every modified file. The exit code follows the core's (`:cq` is 1) — I
  - **Decided** (P1): an App's `Quit(0)` closes that file (it has asked the journal to forget the file's snapshot already); the file after it shows, the one before if it was the last; closing the last one exits with 0. Any other code is `:cq`: sim ends with it at once, the file `:cq` was typed in is given up as in the core, and every other modified file gets a last snapshot as a signal would ask for, which stays. A signal gives every file the signal, so each modified file's snapshot is asked for, and sim exits with 128 + the signal.
  - **Decided** (P1): `:qa`, `:qall`, `:qa!`, `:wa` and `:wall` are taken when Enter submits them, before the App sees the line. `:qa` with modified files says `no write since last change in a.rs, b.rs (:qa! to discard)` and does not quit; `:qa!` and a clean `:qa` ask the journal to forget every file's snapshot and exit with 0. `:wa` saves each modified file as Ctrl-s does (so each is checked against its own disk, SAFE-5); each file tells its result in its own status line, and `no modified files` when there is none. `:wqa` and `:xa` are not built.
  - **Decided** (P1): SAFE-7 holds for each file. `:qa` stays while a file is modified, conflict or not, and names the modified ones (the conflict is on the disk, where `:q!` and `:qa!` leave it); `:qa!` quits with a conflict open and writes nothing. `:wa` leaves out the modified files that conflict with the disk (`App::diverged`) and saves the others as before; it names the left-out ones on the status line, `not saved: conflicts with the disk in a.rs (:w! keeps yours, :e! takes the disk's)`. Each of those two ways out copies the version it gives up first, as the core's do (SAFE-7).

## Finding

- **FIND-1** [P2][S2] `/` in the panel searches every file under the root, not only the expanded ones, as you type (search-directed navigation): the cursor goes to the best match, expanding the directories on its way; every match and the directories leading to it are bold, everything else is dimmed; nothing is hidden or reordered. `n` / `N` go to the next / previous match, Enter opens, Esc ends the search and restores the expansion — U
  - **Decided** (P2): a path matches when the query's chars occur in it in order; smartcase applies to the whole query (one uppercase letter makes it exact); an empty query matches nothing. The match is taken in the file name when the query occurs there, else in the whole path, and is the tightest one that ends first (the earliest end, then the latest chars before it). This is a heuristic, not the best alignment: that costs a table per path, too slow for FIND-3. A search returns every hit in the order of the list with the char positions of its match (chars, not bytes) and the set of directories above the hits (`/`-separated, relative).
  - **Decided** (P2): `/` in the panel starts a search (Ctrl-p's is the picker's, FIND-4); the cursor row and the open directories are remembered for Esc. While the query is typed: a char is added, Backspace takes one off (and ends the search when none is left), Enter opens the best match at once, Down and Up stop the typing and step to the next / previous match, Esc ends the search; every other key does nothing. Enter ends the search with the expansion put back to what it was before it, and then only the directories of the opened file opened (the reveal `:e` uses), so that the tree shows where the file is and nothing the cursor passed on its way (the best match of `i` or `io` is not the file `io/filter` was meant to find; Esc's restore and Enter's leave the same debris-free tree, P2); `n` and `N` are for the matches that stay lit after Down or Up (when typing they are chars of the query, and Enter opens at once, since quick open is P2's shortest way). In that state the panel's own keys work as usual, `/` starts a new query, Enter on a file opens it and ends the search the same way, Esc ends it (the expansion and the cursor are what they were) and a second Esc gives the focus back.
  - **Decided** (P2): the cursor follows the best match by revealing its directories as their listings arrive (the same step-by-step expansion `:e dir` uses), so a match in a directory not read yet is shown a moment later; Enter opens the match by its path, not the row under the cursor, so it does not wait for that. A key of the panel's own cancels a reveal still waiting. `n` and `N` walk the matches in the order the rows are on screen (directories first, then names, the order of TREE-3: `tree::cmp_paths`) and wrap round; the next match is found by a pass over the hits, so the work of one `n` is that of one key of the search.
  - **Decided** (P2): bold is the whole row of a match, and of a directory with a match somewhere below it, even when it is closed; the rest is dimmed (the core's secondary style); an empty query lights nothing, a query with no match dims everything and the status line says `/{query}  no match`. The status line is `/{query}  {n} match(es)` while a search runs, with the terminal's cursor at the end of the query while it is typed. A change to the list of files (FIND-3) while searching runs the search again.
  - **Decided** (P2): in the search line the query is cut from the left when it is longer than the screen and the count of matches (`  3 matches`, `  no match`) stays; the terminal's cursor is at the end of the query shown.
- **FIND-2** [P2][S2] Matching is fuzzy on the path relative to the root (its characters in order, smartcase), ranked by the quality of the match and then by MARK-2's likelihood — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the score uses fzf's scale, untuned: 16 a char, -3 to start a gap and -1 more a char in it, 4 for chars that follow each other (a run keeps the bonus of its first char), 8 for a char after a word edge (` _-.`), 9 after `/` or at the start, 7 for a camelCase hump; plus 12 when the match lies in the file name. The better hit has the higher score, then the higher likelihood, then the shorter path, then the smaller, so the order never depends on the order of the list. The path's length is a tiebreak after the likelihood and no part of the score (fzf's `--tiebreak=length`): as part of the score, a shorter path (`tests/review.rs`) beat a longer one (`src/core/review.rs`) by a point, the scores never tied, and the likelihood of the file you had just opened was never consulted, in the case it matters most (`mod.rs`, `index.ts`, `README.md`, `review.rs` in src and in tests). `best` finds the first: the cursor goes there.
  - **Decided** (P2): the likelihood of a path comes from MARK-2's history with the absolute path (`{root}/{path}`) and the current time, and counts only among the hits that have the top score. The history is at most 1,000 paths, so the work is theirs (each found in the sorted list by binary search), never one per hit of the top score, which can be most of 100,000 paths. Opening a file records it, so the file just opened wins ties the next time.
- **FIND-3** [P4][S2] In a git repository the files come from `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard -z` (so ignored trees such as `target/` don't flood the search); elsewhere from walking the directory. Both run in the background. One key of the search takes at most 16 ms on 100,000 paths — B
  - **Decided** (P4): measured on a release build (AMD Ryzen 9 9950X) over 100,000 synthetic sorted paths of 40 chars: the worst key of `s sr srv c cl client clientrs u ut utl q zzzz` takes 6.7 ms with ten files to a directory and 9.8 ms with a directory of its own for every file (the worst case for collecting directories), including the pass, the directories and `best` with a history of 200 files. A query that matches nothing takes 3.6 ms. `cargo test --release -p sim --test find -- --ignored bench --nocapture` prints them. ASCII paths are scanned as bytes.
  - **Decided** (P1): `io::git_files` returns git's NUL-separated bytes, relative to the directory asked; git runs with `--no-optional-locks` (a background status must not take `index.lock` from the user's own git) and `LC_ALL=C`, so that "not a git repository" is told from other failures in any language: the error is `not a git repository: {dir}`, any other `git {command}: {message}`, and a directory that cannot be entered `cannot run git in {dir}: {why}` (the directory cut from the left to 30 columns, so that the reason is not cut off the status line); a bare repository and the inside of `.git`, where git says `this operation must be run in a work tree`, are told as "not a git repository": they have no marks, and nothing is said at every save.
  - **Decided** (P4): the list is asked for at the start and whenever the panel or the picker opens (Ctrl-n, Ctrl-p, Ctrl-f), and runs in a thread of its own (`io::worker`) that never runs twice at once: requests made while it runs are one more run after it, however many there were. A walk (no repository, git cannot be run, or git is skipped for the repository's config, MARK-1) skips directories named `.git`, never follows a link (a link is a file), and stops after 200,000 files (inside a directory too: one directory of millions of files is one directory), so that a walk of `/` or of a home directory does not eat memory; git's list has no limit. The list is sorted (bytes) and has no duplicates, which the panel's search needs to look a row up by binary search. A file git lists as tracked but that is gone from the disk is in the list; opening it makes a new file, as `:e` of a name that does not exist does.
  - **Decided** (P4): measured on a release build (AMD Ryzen 9 9950X) over 100,000 files in 1,000 directories: `git ls-files` through `io::files` takes 30 ms, `git status` 154 ms (both in their threads). The worst key of a search through `Sim::update` itself, with the frame, is 6.6 ms (7.1 ms measured again on 2026-10-16 with the length no longer in the score, FIND-2, on a busy machine: load average about 10; the worst key of `cargo test --release -p sim --test find` 10.5 ms) (`cargo test --release -p sim --test find_panel -- --ignored bench --nocapture`).

- **FIND-4** [P5][S2] Ctrl-p from anywhere opens the picker (PICK-1) in its file-finding mode (Telescope's find_files key in the author's nvim): a window of its own with the fuzzy matches of FIND-2 as its list and a preview of the selected file — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P5): Ctrl-p is sim's in every mode (Insert and the command line too) and never reaches the App, like Ctrl-n and Ctrl-f. It starts an empty query, asks for the list of files again (FIND-3) and leaves the panel as it is, the tree's own `/` (FIND-1) staying the panel's key. While the picker is open Ctrl-p is the picker's "up" (PICK-2); to find files from the content search, Esc and Ctrl-p. Until the first char is typed the list is the files the history likes best (MARK-2, likeliest first), then the list in its order.
  - **Decided** (P2): the list is FIND-2's order carried through: the better score first, then the higher likelihood, then the shorter path, then the smaller, so the first row is the file `best` would have gone to. At most 1,000 rows are listed (`select_nth` finds them in a pass over the hits, so a key costs what FIND-3's bound says); the count says how many matched. Enter opens the file as the tree's Enter does (the history included) and shows it in the tree as `:e` does.
## Searching contents

- **GREP-1** [P5][S4] Ctrl-f from anywhere (Telescope's live_grep key in the author's nvim) opens the picker (PICK-1) in its content-search mode: the query is typed in the picker's prompt and the results update as you type — I
  - **Decided** (P5): Ctrl-f is sim's in every mode (Insert and the command line too) and never reaches the App, like Ctrl-n and Ctrl-p. It starts an empty query (the picker of Ctrl-p, if it is open, becomes this one) and asks for the list of files again (FIND-3), as the panel's opening does. The picker floats over the screen: the panel does not open, close or move for it, and a name search (FIND-1) that was running waits behind it. An empty query searches nothing.
  - **Decided** (P5): the picker's keys are PICK-2's: the query is always typed (there is no browsing mode). Ctrl-n and Ctrl-p are its "down" and "up", not the panel's key and FIND-4's. Tab, Paste and the mouse do nothing while it is open.
- **GREP-2** [P2][S4] The query is a literal (not a regex), smart-case as FIND-2's is: one uppercase letter makes it exact. It is searched in the text files of FIND-3's list (in a repository `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard -z`, else the walk, so `.gitignore` is respected). A file with a NUL byte in its first 8 KiB is binary and skipped — U
  - **Decided** (P2): a line is the text between two `\n`; a line matches once however many times the query occurs in it, and every occurrence, left to right and not overlapping, is marked. Bytes that are not UTF-8 are read as U+FFFD. Smart-case compares chars lowercased one by one, as FIND-2 does (rg folds more: the line it finds is shown, and a mark that cannot be placed is not drawn).
  - **Decided** (P4): the built-in reader reads a file whole and skips one over 16 MiB (rg has no such limit). A file of the list that is gone, unreadable or not a regular file (a FIFO would block the thread, a device is no text) is skipped without a word. rg decides what is binary for the files it reads, and its rule is wider than the 8 KiB: a NUL anywhere makes it print a notice (which is ignored) instead of the file's lines.
- **GREP-3** [P2,P3][S4] While the picker is in this mode its list is the results, a row for each matching line, `{path}:{line}  {text}` (PICK-3): the text trimmed and cut to the list's width, the matched chars bold, as FIND-1's matches are. Files are ordered by MARK-2's likelihood (high first), then by path; the lines of a file are in file order — U
  - **Decided** (P2): a row is the path (made to fit by the core's `shorten`, in at most two fifths of the row), `:` and the line number (from 1), two blanks and the text; the path and the number are in the secondary style, the matches are bold (and the accent's, PICK-3). The text is the line with the blanks at both ends taken off and a tab shown as a space; a line of more than 200 chars is kept from 40 chars before its first match (after a `…`) to 200 chars on. When the first match would be cut off by the list's width, the text starts at a `…` and 4 chars before the match; a match cut by the edge is bold up to it.
  - **Decided** (P2): results come in batches (GREP-5) and each file is put in its place at once, so the order never depends on the order they arrive in. The selection starts on the first matching line and stays there while more arrive; once a key has moved it, it stays on the line it was on. A new query keeps showing the old results until the first batch of the new one (or its end) arrives, so typing does not blink the list; Enter does nothing in between.
- **GREP-4** [P2][S4] The arrows and Ctrl-n / Ctrl-p move the selection over the matching lines (PICK-2). The picker's preview shows the file of the result selected with that line in the middle and its matches lit (PICK-4). That is a preview, not an open: it adds nothing to the history, leaves no buffer and starts no watcher. Enter opens the file there, as the tree's Enter does (the history included). Esc closes the picker and everything is as it was before Ctrl-f — I
  - **Decided** (P2): the arrows and Ctrl-n / Ctrl-p stop at the first and the last line; Page Up and Page Down move by the list's rows. The list scrolls to keep the selected row in view.
  - **Decided** (P1): the preview is a core App made from the bytes of a read (in a thread, at most 2 MiB; a bigger file shows empty, with its reason) and rendered, never given a key, so the text reaches the terminal as the editor's own does (SAFE-8: `^X`, `<U+XXXX>`). Its syntax colors come from the highlighter's thread (PICK-4). The frame is kept while the line, the size, the query and the spans stay; the App is kept while the cursor stays in the file; a file under the cursor that has not been read yet leaves the last frame in the pane, and a late answer for another file is dropped.
  - **Decided** (P2): Enter closes the picker, shows the file in the tree (the `:e` reveal; the panel is not opened if it was closed) and opens it as `file:line:col` does: `:{line}` and `{col}l` are typed once the file is read. A file that is open is shown instead, and its cursor goes to the line if its App is in Normal mode with nothing pending. Esc, and Backspace on an empty query, close the picker and change nothing else, because nothing else was changed: the preview is not a buffer and the picker does not touch the panel.
- **GREP-5** [P4][S4] Searching never blocks a key: it runs in a background thread. A new query cancels the old one (a generation number; an answer of an older generation is dropped), results are sent in batches, and the search stops at 1,000 matching lines (`1000+ matches`). If `rg` is on PATH it does the reading (`--fixed-strings --smart-case`, restricted to the same list of files); otherwise the built-in reader does — B
  - **Decided** (P4): the thread of a search is the shell's (`io::Greps`); `Sim` asks with `SimEffect::Grep` and gets `SimEvent::Grep(Batch)`. Each request has a thread of its own that looks at the newest generation between files and between the lines of rg's output, and stops, killing rg's process, when it is not its own; an empty query only stops. The files are taken in the order of the history's likelihood first, then the list, so that the cap keeps the likeliest files (rg reads a piece of the list on all cores, so inside a piece its files come as it finishes them). The first group is sent as soon as it is whole, then a batch every 30 ms or 200 lines; a file is never split between batches. `1000+` is said only when a 1,001st line was found. A change of the list of files while searching runs the search again.
  - **Decided** (P4): rg is run on the list in pieces of at most 128 KiB of paths (a command line has a limit), one process at a time, as `rg --null --line-number --no-heading --with-filename --fixed-strings --smart-case --no-config --no-ignore --no-messages --max-columns=65536 --max-columns-preview -e {query} -- {files}` in the root, and its output `path NUL line:text` is read as it comes (`--null` tells the path from the text whatever either holds, and one line is one matching line, which `--vimgrep` is not; `--json` would need a parser of JSON. A line of more than 64 KiB comes as its first 64 KiB.) Only files that pass GREP-7 are named: rg given none would search the working directory. If rg cannot be started, or ends with an exit code of 2 or more (or a signal) before it said anything, the built-in reader reads that piece; rg's own `no match` (exit code 1) is believed.
  - **Decided** (P4): measured on a release build (AMD Ryzen 9 9950X, a warm file cache, load average 2.6 from other work on the machine) over 100,000 files of about 4 KB (415 MB) in 1,000 directories, the size of FIND-3's bench (`cargo test --release -p sim --test grep -- --ignored bench --nocapture --test-threads=1`; the best of three runs of each of five queries: `quasar1` and `quasar` match in some of the files, `let` in all of them, `Quasar` and `zzqxzz` in none): a query that matches early sends its first batch after 1.6–2.9 ms with the built-in reader and 4.8–5.7 ms with rg, and has its 1,000 lines in 4–57 ms and 7–36 ms; a query that matches nothing reads the whole tree in 310–322 ms with the built-in reader and 200–202 ms with rg, the checks of GREP-7 (a `lstat` for every file) included. The main thread's part is small: a batch of 1,000 files with 1,000 lines through `Sim::update` takes 0.19 ms, and the worst of 3,000 keys over those results 0.17 ms. SPEED-1 and SPEED-2 were measured again with the search built in and did not change (the first frame 6.8 ms under a pty, the panel's keys at p99 45 µs).
- **GREP-6** [P2][S4] The picker says what the search has found at the bottom of its border (PICK-5): `{i} / {n}`, the selected line over the matching lines found, `no matches` where the list would be, `1,000+` at the cap — I
  - **Decided** (P2): while the search is not over a spinner follows the count, and while the old query's results are still shown the count is theirs. A query too long for the prompt is cut from the left (`…` and its last chars) and the terminal's cursor is at its end. The status line below is the file's, as it was before the picker.
- **GREP-7** [P1][S4] A search never writes anything and never follows a symlink out of the root. File names and contents reach the terminal only through the safe painting of SAFE-8 — I
  - **Decided** (P1): a file is searched, and previewed, only if it is a regular file (a link is not) and the real path of its directory lies below the real root, so neither a link in the list nor a directory that was replaced by a link leads out of the tree. A link to a file is skipped even inside the root: the file it points to is in the list on its own. The check is made before the file is opened, so a file swapped for a link in between is a race the check does not close. The panel's text goes through `layout::fit` (U+FFFD for control and bidirectional chars), the status line through `fit`, `tail` and `short`, and the preview through the App's frame; the bytes written are checked for names and contents full of escape sequences.
- **GREP-8** [P1][S4] Running `rg` in a repository never runs code that the repository controls — I
  - **Decided** (P1): `rg` is found by sim itself, in the directories of `PATH` that are absolute (an empty or relative entry would find `./rg` in the repository), and run by its full path with an empty environment (`RIPGREP_CONFIG_PATH` is gone) and `--no-config`. `--pre`, `--pre-glob`, `--search-zip` and `--hostname-bin` are never given. The files are named on the command line, so `.rgignore`, `.ignore` and `.gitignore` are not read as rules for what to search (`--no-ignore`). The query follows `-e` and the files `--`, so that none of them is an option. Checked with a config in the environment that names a `--pre` script (plain rg runs it, the search does not), a `rg` placed in the root and relative entries of `PATH`. git is not run by a search.

## The picker

The author finds files and contents in Neovim with Telescope: a window that floats over the editor with a prompt, a list and a preview of what is selected. sim's Ctrl-p (find_files) and Ctrl-f (live_grep) were in the tree panel, with the preview in the editor's pane, and the author called that poor. This section is the presentation that replaces it. What is searched, how it is ranked and how it runs stay as FIND-2, FIND-3 and GREP-2 to GREP-8 say (and their safety: GREP-7, GREP-8).

- **PICK-1** [P5][S6] Ctrl-p and Ctrl-f open a picker: a window centered over the screen, 80% of its width and of its rows above the status line, with a rounded border and, in the top border, its name and its scope, the root home-relative as UI-2's header writes it (`Find files · ~/.local/share/chezmoi`, `Live grep · ~/.local/share/chezmoi`); in depth with a dim shadow at its right and below. Its first row is the prompt, under it a rule and the list with the selected row marked, and at the right of the list (under it where the window is under 100 columns wide) the preview of the selected result. Calm draws it too, with no colors — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P5): the window is `w = max(min(W, 24), W * 4 / 5)` columns and `h = max(min(H - 1, 8), (H - 1) * 4 / 5)` rows, at `((W - w) / 2, (H - 1 - h) / 2)`; a screen under 20 columns or 8 rows (including the status line) draws no picker (its keys still work). Side by side, the list is `(w - 3) * 2 / 5` columns (at least 20) and the preview the rest, a divider between them and the borders around: the divider has `┬ ┴` at its ends and the rule under the prompt `├ ┤`; stacked, the list has `(h - 5) * 2 / 5` rows (at least 3), a border row titled with the preview's path follows it and the preview has the rest. The terminal's cursor is a bar at the end of the query.
  - **Decided** (P5): the title is the mode's name in bold and, after ` · `, the root in the secondary gray; where the part of the border that holds it (the list's width beside the preview, the window's when stacked, less its corners) is too short, the root is cut from the left (`…` and its last chars, `layout::tail`) and left out when two chars of it do not fit: the name of the mode always stays. The root is what the panel's header shows (`tilde` of the root, UI-2), so it is the one the search is about.
  - **Decided** (P5): in flat and depth the window is on the 6% neutral surface (UI-1's cursor line), the rule and the borders in the lines' color and the focused box's (the window is the focus), the title bold. The shadow (depth) is one column right of the window and one row below it, shifted by one cell: the cells keep their text, fade 70% toward the page and are on `Tones::shadow`. The window covers the screen's cells entirely: a wide char cut by its edge is a blank, and nothing under it shows through. Without tones (calm, no truecolor) there are no paints: the selected row is underlined and the matches bold.
  - **Decided** (P2): the picker is drawn after the rest of the screen from the same state (the panel, the tabs and the text are untouched, and so is the App's frame the mouse and the highlighter read), over the screen's rows: a row's text, its styles, its looks and its paints are cut at the window's edges and the picker's are put in, so the cells the window leaves are what they were.
- **PICK-2** [P2,P5][S6] While the picker is open every key is its own and none reaches a file: a char is added to the query (so typing, in Insert mode or any other, never changes a buffer), Backspace takes one off (and closes the picker when none is left), Ctrl-u clears the query, Ctrl-w takes off the last word, Ctrl-n / Down / Ctrl-j select the next result, Ctrl-p / Up / Ctrl-k the previous, Page Down and Page Up a page of the list, Enter opens the selected result, Esc closes the picker; Ctrl-f in the file finder starts the content search; the other keys, Tab, Paste and the mouse do nothing — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): closing it with Esc (or Backspace on an empty query) restores everything because nothing was changed: no buffer, mode, cursor, history, file, panel (open or focus), tree expansion or search, and the preview adds none (GREP-4). The query is not remembered: the next Ctrl-p or Ctrl-f starts empty. A query of a content search that was typing when Esc came stops the thread (GREP-5).
  - **Decided** (P5): a query is added to as it is typed, at its end (as FIND-1's and the status line's are), so nothing of it is edited in the middle; Ctrl-u and Ctrl-w are the editing Telescope's prompt gives that matter most. The keys of the panel (`j`, `k`, `n`, `N`, `g`, `/` and the rest) are chars of the query here.
- **PICK-3** [P5][S6] The list has a row for each result: for a content search `{path}:{line}  {text}` (GREP-3), for the file finder the path with its directories in the secondary gray, and the matched chars bold and in the accent. The selected row is marked by a `▎` and the accent's block across the row, and kept in view; a row too long is cut at the list's edge — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the row of a file is the path made to fit by `layout::short` (the file name at least), the chars FIND-2 matched bold, found again in what is left of the path when it was shortened (the common end of the two). The list of a content search is the groups of GREP-3 one row for each matching line, the first row on the first line. An empty list shows `no matches` in the secondary gray where its first row would be, but not while the query is empty.
  - **Decided** (P2): the list scrolls so that the selected row is in view and the rows above it are not shown again after it moves up past the first row on screen; it begins at row 0 and goes no further than the last row at the bottom of the window.
- **PICK-4** [P5][S6] The preview shows the selected result's file in the pane: its first lines for a file, and the matching line in the middle of the pane for a line of a content search, with line numbers and the syntax colors, the matching line on the accent's block and its matched chars on the accent's solid color. The title of the pane is the path (and the line). The file is not opened, only read (GREP-4) — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the preview is the App of GREP-4 rendered at the pane's size, centered (`center`) on the line; its colors are those of the highlighter's thread, asked for with a request of its own (`SimEffect::Highlight` with an id that no open file has: ids are never reused, and the thread forgets it with `SimEffect::Close` when the preview moves to another file). The colors arrive late, so a preview is plain for a moment and then colored; spans that do not cover the lines on screen are asked for again. A late answer for a file that is no longer selected is dropped, and a file not read yet leaves the last preview in the pane.
  - **Decided** (P2): a file bigger than 2 MiB (GREP-4) shows its reason where the text would be. The picker's text goes through `layout::fit` (a name can hold anything, GREP-7) and the preview through the App's frame (SAFE-8: `^X`, `<U+XXXX>`).
- **PICK-5** [P2][S6] The bottom border of the picker shows the position and the progress: `{i} / {n}` (the selected result over all the results, with thousands separators; `1,000+` at the cap of a content search) and a spinner while the search runs; at its right, the keys, `↵ open  ^n/^p move  esc close` where there is room — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the spinner is one of `⠋⠙⠹⠸⠼⠴⠦⠧⠇⠏`, advanced every 80 ms by `SimEffect::Spin` and `SimEvent::Spin(n)` of the search's generation while the search is not done; a newer search or a closed picker ends the chain, and a finished search shows no spinner. The keys are in that order and each is two blanks from the next; where there is room for part of them `^n/^p move` goes first, then `esc close`, and `↵ open` last (each set needs its width and six columns of the border, the count's included in a stacked window). The file finder runs in the key itself (FIND-3) and has none. While the old query's results are shown for a newer one the count is theirs.

## Marks

- **MARK-1** [P2,P3][S2] Files that git reports as modified, added or untracked, and files that changed on disk while sim runs, get the change color on their name, and every directory above them gets `~`, so a change can be found from the root. The status comes from `git status --porcelain=v1 -z` in the background, run again when files change — U
  - **Decided** (P2): status runs with `--untracked-files=all`, so that every untracked file has a name to color and not only its directory. A renamed file marks the new name, and the old name's directories (something left them); a copy marks only the new name. A deleted file marks the directories it was in. All of them are paths relative to the top of the repository, so `Marks::new` takes the root's `prefix` below the top (`git rev-parse --show-prefix`, which `io::git_prefix` runs) and leaves out what is outside the root. Entries that do not parse are skipped.
  - **Decided** (P1): every git of sim's is built in one place (`io::command`) with, besides the flags of GIT-1, `-c diff.submodule=short` and `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1` in its environment (UI-5 has the reason: a file's name is a path, and a repository's own `diff.submodule = diff` starts a child git in a submodule, under the submodule's own config). Of sim's commands (`config --list`, `status`, `ls-files`, `rev-parse`, `symbolic-ref` and `diff`) only the diff of one file could be made to start a child git or read a submodule's config. Measured with a probe: a superproject whose `diff.submodule` is `diff` and then `log`, `status.submoduleSummary = true`, `submodule.recurse = true`, `diff.ignoreSubmodules = none` and `submodule.<name>.ignore = none` in turn, a submodule whose commit moved and whose own config names a monitor, an external diff and a filter (and a dirty work tree), and each of sim's commands as it ran before: only the diff, with a glob or a magic name and `diff.submodule = diff`, made a witness. `status` runs with `--ignore-submodules=all`, `ls-files` does not recurse (`submodule.recurse` did not change that), and `rev-parse`, `symbolic-ref` and `config` run nothing of a submodule's.
  - **Decided** (P1): sim never runs git where the repository's own config could make it run a command that the command line does not stop (GIT-1 to GIT-3). Before every git command (`io::git_command`) it asks git for the config of the repository around the directory (`io::git`, next item), and git is not run (`io::git` answers `git skipped: ` and the reason, which names the key: see the item on the refusal) if the repository defines a key that can hold a command and that GIT-2 does not neutralize (the class of the item on how the config is read, which still takes in `core.alternateRefsCommand`, `merge.<name>.driver`, `core.sshCommand`, and an `include` or `includeIf` that can bring any of them in), or if git cannot read the config. The user's own configuration (system, global, the command line) is trusted, and git's `safe.directory` check still limits repositories owned by someone else. A repository without a config file is run. The status line says it once per session, on the first answer, and the panel shows no marks (files that change while sim runs still get theirs); the files come from the directory walk of FIND-3 (so ignored trees are in the search). Checked with temporary repositories (no global config, as check.sh runs them) for every kind of definition that is still refused, in the forms that git reads and a parser of sim's own may not (dotted sections, `config.worktree`, continued values, includes, case, quoting), a worktree, a separate git directory, a fake `.git` over a real repository, an unreadable config and one that does not parse. A filter, a monitor, an external diff and a text conversion are neutralized and run (GIT-2).
  - **Decided** (P1): git never fetches for sim, whatever the config says: it runs with `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=sim-none` (no transport at all, which also stops a url that is a command, `ext::`, whatever `protocol.ext.allow` says) and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`. The guard above is about keys that hold a command; this is about what git does with keys that look ordinary. A clone whose `.git` came as an archive can say `extensions.partialClone = origin`, `remote.origin.promisor = true`, leave its objects out, and set `remote.origin.uploadpack` (or an `ext::` url with `protocol.ext.allow`): `git status` then fetches the missing objects from the promisor remote and runs that command (measured with git 2.56: the script left its witness, and the objects came in), which the guard did not see because none of those keys held what it looked for. `uploadpack`, `receivepack`, `vcs` and the `protocol` section are in its class now too. Checked with a partial clone with its objects taken away: no object is fetched, and neither the script nor the url's command runs; plain git does both.
  - **Decided** (P1): the config is read by git itself, not by a parser of sim's (a second parser can disagree with git: continued values, dotted `[filter.x]` sections, `config.worktree`, includes). sim runs `git config --list --show-scope --show-origin -z` (with the flags of GIT-1) in the directory (reading config never runs a command: `builtin/config.c` only parses and prints, and starts an editor for `--edit` alone) and skips git when any entry of the repository's own scopes (local, worktree, and the files they include, which have the scope of the file that includes them) has a key that can hold a command and that GIT-2 does not neutralize, as a class and not a list of today's uses (so a setting git adds later is in it): the section is `filter` (but `clean`, `smudge`, `process` and `required` of a named filter are neutralized), `include`, `includeif` or `protocol` (`protocol.ext.allow = always` lets a remote's url be a command), or the variable (the last dotted component) is `driver`, `textconv` (but not `diff.<name>.textconv`, which GIT-1 turns off), `helper`, `process`, `clean`, `smudge`, `askpass`, `editor`, `pager`, `gitproxy`, `fsmonitor`, `uploadpack`, `receivepack` or `vcs`, or ends with `command` (but not `diff.<name>.command`), `cmd`, `program` or `hook`; `core.fsmonitor` and `core.hookspath` are not refused, because the command line overrides them; the ordinary keys of a repository (`core.repositoryformatversion`, `remote.*.url`, `branch.*.remote`, `user.*`, `submodule.*.url`, `lfs.url`) are not. Git lists section and variable names in lower case and keeps the case of a subsection, so the keys are compared as they come. The user's system and global scopes, and the command line's, are trusted; any other scope and a listing that does not parse are not. An error from `git config` (an unreadable file, one that does not parse) is a skip that says `git skipped: this repository's config cannot be read`; "not a git repository" is told as for any other git command, and the directory is walked without a message. Outside a repository, and where git refuses the repository for its owner, `git config` lists the user's own config and succeeds; the command that follows then fails by itself before it runs anything.
  - **Decided** (P1): hooks do not make sim skip git, and neither does `core.hooksPath`: GIT-1 gives every command `-c core.hooksPath=` an empty private directory, which wins over the repository's config (husky, prek and pre-commit set it in most repositories; it is no key to refuse), over a hooks path of the user's own, and over `.git/hooks`. Hooks run only in commands that write (`commit`, `checkout`, `merge`, `push`, `rebase`, `am`, ...), and the commands sim runs are not among them, but `post-index-change` runs when git writes the index, which a plain `git status` does to keep what it learned about a file whose time moved; sim's git also runs with `--no-optional-locks`, which stops that write. The override makes it a rule of the command line and not of a list of today's commands (GIT-2 has the witness).
  - **Decided** (P1): of the class above these keys stay refused, each for a command that sim, or the git it starts, can reach: `include`, `includeIf`: bring any other key in, and what a tracked file brings in can change between the listing and the command (no listing can show the expansion is complete when the command runs, and a refusal can); `protocol`, `uploadpack`, `receivepack`, `vcs`, `gitproxy`, `askpass`, `helper` and the `command` endings (`sshCommand`, `alternateRefsCommand`; and a `hook.<name>.command`, git's hooks in config): run to reach a remote, which a status in a partial clone with objects missing does, and the environment that stops every fetch (`GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH`, `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL`, above) is the line behind this one; `driver`, `editor`, `pager`, `cmd`, `program`: a merge, an editor, a pager, a difftool or a signing program, started by commands that sim does not run. These are not proved needed by any of sim's commands, and not proved harmless by a test either, so they stay: only what a test shows harmless or what the command line stops is taken out (GIT-2). `filter` (`clean`, `smudge`, `process`), `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath` and `diff.*.textconv`, `diff.*.command`, `diff.external` were here once: they are neutralized now.
  - **Decided** (P1): a refusal says which key caused it and where it is, so that the user knows what to change: `git skipped: core.alternaterefscommand in .git/config could run a command`. The key is as git lists it (section and variable in lower case) and the file is the origin that git gives with `--show-origin`: `.git/config` for the repository's own, `.git/config.worktree` for the worktree's, an absolute path for a linked worktree's or a separate git directory's. The first key of the repository's own scopes that can hold a command is the one named: for an `include` that is the `include.path` (it comes before the keys it brings in, and is in the class itself), so the user sees the line to take out. The words after the file are the first thing a narrow status line cuts. The file is cut with the core's `shorten` rule (`…/` and the last components that fit) to 30 columns, and a key longer than 30 columns from the left (its end is the variable), so that a long subsection never pushes the file off the line; chars that the terminal must not get (SAFE-8) are replaced, as in every message of sim's. Checked for a key in the repository's config, from a directory below the top, an include, `config.worktree`, in a linked worktree (a path that is cut), a key of 100 chars, a subsection with an escape char, and a config that cannot be read.
  - **Decided** (P2): git is asked at the start, whenever the panel opens, after a file is saved and when a file changes on disk, in a thread that never runs twice at once (`io::worker`, as for FIND-3). The paths of files that changed on disk while sim runs are the ones sim saw itself: a change reported by the watcher of an open file, and a save of one (a save is a change too, and without git it is the only news). They are relative to the root, so a file outside the tree is not marked. In a directory that is not a repository the status is not asked again until the panel opens (a repository may have been made); another failure of git is told on the status line.
  - **Decided** (P3): in the panel the change color (the core's Change style) is on the name of a changed file only, `~` follows the name of a directory with a change below it (` ~`, after the `/`), and a closed directory shows it too. The change style wins over everything else on a name: over the dimming of a search, over a syntax color.
- **MARK-2** [P2][S2] sim remembers which files you open (in its state directory) and ranks them by recency and frequency together, in the spirit of AccessRank. The five likeliest files are marked with `•` where they are in the tree — U
  - **Decided** (P2): likelihood(path) is the sum, over the times it was opened, of (age in seconds, at least 60)^-1/2. That is ACT-R's base-level activation (Anderson & Schooler 1991), a power law that fits real access streams: one open a minute ago beats many from last week, yet opens add up, so recency and frequency count together as in AccessRank. It is not AccessRank itself (which also learns from the sequence of accesses). The history is bounded at 16 opens a path and 1,000 paths (the least likely go); a file opened daily for years must not outweigh today's work, and 1,000 files is far beyond one person's working set. Keys are absolute paths; `top(root, n, now)` returns the paths below `root`, relative to it.
  - **Decided** (P1): the history file is `$XDG_STATE_HOME/sim/history`, else `$HOME/.local/state/sim/history` (a relative `XDG_STATE_HOME` is ignored, as the XDG spec says); with neither there is no history, never a fallback to `/tmp`. The directory is 0700 and a real directory (a link is refused: `history: cannot use {dir}: it is a link`, where the `chmod` and the file would otherwise go through it), the file 0600, and a save is a temporary file in the same directory, synced, then renamed over the old file, then the directory is synced. One line a path: the times in hex, a tab, the path; a path with a line break is not remembered. Reading skips what does not parse and applies the bounds, so a damaged or edited file cannot do harm; an unreadable file is an empty history. Two sessions saving at once: the last save wins.
  - **Decided** (P2): an open is recorded when the user chooses a file: Enter, `l` or Right on a file in the panel, a match opened by Enter, `:e name`, and the file named on the command line; showing a file that is open again counts, Tab does not. The time is the shell's: seconds since the epoch at the start, and the ticks (one a second) after it. Each record asks the shell to save the history; the saves are jobs of the journal thread, in order, so sim waits for the last one on exit as it does for snapshots, and the first failure is told once on the status line.
  - **Decided** (P2): a directory is not a file that was opened. `:e dir` (and Enter on a link that leads to a directory, which the panel lists as a file) is noted when it is asked for, before the shell can tell, so the shell's answer that it is a directory (`SimEvent::Dir`) takes it out of the history again: left in, it filled one of the five `•` and, in the narrowed tree (MARK-3), stood in the place of the directory, so that the recent files below it vanished. A path is noted in its lexical form (`./a` and `a` are one key, as the same file is for BUF-1), so that the opens of one file add up.
  - **Decided** (P2): `•` is where a file's marker goes (the two columns left of its name that a directory spends on `▸`), so no name moves; it is shown for the five likeliest files whose directory is open, and not on a directory.
- **MARK-3** [P2][S2] `.` in the panel narrows the tree to the files that changed, the open files and the recently opened ones, with their directories (as Mylyn's task context); `.` again shows everything — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the files kept are those git (or the disk) says changed, the open files and the recent ones: a file opened as often as one opened once a day ago counts (likelihood 1/sqrt(86,400) = 0.0034; the history is bounded at 1,000 files), so that "today's work" is the horizon, not an arbitrary count. The narrowed tree is a `Tree` like the other, made from listings that hold only these files and the directories above them, all open; so its keys are the panel's (`h` closes a directory, `-` closes all). It is made again when the marks, the open files or the history change. `.` pressed again shows the whole tree with the entry under the cursor revealed (its directories are opened, reading what they need), `/` and Ctrl-p leave the narrowing, and a deleted file that git reports is in it (opening it makes a new file).

## Git and the changes tree

Every git command of sim's goes through one guarded function (GIT-1 to GIT-4), and the panel has a view of what differs from HEAD (DIFF-1 to DIFF-6): the author opens Neo-tree's `git_status` in the author's Neovim with `<leader>ge`. Deleted and renamed files never show in a normal tree: they are hidden changes.

- **GIT-1** [P1][S8] Every git command of sim's is built in one function, `io::git_command`, and nowhere else: no shell; `-c core.fsmonitor=false`; `-c diff.submodule=short`; `-c core.hooksPath=` an empty private directory; `--no-optional-locks`; `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1`, so that a name is a path and never a pattern or magic (UI-5); `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv --ignore-submodules=all` on `diff`, `show` and `log`; `--ignore-submodules=dirty` on `status` (GIT-4); and for every filter that the repository's own config names an empty `clean`, `smudge` and `process` and `required=false` (GIT-2). The environment is what MARK-1 gave it: `LC_ALL=C`, `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=sim-none`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`. In plain mode none is run (PLAIN-1) — U, I
  - **Decided** (P1): a test finds no other `Command::new("git")` in sim/src, so that a new caller cannot leave a flag out; the flags that depend on the subcommand are added by the function, not by its callers. The config is listed with the same flags (listing it runs nothing; the flags cost nothing there). A caller may still give a flag after them (a later `--ignore-submodules` wins: the counts of DIFF-2 use `=all`).
  - **Decided** (P1): the directory of `core.hooksPath` is `no-hooks` in the state directory (STATE-1: private, made by the shell at the start, unless in plain mode, which runs no git, and always empty; sim writes nothing into it); where there is none (and in the library's own tests, which start none) it is `/dev/null`, which can hold no hook either. A repository's `core.hooksPath` is not a key to refuse (the item on hooks of MARK-1), and the `.git/hooks` of the repository itself is stopped by the same override.
  - **Decided** (P1): the environment stays as it was: the PATH is the user's own, and git, and git-lfs under GIT-2, are found along it (an empty or relative entry of it would find them in the repository, as it would find `git` itself: that is the user's choice of PATH, which every program they run shares).
- **GIT-2** [P1][S8] What the command line stops is neutralized and the repository's git runs; it is no reason to refuse: `core.fsmonitor`, hooks, an external diff (`diff.external`, `diff.<name>.command`), a text conversion (`diff.<name>.textconv`) and a filter (`filter.<name>.clean`, `smudge`, `process`, `required`) — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P1): each has a witness test: a repository whose config names a script that leaves a witness file, run by plain git as the control and not by sim's call, which still answers. A monitor: `status`; a hook: a `git commit` built by `io::git_command` (which takes any command), with a `pre-commit` hook in `.git/hooks` and one in a hooks path of the repository's config; an external diff and a text conversion: sim's `git diff HEAD` with `diff.external`, and a `diff.cat.textconv` that an attribute names; a filter: its `clean`, `smudge` and `process`, run on a file whose time moved (same size, newer time: git reads it again to compare) by `status` and by `diff HEAD`.
  - **Decided** (P1): a filter is neutralized by its name: for every `filter.<name>.*` entry of the repository's own scopes the command gets `-c filter.<name>.clean= -c filter.<name>.smudge= -c filter.<name>.process= -c filter.<name>.required=false`, all four whichever of them the config defines (a `process` left in place still runs, and a `required=true` with no command makes `status` fail). The name is git's own spelling, passed as one argument (quotes, spaces, `;` and `#` in it do no harm: there is no shell). Refused instead (GIT-3): a name with an `=` (git cuts the key of `-c` at the first one, so it cannot be given), a name that is not UTF-8, and a `filter` key that is not one of the four.
  - **Decided** (P1): what neutralizing costs: a file that a filter converts looks changed, because git compares the work tree's content with the blob, which holds what the filter made of it. The exception is git-lfs's own filter: if every `filter.lfs.*` entry of the repository's own scopes is exactly what `git lfs install` writes (`clean = git-lfs clean -- %f`, `smudge = git-lfs smudge -- %f`, `process = git-lfs filter-process`, `required = true`), it is left alone and git runs `git-lfs`, the user's program. Any other value (`git lfs install --skip-smudge` writes `--skip`) is neutralized like any other, and its files show as changed. The exception also needs every `lfs.*` key of the repository's own scopes to be harmless, because git-lfs reads its settings from the repository's config and a setting can make it write or run outside the repository: with `lfs.storage` pointing to a directory and a tracked LFS file whose content changed, `git status` made git-lfs write `<dir>/objects/…` and `<dir>/tmp` (measured with git-lfs 3.8.0; git-lfs itself refuses that key from a `.lfsconfig`: "These unsafe '.lfsconfig' keys were ignored"). The harmless keys are git-lfs's own list of what a `.lfsconfig` may set (`git-lfs-config(5)`: `lfs.allowincompletepush`, `lfs.fetchexclude`, `lfs.fetchinclude`, `lfs.gitprotocol`, `lfs.locksverify`, `lfs.pushurl`, `lfs.skipdownloaderrors`, `lfs.url`, and `lfs.<url>.access`) and what `git lfs` writes into `.git/config` itself (`lfs.repositoryformatversion`, and for a url `access` and `locksverify`; checked against git-lfs's warning, which names `lfs.storage`, `lfs.concurrenttransfers`, `lfs.standalonetransferagent`, `lfs.cachecredentials`, `lfs.pathfiltercachesize`, `lfs.tustransfers`, `lfs.customtransfer.<name>.path`, `lfs.extension.<name>.clean`, a url's `locksverify` in a `.lfsconfig` and `remote.<name>.lfspushurl` as unsafe there). Any other `lfs.*` key (the next one git-lfs adds too) takes the exception away and the filter is neutralized like any other, and where only the user's own config defines the `lfs` filter (`git lfs install` writes it there) it is turned off all the same. The choice is a pure function with tests of its own; the fixture of real LFS files runs where `git-lfs` is installed.
- **GIT-3** [P1][S8] Where the config can still make a command of sim's run something that the command line does not stop, git is not run, and the reason names the key and the file, as MARK-1 has it: `git skipped: core.alternaterefscommand in .git/config could run a command` — U, I
  - **Decided** (P1): "still" is the class of MARK-1 minus GIT-2's: the items of MARK-1 on the config's reading, on the keys that stay and on the form of the refusal are this ID's. A refusal of a filter says what cannot be given: a name with an `=` is cut to 30 columns like any key.
- **GIT-4** [P1][S8] sim does not recurse into submodules: `status` runs with `--ignore-submodules=dirty` and every `diff`, `show` and `log` with `--ignore-submodules=all`, and every command with `-c diff.submodule=short`, so git runs no child git in a submodule (under the submodule's own config, which the guard did not read: the patch of a diff would start one for a repository's own `diff.submodule = diff`, UI-5), and a submodule is one entry with its status, as a file is (`M` when its commit is not the one the repository records) — U, I
  - **Decided** (P1): `--ignore-submodules=all`, which MARK-1 used, never showed a submodule; `dirty` shows the commit's change and leaves out the submodule's own work tree, so a dirty submodule with its commit unmoved shows nothing (NOTES). Checked with a submodule whose own config has a monitor and a filter: plain `git status` runs the monitor, sim's does not and says ` M sub` once the submodule's commit moved. The numstat of DIFF-2 runs with `=all`: a submodule has a letter and no counts. `git ls-files` does not recurse.
- **GIT-5** [P1][S10] The frame says what the guard did to the repository, so that it cannot be missed. When git is not run for the repository's config (GIT-3), the top border of the tree's box says `git not run` and the lines of both boxes are the alert's; when git runs with settings of the repository's own turned off (GIT-2: the monitor, the hooks path, an external diff, a text conversion, a filter), the same place says `n git settings off`. In flat, where there are no boxes, the words are at the right end of the tree's header. The words are the alert's block (a surface in the alert's ink; inverse and bold where there is no color, COLOR-2), since what the guard turned away is what the alert is for (UI-1). The status line says it in words once, as before, and for GIT-2 now says which setting (`git: turned off the repository's core.fsmonitor and 2 others`, the first of them by name), and `<Space>gi` says it again whenever it is asked.
