Calm has no frame to draw it on and keeps the status line's message — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P1): the frame, because the status line's message is told once and the next message replaces it, while the frame says it for as long as it is true: someone who opens a repository they did not write and looks away for a second still sees what sim did about it.
  - **Decided** (P2): salience follows what was lost. A refusal takes the marks, the signs, the diff and the changes away, so it is the badge and the lines of both boxes in the alert's ink (a color, so the badge carries it where there is none); a neutralized setting takes nothing away, git works, so it is the badge alone.
  - **Decided** (P1): the words on the frame are fixed words and a number, none from a file. The name of a setting that the status line's message gives comes from the repository's config and passes `layout::fit` (SAFE-8) like every string of the interface that a file can write. A panel too narrow for the words gets a shorter form (`no git`; `git: n off`, then `n off`), and where there is no panel at all, depth puts them in the editor's top border and flat leaves them to the status line. Depth has them in the top border only and flat in the header only, never both. The window draws them as the alert's pill (GUI-9); the scene golden `guard_dark` pins it.
  - **Decided** (P2): what the guard turned off is what `io::turned_off` finds in the config that git lists (MARK-1): an entry of the repository's own scopes (any but system, global and the command line) whose key GIT-1 and GIT-2 turn off: `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, `diff.external`, `diff.<name>.textconv` and `.command`, and the four keys of a filter (counted once for the filter). The hooks of a default `.git/hooks` are off always (GIT-1) and are not a setting of the repository's, and git-lfs's own filter, which GIT-2 lets run, is not counted because nothing is turned off. The number is of settings, a filter being one.
  - **Decided** (P2): `<Space>gi` says it again: the status line's message is told once and a key ends it, and the frame says that something was done and not what; with `git not run` on the frame there was no way left to learn which setting caused it. Vim's `Ctrl-g` (the file's info) has no key in sim, and the leader's `g` is git's (`<Space>ge`), so the smallest way that fits sim's keys is `<Space>gi`. It puts on the status line the message of the first refusal (the key and the file), or the repository's own settings that were turned off, by name and in git's order (`git: turned off the repository's core.fsmonitor, filter.wit, diff.external`), or that none is, or that there is no repository, or in plain mode that no git is run. The names come from the repository's config: each is cut to 30 columns (`layout::tail`) and the line passes `fit` where it is drawn (SAFE-8). The key is in the key table (KEY-1: which-key and `:help` list it) and in the README.
- **DIFF-1** [P2,P3][S8] `<Space>ge` opens the panel in the changes view: only the files that differ from HEAD (staged and unstaged together, untracked ones too), in their folders, all open. `<Space>ge` again, or `q`, returns to the normal tree. The keys are the tree's own (TREE-4); `.` (MARK-3) stays as it is, and in this view it also leaves it — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the view is a narrowed tree (MARK-3's own machinery: a tree of listings that hold only these files and the directories above them), made from the marks of MARK-1 (modified, added, deleted, renamed, untracked and conflicted files) and made again with every answer of git's status while it is open (a save, a change on disk). A renamed file's old name is no row of its own. `Esc` gives the text the focus and the view stays; Ctrl-n closes the panel and it stays for the next opening. Outside a repository, or where git is not run (GIT-3), the key says so on the status line and the panel keeps the tree. Space is a leader where the panel has the focus or a file is open (KEY-2), so with the panel closed and no file open `<Space>ge` is for Ctrl-n first.
  - **Decided** (P2): the counts are asked of git when the view opens and with every answer of the status while it is open (`SimEffect::Changes`, answered by `SimEvent::Changes`, in a thread: the main thread asks git for nothing); until they come the rows have their letter only. A view that is empty says so (`no changes from HEAD`).
- **DIFF-2** [P2][S8] A file's row shows its status letter (`M`, `A`, `D`, `R`, `U`) and its `+n −n` from `git diff --numstat HEAD`; an untracked file shows `U` and its line count; a folder shows the sums of what is below it — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the numbers come from `git diff --numstat -z --ignore-submodules=all HEAD` (the empty tree before the first commit), which sees the index and the work tree against HEAD together; an untracked file's lines are counted by reading it (at most 1,000 files, none over 1 MiB, none binary: a count git does not give); a zero part is left out (`+5`, `−2`, nothing for a pure rename), a binary file shows `bin`. The counts are written for people: `+12 −3` with the real minus sign, in green and red where there are colors, at the right edge before the letter (and in calm, after the name).
- **DIFF-3** [P2][S8] A deleted file is listed with `D`; Enter on it opens HEAD's version, `git show HEAD:path` through the guard, read-only, and the status line says `HEAD:path (read-only)`; its tab says `HEAD:name`, so does its letter of `<Space>bp`, and nothing that shows its name drops the `HEAD:` — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the buffer is an App named `HEAD:path` (so the syntax is by its extension) whose text is read-only: no watcher, no snapshot, no history, and a write is refused with the read-only message, as for a binary file. The message line says `HEAD:path (read-only)` when it opens and the status line keeps `read-only`. A blob over 20 MiB is not shown (a message instead). Only a deleted file opens this way: any other file of the view opens as in the tree.
- **DIFF-4** [P2][S8] A rename is one row, `R old → new`, under the new name's folder (the old name as a path from the new name's folder: `../top.md → top.md`, `y.rs → y2.rs`, `a/b/c.rs → z.rs`), with the counts of what changed in it; in a panel too narrow for the row the new name stays whole (it is where the file is now) and the old one is cut from the left with a `…`, and where even `… → new` does not fit the new name is the row, with its `R`; no name of the panel is cut without a `…` — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): git says `R` for a rename it has in the index (`git mv`, `git add -A`); a file moved without `git add` is a deleted and an untracked file, two rows. The row's path is the new name's: Enter opens it.
- **DIFF-5** [P1][S8] In `--plain`, `<Space>ge` is refused with a message that names plain mode, since it runs no program — U
  - **Decided** (P1): the message is `plain mode runs no program: no changes view`; the panel does not open and no git is asked for.
- **DIFF-6** [P5][S8] `<Space>g` and `<Space>ge` are entries of the key table (KEY-1), so which-key and `:help` show them, and `q` says what it does in the changes view — U, I
  - **Decided** (P5): `<Space>g` is `git` and `<Space>ge` is `changes from HEAD`, in the tree's and the text's modes; the coverage test of KEY-1 holds.

## Highlighting

- **SYN-1** [P3][S3] Syntax highlighting colors the text with the terminal's own palette, read with OSC 4 the way the core reads the background with OSC 11 (VIS-4): keywords red (and bold), strings, numbers and constants green (both are literal values), types yellow, functions cyan; comments in the secondary gray and italic; everything else primary. Blue is never used, being near the change hue (250). Each color keeps its hue but is moved in OKLCH lightness until |Lc| ≥ 75 against the background, so the theme stays and the text stays readable. Without an OSC 4 reply the color is derived from its hue when the background is known (see the last Decided of SYN-1), and SGR 31–36 stand in where it is not — U (golden)
  - Changed from "lightness and type only" at the author's request (sim is for convenience); P3 still holds, because the change is a background and its hue is kept free.
  - **Decided** (P3): scopes map to looks by the longest matching prefix in a table (`RULES` in `syn.rs`); where the scopes of a char disagree, the order of `Look` decides: comment, string, keyword, number, type, function, so a keyword inside a comment or a string stays a comment or a string. Comment: `comment`. String: `string` (but not `string.unquoted`: a bare word, YAML's plain value, bash's `$(...)`) and `constant.character`. Keyword: `keyword` (but not `keyword.operator`, except `CMD` of a Dockerfile), `storage` (not `storage.type.string`, the `f` of an f-string, and not lifetimes) and `variable.language` (this, self, super). Number and constant: `constant`. Type: `support.type`, `entity.name.type`, `.class`, `.struct`, `.enum`, `.trait` and `entity.other.inherited-class`. Function: `entity.name.function`, `support.function`, `support.macro` and `variable.function`. Markdown headings and strong are bold with no color. Operators, names and punctuation are plain. Known quirk of bat's Rust syntax: `let` and the primitive types (`u8`, `str`) share the scope `storage.type`, so both are keywords.
  - **Decided** (P3): keywords are SGR 1;31 (bold red), strings, numbers and constants 32, types 33, functions 36, comments italic (3) in the core's secondary gray, or 2;3 (faint and italic) while there is no palette of the core's (VIS-4). The change style wins: on a char that has it a look can be bold or italic but never a color, because reverse video would turn a text color into a background (P3: the change is the only chromatic background).
  - **Decided** (P3): the background is the core's: `term::enter` asks for it (OSC 11) right after the screen is switched, the core's `Reply` puts the answer together out of the keys and makes `Event::Bg`, the first frame waits for it at most 3 ms, and a late answer redraws everything, all as in the core's main. sim gives `Event::Bg` to every App (so the core's APCA palette, VIS-4, paints Primary, Secondary, Change and Hidden in every Frame, by `term::sgr`) and to the syntax colors; a file opened later is told it as soon as it is open. sim's own OSC 11 handling is gone. When COLORTERM is `truecolor` or `24bit`, sim also asks, after the first frame (never before it), for ANSI red, green, yellow, magenta and cyan (OSC 4; blue is never asked for). Their replies come in as keys too (crossterm reads `ESC ]` as Alt+`]`, the payload as plain keys and `ESC \` or BEL as Alt+`\` or Ctrl-g); `color::Input` is in front of the core's `Reply`: while replies are still to come (five were asked for, and they are counted as they arrive, however late: over a slow link they take seconds, and a reply that is not put together here is typed into the text, as keys, `cc` and `r` among them) an Alt+`]` is held until the next key says which reply it begins (`4` is sim's, anything else goes to the core's `Reply` with the held key, in order; the core's `Reply` holds an Alt+`]` for OSC 11 as long, so a typed Esc and `]` wait no longer than before), and an OSC 4 reply is put together here and never reaches the App; a key that turns out not to belong to a reply goes on as a key. Each color is then moved in OKLCH lightness, away from the background (lighter on a dark one: the side with more contrast to give), in steps of 0.01 until |Lc| ≥ 75 (the core's `apca`; APCA-W3 0.0.98G-4g), keeping its hue and chroma (chroma shrinks only to fit sRGB, by the core's `oklch`). Without COLORTERM, or for a color without a reply, or without a background reply, SGR 31–36 stand in. A late reply redraws the screen. `color.rs` is what the core's `core::color` does not provide: the other way round of `oklch`, the contrast loop, the selection's gray and the assembling of OSC 4.
  - **Decided** (P1): no answer of the terminal may be typed into the shell (VIS-4): sim's quit does what the core's does. When the background or an ANSI color may still be on its way (`Sim::asking`), the quit wakes the input thread (SIGWINCH), which asks the terminal for its attributes (`CSI c`, answered after everything asked before) and tells the quit, which waits for that for at most 100 ms before it gives the terminal back. Checked with the core's `scripts/pty_bench.py leak` against sim's binary: a terminal that answers 30 ms late, in order, and `:q` at the first frame leaves no byte for the shell; a terminal that answers everything, or all but OSC 11, costs a quit under 80 ms, one that answers nothing about 100 ms.
  - **Decided** (P2): the core's `Row` has four styles and no color of syntax, so sim composes its own `Screen` (the core's `Frame`, which carries the core's palette, the looks of each row, the syntax colors) and draws it with `paint::Painter`, the core's `Term::draw` with looks (the four styles are the core's `term::sgr`; the looks go on top). The looks of an editor row come from the frame: the line is read from its gutter, its chars from the buffer, and the columns of each char by the rules of view.rs (a tab to the next multiple of 4, `^X`, `<U+XXXX>`, the width of the char); a wrapped line goes on in the rows below. The columns of a char are the core's own `view::width` (VIS-7's emoji sequences included) in the looks of a row, in the painter (so that a span starts where the core's `Term` starts it) and in the panel's names (`layout::fit`); what is repeated is only which chars the core writes as `^X` or `<U+XXXX>` (`layout::hostile`, with U+061C since SAFE-8's decision), and a golden with tabs, wide and control chars and a test with emoji sequences and the bidirectional controls keep the two together.
  - **Decided** (P3): the owner's call, 2026-10-16, after sim showed almost no color inside tmux 3.7c, where the replies to OSC 4 often never arrive. A color whose reply has not come is no longer left to SGR 31-36 when COLORTERM is truecolor and the background is known (OSC 11, or tmux answering for its client): it is derived from the OKLCH hue of its class, red 25 (keywords), green 145 (strings, numbers and constants), yellow 85 (types), cyan 195 (functions), never blue or magenta (magenta is only asked for the alert's sake, COLOR-1), at chroma 0.12 (shrunk by `from_lch` until it fits sRGB), starting at lightness 0.5 and moved by the same contrast loop as a real one until |Lc| ≥ 75 against the background. A reply that comes later replaces the derived color; the Painter compares the Syntax with the last screen's, so everything is drawn again. SGR 31-36 stay for a terminal without COLORTERM, or whose background is unknown too. `Syntax::pending` answers for the replies still to come, not for the colors, so the quit still waits for them (VIS-4). Checked on VIS-4's eight backgrounds: each class has a truecolor, its hue is within 6 degrees of the class's, its chroma is over 0.03 and |Lc| ≥ 75; a late reply replaces it; a keyword is painted `38;2` and not `31`.
  - **Decided** (P2): the query for the five ANSI colors is sent plain (`OSC 4;N;? ST`), also inside tmux: tmux 3.7c run with `-f /dev/null` forwards it to the outer terminal and relays the reply (measured with a pty harness, 2026-10-17), and loses it only when the query is wrapped in DCS passthrough, which sim never does (only the clipboard's OSC 52 is, as in the core). So the terminal's real palette is used under tmux when the terminal answers, and the derived colors are the fallback for one that does not. A separate cause of lost colors in tmux was found on the way and is fixed: a Painter sequence longer than tmux takes (UI-8's Decided on SGR length).
  - **Decided** (P3): since UI-1 (2026-10-17) blue is free for the interface in flat and depth, where diffs are red and green; syntax colors still never use it, and neither does calm, where blue is the change color. The query stays at five replies (a blue is derived, UI-1), so `Syntax::hue` and its tests are as they were. Markdown's looks (UI-8) have no hue of their own: a heading is bold, a bullet and a quote's bar secondary, and only a link is blue, in flat and depth, as the accent (COLOR-1).
  - **Decided** (P3): the owner's call, 2026-10-17: numbers and constants take the strings' green, and magenta leaves syntax entirely. Magenta is the alert's (COLOR-1: only what is hidden or dangerous), and a target is found slower among distractors of its own color (Duncan & Humphreys 1989), so nothing in the text may share the alert's hue. Strings and numbers are both literal values, so they share a hue as they share a meaning (a nominal channel, Mackinlay 1986). The terminal is still asked for its magenta, since the alert is derived from it (UI-1), and `Syntax::hue` has four classes now (keywords, strings and numbers, types, functions).
- **SYN-2** [P2][S3] It is on by default; `zh` turns it off and on for the session — U
  - **Decided** (P2): `zh` is taken when the `z` was typed in Normal mode with nothing pending and the next key is `h`; the App still gets both keys (its parser drops `zh`), so `zz` and a count in between are the App's. The status line says `syntax colors off` or `on` until the next key. It holds for every file until sim ends.
- **SYN-3** [P4][S3] Highlighting never delays a key: per-line parse states are cached, and a jump that needs parsing from far above is parsed in the background (those lines show plain until it arrives). Files over 10 MB are not highlighted — B
  - **Decided** (P4): `Syn` keeps the parse state at the start of every 16th line (`EVERY`); an edit at line E keeps the states at or above E, and the next call parses again from the last of them. A line takes about 40 us to parse (release, fancy-regex), so the edited line is back after at most 15 lines, 0.6 ms measured (a step of 32 measured 1.2 ms, over SPEED-1's 1 ms; a step of 8 costs five times the memory for 0.2 ms). The states of 70,000 lines held 1.1 MB resident. A cold first window of 50 Rust lines takes 17 ms (the syntax's contexts are decoded and its regexes compiled the first time it is used) and loading the syntax set 0.6 ms (bat's set, SYN-4): both are in the highlighter's thread, never before the first frame. A jump 10,000 lines below the cached states takes 0.4 s. Spans are not cached in `Syn`, only states; the spans of the window on screen are kept by `Sim` (below). `cargo test --release -p sim --test syn_bench -- --ignored bench --nocapture` prints these.
  - **Decided** (P4): all parsing is in the highlighter's thread (`io::highlight_thread`), which owns a `Syn` for each file, so no key waits for it, near or far. `Sim` asks for the lines on screen plus a screenful above and below (as a `Req` with a clone of the buffer's rope, which is O(1)) whenever the text changed or the window is not covered, once for each window and version, and shows the answer (`Hl`) when it arrives. Until then, and after an edit, the spans of the last answer are used (an edit leaves the colors of the other lines as they were for a few milliseconds); when lines were inserted or removed at the cursor, the spans of the lines below it are shifted by the difference, so Enter or `dd` does not make the screen flash plain. Of the requests waiting, the newest for each file is answered. The thread finds where the text changed by comparing the new rope with the one it parsed last (the first chunk that differs), so undo, paste, `:s` and a reload are all handled without the App saying where, and tells `Syn::edit` that line; ropes cut into chunks in another way count as different where their chunks first do, which only costs a longer parse.
  - **Decided** (P4): files over 10 MB (`sim::sim::MAX_HL`) are never asked for. Measured on a release build (AMD Ryzen 9 9950X), a window of 70 lines of a 100,000-line Rust file: the first one (loading the syntaxes) 17 ms, an edit at line 50,000 with its window 3.7 ms mean and 3.9 ms worst (4 MB of text compared included), a window where the states are cached 3.3 ms, a first window 50,000 lines down 2.6 s (plain until then). A key on the main thread, with the panel open, colored text and a history of 1,000 files, takes at p99 90 µs (80x24) and 410 µs (240x60) (SPEED-1). `cargo test --release -p sim --test syn_bench -- --ignored bench --nocapture` prints them.
  - **Decided** (P4): the main thread finds the span of a char by binary search (`layout::colors`; the spans of a line are in order and apart): scanning them made each plain char of the screen cost all the line's spans, so a long line with many (a minified file, 18,000 spans in 100,000 chars) took 7 ms a key instead of the 0.1 ms the rest of the frame takes, measured with a release build.
  - **Decided** (P4): a line of more than 10,000 bytes (`syn::MAX_LINE`) is plain and is not parsed, and the parse state stays as it was before it, so that the lines after it do not start from a guess. The time one line takes is bounded: with the pure-Rust regex backend some syntaxes take time that grows with the square of the line (measured on a release build, a shell heredoc whose word is 100,000 chars: 5.8 s; `A<` 30,000 times in TypeScript: 0.4 s; the rest of 45 languages and shapes of 100,000 chars: 0.2 s or less), and the highlighter is one thread for every file, so one such line made every other file wait and kept a core busy at every edit. Code with lines this long is minified or generated; it is the Spec's P4 (speed) that decides, and no higher principle asks for colors there.
  - **Decided** (P4): a parse that takes too long is given up. The highlighter is one thread for every file, so a request that does not end would lose it for the session and keep a core busy. Every request runs in a thread of its own that the highlighter waits for (`io::HL_BUDGET`, 5 s, and 100 us for each line down to the window: the first window 50,000 lines down takes 2.6 s); one that does not finish is given up: the file is plain from then on (its next requests are answered at once, plain), the status line says `syntax colors stopped for {name}: the highlighter did not finish`, and the highlighter goes on, with a new state, for the other files. The thread that was given up cannot be stopped (nothing in Rust stops a thread): it goes on until its parse ends, which it does (the next row), and then it is gone. Checked in the thread (`highlight_thread_within` with a short budget, on a shell script of 20 heredocs whose word is 9,000 chars, 50 ms a line in release: `slow_script`) and by E2E (24 of them in a (debug) sim, whose 5 s it exceeds).
  - **Decided** (P1, P4): no parse loops for ever (decided 2026-10-18 under the owner's delegation: syntect is vendored with a guard). Before it, syntect 5.3.0 did on some text in bat's JavaScript and the syntaxes that embed it, and the highlighter's thread kept a core busy until sim ended: `fence.md` (a fence of `js` with `return start`, a blank line and a JSDoc comment in it) and `script.html` took 398 and 399 ticks of CPU in the first 4 s of the sim installed before this change (a core is 100 ticks a second), and 8 and 3 (the start's) with the guard. **Why it looped**: not the regex engine. syntect's parser goes round in a circle on matches that consume nothing, and its guard (`would_loop`) knows only a push followed by a pop. In sublimehq's JavaScript, the one that two-face's pure-Rust set has for `source.js` (syntax 37 of the set), the contexts `expression-statement-end` and `#anon_expression-statement-end_0` `set` each other at one position: the first when the rest of the line is blanks and block comments (the last one may be unfinished), the second when an operator comes next, and its operators include `/`, which is how `/*` starts. So an expression followed by a line that is only a block comment, or by one that ends in one (`y /* c */`, a JSDoc comment after `return start`), went round a million times in 3 s and never out. Oniguruma loops in it too (the same syntect with `regex-onig`), and so does the `bat` of this machine (0.26.1) given `-l JavaScript`; bat is fine with a `.js` file and with a fence of Markdown because its set also has `JavaScript (Babel)`, which has the scope `source.js` and so takes it from the sublimehq one, and which only the onig set carries (two-face leaves it out of the pure-Rust one). Everything that embeds `source.js` had it: of the 195 syntaxes of the set 19 spun on random text (HTML and its Rails, ASP, Erlang, Tcl, EEx, Twig and Jinja2 forms, JSP, Javadoc, JavaScript, JavaScript (Rails), MultiMarkdown, PHP, CFML, QML, Ruby Slim, Svelte and Vue), and Markdown through a fence of `js`. 195 syntaxes of 1,500 documents of 2 to 9 lines of up to 6 tokens of 190 (operators, comment marks, quotes, keywords, fences, tags) found the 19, each traced to this one pair of contexts; 6,000 documents of each without the tokens `/*` and `/**` found 1 more, the same; no other circle. **The guard**: `vendor/syntect` is syntect 5.3.0 as crates.io has it (vendor/README.md: what is left out, the sha256 of the crate) and `vendor/syntect.patch`, 24 lines in `parse_next_token`: more than `MAX_EMPTY_RUN` matches that consume nothing at one position, and the parser goes on one char without making the match, as Sublime Text does for the loop it detects. It is the workspace's `[patch.crates-io]` (the root Cargo.toml), `two-face` and the editor use the one copy (`cargo tree -i syntect`), and `cargo install --locked --path sim`, which Homebrew's formula runs, and `cargo build --frozen` in the tarball, which the AUR's PKGBUILD runs, build it (both read: NOTES says what does not). `scripts/vendor-check.sh`, step 13 of check.sh, takes the patch off a copy and compares every file with the sha256 of upstream's, and fails for a byte changed in `vendor/syntect`, in the patch or in the list, and for a file added. **The number**: the longest run of matches that consume nothing at one position, with a counter in a copy of the parser that stopped a circle at 5,000: 17 in the random documents of the 195 syntaxes (3,000 each; random text makes the longest chains, and the 311 circles it met were cut at 5,000 and are not counted), 13 in the 237 files of this repository, 11 in 10.5 million lines of 48,831 files of real code under `~/ghq` (about 60 extensions, 1,500 files of each at most) and 10 in 6,000 Markdown files; none of the real files met a circle. `MAX_EMPTY_RUN` is 128: 7.5 times the longest of the random text and 10 times the longest of the real files; more would cost in the worst line (below) and less would cut a chain that a syntax made. A chain cut is a char skipped a position early in one line, never a hang. **What it costs** (release, best of 5, the same machine): the guard fires after 128 matches, 46 us for `y /* c */` where it is 168 us at 1,000; a line of 10,000 bytes made to fire it as often as it can (`y` and 2,000 comments `/**/` after it, each one a circle that the guard cuts) takes 182 ms (167 ms at 64, 426 ms at 1,000), so a window of 48 such lines is 8.7 s: over the 5 s of the highlighter's budget, which gives it up (the row above), and its thread ends by itself, in bounded time, and not for ever. Without a circle it costs a compare and an addition on each match that consumes nothing: the benches of `syn_bench.rs` (the load, the first window, a line in a long run of Rust, the jump, the edit), 9 rounds, interleaved, before (crates.io's syntect) and after, at a load average of 12: 0.046 ms a line before and after, the edit 0.73 ms before and after, the first window 4.77 and 4.71 ms, within 2 %; the release binary is 5,170,424 bytes before and 5,170,304 after (limit 5,242,880). **What is checked**: `syn_hang.rs`: the fast test gives the 19 syntaxes, Markdown with a fence of `js` and HTML with a `<script>` the document that made each spin (red with the guard off, 10 s, and green with it, 2 s in debug) and checks that the keywords of the code in a fence and a tag are still colored; the long fuzz of the whole set (ignored, minutes, `SIM_HANG_DOCS` and `SIM_HANG_SEED`) in a process for each syntax with a watchdog on every line, and the files of the repository. With the guard 0 of 195 syntaxes spin in 1,500 and in 6,000 documents of each, and the parse of 3,000 documents of each (where no circle is met) is the same as without it.
  - **Decided** (P3): where the highlighter has given a file up, the bar says `colors stopped` among its state words (UI-10) for as long as it is so, as GIT-5's frame says what the guard did for as long as it did it: the status line's message above is said once, and a key ends it. The highlighter's answer carries it (`Hl::stopped`: true for every answer to a file that was given up, which the thread answers plain at once from then on), so the word is there from the first plain answer until the file is closed; the file stays shown, plain, and the rest of the bar is as it was. Calm has no bar and keeps the message, as it keeps GIT-5's. `ui_10_the_bar_says_colors_stopped_for_as_long_as_the_highlighter_has_given_the_file_up`, the flag in `syn_3_a_parse_that_takes_too_long_is_given_up_and_the_other_files_are_still_highlighted`, and E2E.
  - **Decided** (P4): a file that is closed is forgotten by the highlighter (`HlJob::Forget`, sent with the shell's `Close`): the parse states and the rope it keeps to find where a text changed would otherwise stay, for every file ever opened, as long as sim runs.
  - **Decided** (P4): `ropey` is a dependency of sim for this, at the core's version and features (the core has it, so the build gains no code): the thread must be given the text without copying it.
- **SYN-4** [P2][S3] The language comes from the file name and extension, then from the first line (`#!`); the languages are those of bat's syntax set — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the syntax is looked up by the whole file name (`Makefile`, `.bashrc`), then by the extension, then by the first line (`#!`, `<?xml`); case is ignored. Plain text counts as no syntax. `Syn::new` runs off the first frame, since it loads the set.
  - **Decided** (P2): changed from "syntect's default set" at the author's request: syntect's default set has no TOML, TypeScript, TSX, Dockerfile, Kotlin, Zig, Svelte or Nix, which the author uses every day, and a highlighter that shows them plain is a highlighter that does not work for them. The `two-face` crate ships bat's syntaxes (213, including all of syntect's) as a compressed dump for syntect; with `default-features = false` and `syntect-fancy` it uses the pure-Rust regex backend and no onig (checked with `cargo tree -i onig`), and syntect's own `default-syntaxes` is off so that the binary holds one set only. Measured on a release build: the binary is 4.17 MB with bat's set and 3.57 MB with syntect's (0.6 MB for the extra languages; without syntect at all sim is 1.2 MB); loading the set takes 0.6 ms (0.3 ms before) and the first window of Rust 17 ms, in the highlighter's thread. Its scopes are what `RULES` (SYN-1) was written for; goldens for Rust, Python, shell, Markdown, TOML, TypeScript and a Dockerfile keep them. Kotlin, Zig, Svelte and Nix have a syntax; the table says nothing special about them.
  - **Decided** (P4): `.js`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.es6` and a first line that names node are read with the syntax of TypeScript, and `.jsx` with that of TypeScriptReact; bat's "JavaScript" is never used. syntect's parser looped for ever in it on common code (`x = y /* c */`: any statement without a semicolon that ends in a block comment; closed by the guard of SYN-3, but the set has no `.mjs`, `.cjs` or `.jsx` at all (bat maps those in code of its own, which two-face does not carry). TypeScript's syntax reads JavaScript, and does not loop on this (a thread that is waited for, in the test). This departs from "the languages are those of bat's syntax set, by extension": the language is JavaScript's, the syntax file is TypeScript's. The watchdog of SYN-3 stays for a parse that is slow.

  - **Decided** (P2): the author's dotfiles are chezmoi's source tree, where `dot_zshrc` is `.zshrc` and `dot_gitconfig.tmpl` is `.gitconfig` on the machine; Neovim colors them and sim showed them plain, since no syntax has the name `dot_zshrc`. A file whose name has chezmoi's attributes (`private_ readonly_ executable_ exact_ empty_ encrypted_`, `dot_`, a `.tmpl` at its end) is looked up by what it stands for (`icons::target`, which the tree's icons use too), then by its own name; a name without them is looked up as before.
- **SYN-5** [P2][S3] The Visual selection has a neutral gray background (lightness only, |Lc| ≥ 15 against the background, text on it |Lc| ≥ 75), as in common editors; the core's underline stays for terminals without truecolor — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the gray is the background's own lightness moved (away from it, as for SYN-1) with no chroma until |Lc| ≥ 15, and its text is a neutral gray moved until |Lc| ≥ 75 against it; both are computed with the colors of SYN-1 (so they need COLORTERM and a background reply; otherwise the selection is the core's underline). On the selection the syntax looks are dropped (the text is the neutral gray, plain), so that the text keeps |Lc| ≥ 75 whatever the hue; the change style wins over the gray. Checked on `#000000 #1e1e1e #282c34 #002b36 #ffffff #fafafa #fdf6e3 #eff1f5`.
## Keys and help

Help comes in layers, lightest first, so that an expert is never slowed (progressive disclosure): a hint at the moment of need, which-key on a pause, `:help` for everything. The author's Neovim has `mapleader = ' '`, which-key and BufferLinePick, and these are their counterparts.

- **KEY-1** [P2][S7] One key table is the only source of which-key and `:help`, and of the README's table of keys when that is made from it: `sim/keys.tsv`, a line for each entry, `keys`, `modes`, a one-line description and a longer text, separated by tabs. `keys` are tokens separated by blanks (`g g`, `<Space> b p`, `r {char}`); a token may have alternatives (`h|<Left>`, `w|W`). `modes` are letters: `n` Normal, `v` Visual, `o` after an operator (the motions and the text objects), `i` Insert, `c` the command line, `t` the tree, `p` the picker. A test feeds every key, every second key after each prefix and every third after `i` and `a` after an operator to a Sim in a state in which any bound key changes something, and checks that the keys that do something are exactly the Normal-mode keys of the table, no more and no fewer — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the table is data in a file, not code in a list of strings: it is prose (a hundred lines of descriptions for people), the cap on lines is a pressure on code, and the README's table can be made of the same file. The code that reads it (`keys.rs`) counts.
  - **Decided** (P2): a key is bound when typing it changes the screen or the state of the editor (the text, the cursor, the mode, a message, a pending prefix, the panel, a picker, the quit): the check is on what a key does, not on a list in the code, so that a key added to the core, to sim or to this file without the other two is found by the test. Text that is typed (the char after `f`, `t` and `r`, Insert mode, the command line, the picker's query) is not a key of this table, and counts are one entry (`1` to `9`, and `0` after them).
  - **Decided** (P2): the descriptions say what a key does, in the words of the author's Neovim where there is a counterpart (`{motion}`'s are the vi grammar's, which the core documents once in the long text of the operator). Where sim differs from vi, the long text says so (`Y` yanks to the end of the line, `U` redoes, a count of `G` is a line).
- **KEY-2** [P5][S7] Space is a leader, as in the author's Neovim: in Normal mode and in the tree a Space starts a sequence, and the keys after it run it (`<Space> b p`, `<Space> b c`, `<Space> b k`). Space followed by a key that no sequence has does nothing, and Esc ends it. Where Space is text (Insert mode, the command line, the query of the tree or of the picker) it is the char — U, I
  - **Decided** (P5): sequences of the leader are sim's: the core's parser never sees a Space (in Normal mode it has no meaning), and the keys that complete a sequence are not given to the core either, so `<Space> b x` leaves no half-typed command in it. The pending sequence is one of the prefixes that which-key and the key table know (`Sim::prefix`).
- **KEY-3** [P2][S7] A pause on a prefix opens which-key: a small window near the bottom of the screen, with a rounded border and the prefix as its title, that lists the next keys one line each as `key  description` from the key table. The prefixes are Space and what follows it (`<Space> b`), `g`, `z`, `Z`, `[`, `]`, and an operator (`d`, `c`, `y`, `<`, `>`, `!`) with what follows it (`d i`), in Normal and Visual mode and in the tree. The delay is 200 ms, which-key's default for a mapping that is not a plugin's (`delay` in `config.lua`). Typing on is exactly what it is without the window: the keys are the editor's, Esc closes it by ending the prefix, and the window is never given a key. Calm draws it without color — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): a key after the pause (or a pause that never ends) changes nothing in the prefix: the window is a view of the prefix and not a mode. It is made when the shell's timer says the pause is over (`SimEvent::Pause(n)`, asked for by `SimEffect::Pause(n)` as the spinner's frames are), and only for the prefix that is still pending and the key typed last: a pause that was asked for before a later key is dropped. Counts, and the char after `f`, `t`, `T`, `F` and `r`, are not prefixes.
  - **Decided** (P2): the rows of a prefix are the next token of every entry whose keys begin with it, once each, with the description of the entry that is exactly that key, or of its first entry; after an operator they are the entries of mode `o` that begin with what follows it, and the operator's own key (`dd`). A window is as high as the rows, up to 16 and up to what the screen has (at least 3 rows above it, the tab bar and the status line below): it is a small window, not a page, and ends in `… n more (:help)` where the rows do not all fit. It is laid over the text as the picker is (PICK-1), on the surface of the interface in flat and depth. It has no shadow, which the picker has in depth: a dark strip beside a window this small read as a second cursor in a screenshot. The terminal's cursor is not the window's: it stays at the cursor of the text, as the Painter writes it, and nothing outside the window changes (`key_3_nothing_outside_the_window_changes_and_the_terminal_s_cursor_stays_in_the_text`).
- **KEY-4** [P2][S7] `:help` and F1 open the picker (PICK-1) as `Help · keys and commands`: one row for each entry of the key table, `{keys}  {description}  {mode}`, fuzzy-filtered as the finder filters paths (FIND-1), best first; the preview shows the longer text under the keys and the modes. Enter and Esc close it, and it runs nothing. `:help {words}` opens it with the words typed. Typing `tab` finds `<Tab>`, `<S-Tab>` and `<Space>bp` — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): F1 works in every mode and in the tree, and while a picker is open it is the picker's (it closes it). The query is matched against the row as it is written, so what a user sees is what is searched; the keys of an entry with alternatives are written with ` / ` between them (`h / <Left>`, `i< / i>`).
  - **Decided** (P2): keys are written for people, in the help, in the preview and in which-key's rows and title: the escapes of the table are never shown, `<lt>` is `<`, `<Bar>` is `|` and `<gt>` is `>` (an operator typed `<` has the title `<`, and `<<` outdents a line), and the names of keys that have no char stay (`<Space>`, `<Tab>`, `<C-n>`). More than four alternatives are written first and last (`1 … 9`). A row of the help whose keys are wider than its column (16) keeps the alternatives that fit and ends the keys in ` …` (`<Down> / <C-n> …`), never half of a key; the preview has them all. The test that no row, title or preview shows `<lt>`, `<gt>` or `<bar>` is `key_3_no_row_or_title_of_the_window_shows_an_escape_of_the_table` and `key_4_no_row_or_preview_of_the_help_shows_an_escape_of_the_table`.
- **KEY-5** [P5][S7] `<Space> b p` puts a letter on each tab, and the letter jumps to its file, as the author's BufferLinePick does; `<Space> b c` does the same to close one. The letters are bufferline's: its alphabet `abcdefghijklmopqrstuvwxyzABCDEFGHIJKLMOPQRSTUVWXYZ1234567890`, the first letter of the file's name if it is in it and not taken, else the first one of the alphabet that is not. Esc, or any key that is no tab's letter, ends it and does nothing. A file that is modified is not closed by `<Space> b c`: it follows `:q` (its message, `no write since last change (:q! to override)`, and its tab is shown), never `bdelete!` — the author's nvim forces, sim never loses work (P1) — U, I
  - **Decided** (P5): where there is no tab bar (calm, a screen too short for the interface) the letters are on the status line (`pick: a point.rs · b lib.rs · c README.md`) until the key. A tab's letter is drawn in the place of its icon, bold on the accent's block in flat and depth, inverse without color.
- **KEY-6** [P2][S7] The first time a second file is open the message line says `Tab / S-Tab: next / previous file`, once for the session, until the keys have been used three times: after three uses of Tab or Shift-Tab it never shows again. How many times they were used is kept in the state directory (`tab-uses`, a number, never more than 3), as the icons' hint is (ICON-1) — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): "used" is a Tab or a Shift-Tab that changed the file shown; `<Space> b p` and a click on a tab are other ways and do not count, since the hint teaches the two keys. The hint is a message and not a window: the next key puts it out, as for any message.

- **CLI-7** [P5][S7] `sim --version` prints `sim {version}` (the Cargo version) and exits 0; `sim --help` prints a short usage — the arguments, `--plain`, `--version`, `--help`, the variables `NO_COLOR`, `SIM_ON_NORMAL` and `XDG_STATE_HOME`, and "inside sim: :help or F1" — and exits 0; an argument that starts with `-` and is none of these (and `--plain`) prints the same usage to stderr and exits 2, as CLI-1 has it. None of them touches the terminal: no raw mode, no query, no escape — U, E
  - **Decided** (P5): they are looked for before anything else, among the options (before a `--`), so `sim --plain --version` and `sim --version a.txt` print the version; packaging asks for them (Homebrew's formula test, the AUR's `check`). The usage says what sim is in the owner's words, the next-generation Vim, and never that it is like another editor.

## Pointer

- **PTR-1** [P2][S4] The mouse: a click in the panel moves there (a click on a file opens it, on a directory toggles it); a click in the text moves the cursor; the wheel scrolls what is under the pointer; dragging in the text selects as Visual mode does — I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): sim reports the mouse in SGR coding (1006) with button events (1000) and drags (1002), asked for after the core's `enter` and given back before the core's `leave` by every way out: a quit, SIGTERM and SIGHUP (they end in the quit), and the panic hook (`paint::leave`, which writes `MOUSE_OFF`, the reverse of `MOUSE_ON`, and then the core's `term::leave`); the E2E cases ask tmux which mouse modes the pane has on, while running and after each way out. Only the left button acts; the other buttons, a pointer that only moves, and the horizontal wheel are ignored. A pointer is in the panel (its columns, the rows of the body), in the text (after the blank column), in neither (the blank column, the status line), or on the status line: the last two take every event and do nothing with it.
  - **Decided** (P2): a click on a row of the panel puts the panel's cursor there and gives it the focus, and does what Enter does there (TREE-4): a file is opened (and the focus goes to the text, as it does for Enter), a directory that is closed is expanded and one that is open is collapsed; a click below the last row does nothing. It cancels a reveal that waits for a listing, as a key of the panel's own does, and a search that is typed stops being typed (the matches stay lit; a click on a file opens it and ends the search, as Enter does).
  - **Decided** (P2): a click in the text gives the text the focus and puts the cursor on the char under the pointer, as a key that moved it would: through the App's own `clamp` (a Normal-mode cursor rests on a char, not after the last one; Insert mode may be after it), with the wanted column starting again and the mark of the line it enters seen (DISK-6). The char is found through the frame the user sees (`layout::hit`, the walk of `layout::colors`): the line of a row is read from its gutter, a wrapped line goes on in the rows below, a tab, a wide char and the tokens of SAFE-8 (`^X`, `<U+XXXX>`) take the columns the core gives them (`view::width`), so a point on any column of a char is that char; a point in the gutter is the start of its row, past the end of a row that goes on the last char of the row, past the end of a line the end of the line, and below the text the last line. While a command or a search is typed on the command line, a click or the wheel in the text does nothing to it.
  - **Decided** (P2): a drag begins with a press in the text and goes on with the pointer. The first move to another char starts Visual mode where the press put the cursor, by the key `v` (so its looks and keys are Visual's), and from then on the pointer's char is the cursor; going back to the press leaves one char selected; a pointer outside the text is at its edge (above the first row the first row, below the last the last, left of the text the start of its row); releasing keeps the selection, and the next click ends it (as Esc would). Insert mode and a half-typed command select nothing (a `v` would be text), and a selection ended by a key meanwhile is not taken up again. Double and triple clicks, scrolling by dragging past the edge, and the right and middle buttons are not built (NOTES).
  - **Decided** (P2): the wheel over the text scrolls it three lines a notch (a wrapped line counts as one: the App's view starts at a line), and the cursor keeps the context VIS-12 gives it: sim sets the App's top and has `render` lay the screen out; while `render` pushes the view back to keep the cursor in its context, the cursor moves towards the view by the lines it was pushed (keeping its column), so the cursor moves only when it has to. A scroll that moves nothing (the text's end, or a text that fits) leaves the cursor where it was; the mark of the line the cursor ends on is seen. The wheel over the panel scrolls its rows three a notch and keeps the panel's cursor on the screen; it gives no focus. On the command line the wheel does nothing.
  - **Decided** (P4): an event of the mouse is timed as a key is (SPEED-1's bench, kind `mouse`). A wheel notch and the first move of a drag render the App's frame twice (the cursor goes where the first render showed it must; `v` renders), so the bound for the mouse is twice a key's.
- **PTR-2** [P1][S4] The mouse never edits by itself: a click changes the cursor, the focus, the scroll and the selection only; a click in the status line does nothing — I, E
  - **Decided** (P1): the mouse does not complete what a key began, and does not decide for the user: a click forgets a count, an operator or a prefix that waits for the rest of its command (Esc to the App: `d`, a click, `w` deletes nothing), and ends a Visual selection; the wheel forgets a half-typed command too, but leaves a Visual selection alone. Nothing the mouse does is an edit, a save, a snapshot, a filter or a copy: checked by running thousands of events of every kind at every place, in every mode, against two files, on screens from 80x24 down to 3 columns and 2 rows, and looking at the text, the modified flag and the effects asked for (a click on the panel may open a file, which is no edit). The smallest screens found that a screen narrower than its gutter, whose line numbers are cut, made `Sim::colors` ask the highlighter for a window that ends before it starts: fixed.

## Look

sim has three looks, which differ in how the change is shown and in how much of the screen is drawn around the text. In calm the change color stays the only chromatic background in the text (P3); flat and depth show what comes on green and what goes on red (LOOK-2).

- **LOOK-1** [P2][S5] The looks are `calm` (exactly the core's four styles: nothing in this section is drawn), `flat` and `depth`. `zl` cycles calm, flat, depth, calm; the status line says `look: depth` until the next key. The choice is remembered in sim's state directory for the next run, and the first run is depth. There is no config file — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): `zl` is like SYN-2's `zh`: taken when the `z` was typed in Normal mode with nothing pending and the next key is `l`, in the text (not while the panel has the focus); the App still gets both keys (its parser drops `zl`). It holds for every open file.
  - **Decided** (P2): the choice is the file `$XDG_STATE_HOME/sim/look` (else `$HOME/.local/state/sim/look`: MARK-2's directory and rules, `io::state_dir`) with one word, `calm`, `flat` or `depth`. A `zl` asks the journal thread to write it, by the same atomic private write as the history; the first failure is told once on the status line. A file that is missing, unreadable or holds anything else means depth. `Env` names the look `Sim` starts with and its default is calm, so that the tests of everything above see the core's styles; the binary gives it what the file says, or depth.
  - **Decided** (P4): all of the looks are computed from what the screen is (the frame of the core's App, the marks of its buffer, the mode, the cursor) by sim's own pure code, and drawn by the Painter from colors that `layout` has already worked out (`paint::Paint`: a foreground, a background, a fade, for a run of cells). the core's `src/` is not touched.
- **LOOK-2** [P2,P3][S5] Flat is calm with two hues in place of the core's one, and a tint behind every row of a change. Green (OKLCH hue 150) is the background of changed and added words and of the marks of changes from disk (only the new text is shown there), and of the gutter's `+` and `~`; red (hue 25) is the background of the gutter's `-` and of a deletion point's row. A row of a line that only gained or changed text has a background tint from the first text column to the right edge of the editor pane, `mix(bg, green, 0.30)` in OKLab; a deletion point's row `mix(bg, red, 0.30)`. A gap of exactly one blank cell between two cells on the same one of the two backgrounds takes it too, so that the words are one patch. The continuation rows of a changed line get the gutter's symbol column in the color of the line, one continuous bar. A tree row whose name is on green (MARK-1) has the tint across the panel — U
  - **Decided** (P3): the owner's call, 2026-10-16: green for what comes and red for what goes, as GitHub, VS Code and delta show a diff, and color vision deficiency is not a requirement of flat and depth. This departs from P3's single chromatic hue for these two looks; calm is exactly the core's look (blue, VIS-4 and VIS-5 with their color vision checks) and is the choice, with `zl`, for anyone who cannot tell red from green.
  - **Decided** (P2): each hue is found the way the core finds its change color (VIS-4; `color::hued` is that search with the hue as a parameter, and the core's palette and `src/` are not touched): the most chromatic color of that hue at each OKLCH lightness, and of those the nearest to the page that is at least |Lc| 15 and a margin of 0.5 from it, or the farthest when none is; the color vision simulations are not applied to these two. The text on each is a gray at |Lc| ≥ 75.5 against it, on primary's side of the page: primary's own gray moved in lightness by `color::contrast` until it reads (`change_text` finds the nearest gray by steps of one level; the result differs by two levels at most), and is put on the words (their own text color is never a syntax color, as before).
  - **Decided** (P2): a row has a change when its gutter symbol (the first column) is not blank, and so do the rows after it whose gutter is blank (the wrapped rows of the line): the frame says it, so nothing is asked of the core. Its kind is read from the gutter: a `-` is a deletion point and the rest gained or changed text. A conflict with the disk (SAFE-7) is drawn as a mark is: its rows are mine, marked whole, on the change style, which is green here, and `~` on green.
  - **Decided** (P2): the Painter lays a background of a paint over the style's own, the change style's blue included; `layout` gives a paint to every word of the change style, ahead of the tint of its row (the first paint that names a cell decides), so the Visual selection's gray still wins over both and nothing but the words is on green or red.
- **LOOK-3** [P2][S5] Depth is flat plus btop-like rounded boxes (`╭╮╰╯─│`) around the panel and the editor pane. The lines are `mix(bg, secondary, 0.45)`, the box that has the focus is the secondary gray. The top border carries a superscript number and a title, bold: `¹files` for the panel, `²` and the file's path (shortened by the core's `view::shorten` rule) for the editor. After the title come the badges: ` 2 changes on disk ` on green, and ` 1 conflict with the disk ` on the neutral surface (the card's tone) with primary text. the core's status line stays in the row below the boxes, for messages and the command line — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the layout of a screen `w` x `h` with the panel open is `│panel│││editor│`: the panel box is `pw + 2` columns wide (`pw` is TREE-1's), the editor box `ew + 2`, so `ew = w - pw - 4` (`w - 2` with no panel). Row 0 is the top border, rows `1..h-2` the body, row `h-2` the bottom border and row `h-1` the status line. The App is given `h - 2` rows, so that its last row, which is its status line, is what the screen's last row shows. `Sim::widths` is the inner width of each box; `layout::Geo` says where everything starts, and the mouse (PTR-1) and the panel's paging use it, so that a click on a border does nothing and the rest lands where it is drawn.
  - **Decided** (P2): the border has `╭─`, then the title with no space around it (the number in the secondary gray when the box has the focus, else dimmed toward the page: `mix(secondary, bg, 0.35)`; the title bold, primary (no color of its own) or dimmed likewise), then one `─` and the badge, then `─` to the right end. The badge is the notes of the core's status line for changes and a conflict: `{n} change(s) on disk` (a run of marked lines is one) and `1 conflict with the disk` (SAFE-7), one badge each when both exist. With no file open the editor's border has no title (a title is made of the path with the core's SAFE-8 rule for names, so a hostile char in it is U+FFFD). The path is cut to what the border has left (`layout::short`) after the badges and `i/n`, and keeps 10 columns at least; what does not fit beside it is cut at the corner (a pathological box of 25 columns shows the start of the first badge).
  - **Decided** (P2): the focus is the panel's when it is open and focused, else the editor's. The keys of the bottom border are cut at the corner when the box is narrow. The panel's bottom border and the editor's in Normal mode are plain: the position and mode are in the core's status line, which stays whole.
  - **Decided** (P4): a screen narrower than 40 columns or shorter than 10 rows is too small for the boxes (the editor would be left with fewer than 24 columns): depth is then flat, with the core's geometry. The App is told the size of what is drawn, so nothing is cut.
- **LOOK-4** [P2][S5] In depth the editor box's right border is an overview ruler: each row of the border stands for a range of the file's lines, and is a green `┃` when a mark is in that range (a red one where there is only a deletion point: a `-` mark), a `┃` in the lines' color for the lines that are on screen, and the plain border otherwise — U
  - **Decided** (P2): row `r` of `rows` stands for the lines `l` of a file of `n` lines with `l * rows / n == r`, so a short file marks the rows its lines are on and a long one marks every row that has a change in its range, green before red before the lines on screen. The rows of the lines on screen are those of the first and last line the frame shows.
- **LOOK-6** [P2][S5] Depth shades the text like btop's gradients: a row that is no change fades toward the page's background by its distance `d`, in rows, from the cursor's row, `f = min(0.45, max(0, (d - 3) / 24 * 0.45))`, on every foreground color of the row, the syntax colors and the line numbers included. Changes (the tints, the green and red, the gutter, the badges, the ruler) and the status line never fade, and a fade never takes text below |Lc| 75 against the page (APCA's minimum for body text) and the line numbers below |Lc| 40: it stops where that is reached. Secondary text (comments, done tasks, markers, URLs, the dim `#`s, deleted text) does not fade at all: it is rank, and the fog is distance. Flat and calm have no fog — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the owner's call, 2026-10-17: the floor of the text goes from |Lc| 40 to 75, APCA's minimum for body text, so the far rows of a file never fall below body readability; the numbers keep 40 (they are for finding a line, not for reading). The gutter of a row has a paint of its own with the same fade and the lower floor (`Paint::soft`), ahead of the row's.
  - **Decided** (P2): the owner's call, 2026-10-17, replacing a floor of 60 that was tried first: the fog does not fade the secondary gray. With a floor of 60 the fog took primary text to where secondary text already sits (measured on the pixels of a depth screen: the brightest text of a done task, secondary, was 179 and of an open one, primary, 182, far from the cursor), so in far rows every cue of the kind "lighter means more important" (done tasks, markers, dim URLs, comments) collapsed, and lightness would have carried two meanings, distance and rank (COLOR-1: lightness and weight are the channel of rank, Mackinlay 1986). Primary text fades no lower than 75 and secondary text stays where it is (about 60), so at the farthest fog a primary span is at least 15 Lc above a secondary one on the same row, on each of the eight backgrounds (measured: primary 75.3 to 76.8, secondary 59.8 to 60.2, the least gap 15.3; `cargo test -p sim --test look_paint farthest -- --nocapture` prints them). The margin is thin by construction (75 less 60), and the gap is the test's to hold: moving either floor needs the test to pass again. The Painter fades a cell only if its foreground is not the palette's secondary (or the cell is the gutter's: the line numbers keep their own fade, down to 40).
  - **Decided** (P2): the cursor's row is the row of the App's frame the cursor is on (a wrapped line has several rows, and the distance is in rows); on the command line, which takes the cursor to the status line, it is the row it was on before. The fade of a row is one paint over its columns, so a Visual selection's gray and the syntax colors are faded with the rest.
- **LOOK-7** [P2][S5] The tree is not in fog: its rows are all as clear as the editor's, from the row of the open file to the last (the distance from the open file means nothing in a tree, which is where files are looked for, and a faded name is harder to find). The one fade there is is relevance: during the panel's `/` search (FIND-1) the rows fade by their distance `d` from the cursor row, `min(0.7, 0.12 + d / 16 * 0.6)`, except the bold rows (the matches and their directories, the rows that have a match). The editor's own fog (LOOK-6) is as it was — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the review of 2026-10-18, under the owner's delegation, took out the gradient that this ID had at first (`f = min(0.6, max(0, (d - 1) / 14 * 0.6))` by the distance from the open file's row, or from the tree's cursor while it had the focus), and with it the rule that a row with a git status or on green never fades: no row fades now but a non-match of a search, and a file with a status keeps its color whatever the rest of the row is (UI-3).
  - **Decided** (P2): the fade of a search is for a search that has a query (one that lights nothing dims nothing), and for as long as the panel has the focus: with the focus in the editor the tree is not faded at all, a search lit or not. The row of the open file (which the tree marks with the card's surface, UI-2) is the row of its path in the tree; when a directory above it is closed, the row of the nearest directory above it that is shown.
- **LOOK-8** [P2][S5] Without a palette (no answer to OSC 11) or without truecolor (VIS-4's stand-ins) flat and depth draw no tints, fog, shading or joined words: flat is calm, and depth is calm inside its boxes, in the terminal's default colors — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the tint, the fog and the surfaces are colors of the palette, and a terminal that does not give one gets the core's reverse video and faint, which would turn a gutter bar or a joined gap into noise; the boxes use no color of their own then (the badges are bold, with no color of their own). Truecolor is the `COLORTERM` of SYN-1.
- **LOOK-9** [P2][S5] Primary text keeps |Lc| ≥ 75 against every tint of flat (green, red, neutral) and against the card's, and the text on green and on red |Lc| ≥ 75, on `#000000 #1e1e1e #282c34 #002b36 #ffffff #fafafa #fdf6e3 #eff1f5` (VIS-4's list) — U
  - **Decided** (P2): measured on those backgrounds, the lowest |Lc| of primary text on any tint or the card is 77.1 (the card, on `#eff1f5`; the neutral tint and the green and red ones are at 81 or more), and the text grays on green and red reach 75.5 or more, as the core's text on its change color does. The card's mix was 22% and fell to 74.4 on the light backgrounds, so it is 18%. The tests print the numbers (`cargo test -p sim --test look_paint look_9 -- --nocapture`) and check the hue (within 4 degrees), the floor against the page and the text on each of the two, on the eight backgrounds.

## Interface

The author compared sim with the author's own Neovim (Neo-tree, BufferLine, lualine, gitsigns, indent-blankline, todo-comments, render-markdown, `listchars`) side by side and found the drop far too big: sim had to match every element of it and beat it where it can (depth, what the agent changed, what is safe). This section is that interface. It belongs to flat and depth; calm stays si-like and minimal.

- **UI-1** [P2,P3][S6] In flat and depth, where LOOK-8 lets them draw tints (a palette and truecolor), sim draws the interface of UI-2 to UI-11: the tree's header, icons and git status, the tab bar, the sign column, indent guides and whitespace, TODO badges, markdown, the status line with the security state, and the right edge. Calm draws none of it, and neither do LOOK-8's fallbacks: they are what they were. Every color of it comes from the terminal's palette and has one meaning, which COLOR-1 says: neutral grays and weight for structure, green, amber and red for changes, blue for where you act, magenta for what is hidden or dangerous — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P3): the owner's call, 2026-10-16: blue is free for chrome, since flat and depth show diffs as red and green (LOOK-2) and calm, which keeps blue as the change color, draws no chrome. Syntax colors still never use it (SYN-1), so in the text blue means interface, never code. (2026-10-17: and it means one thing, the accent: COLOR-1.)
  - **Decided** (P2): a hue of the interface is the terminal's ANSI color as SYN-1 derives it (red 1, green 2, amber 3, magenta 5; the reply of OSC 4 when it came, else the OKLCH hue of its class) and blue, which is not asked for (the query stays at five replies, so a quit waits for no more than before) and is derived: OKLCH hue 250, chroma 0.12, lightness 0.5. Each is moved in lightness by `color::contrast` until |Lc| ≥ 75.5 against the card (the raised neutral surface, the strongest one), so it reads on every neutral surface and on the page (the contrast of a fixed color against the line from the page to `secondary` is monotonic).
  - **Decided** (P2): a block is the accent's hue found as LOOK-2 finds green and red (`color::hued`: the nearest color to the page that is at least |Lc| 15.5 from it), and the text on it is `color::text_on` (≥ 75.5), so a mode block or a badge reads as the green of a change does.
  - **Decided** (P2): the neutral surfaces are mixes of the page toward secondary, in OKLab: the cursor's line 6%, a fenced code block 9%, the tab bar and the status line 10%, inline code 14%, the open file's row of the tree and the active tab and the card 18%. None is above the card's 18%, so primary text keeps LOOK-9's floor on all of them (UI-12). Guides, whitespace marks and the lines of boxes are decoration, not text: they have no floor, only to be seen. On a page of pure black the 6% and 9% steps are under one level of eight bits and are the page's own color: the surfaces collapse there, and the text on them is as readable as on the page.

  - **Decided** (P4): the tree, the header and the tabs are drawn when `Sim::ui()` is true: the look is flat or depth with tones (LOOK-8) and the shell has asked for the interface (`Env.chrome`; the binary does, and the tests of the looks that predate it start without, as they start in calm, so that their screens stay what LOOK-1 to LOOK-9 describe). Markdown (UI-8) and the Painter's single colors are not behind it. With the interface the screen has a header row (`Geo::head`, 1 on a screen of 10 rows or more): the first row inside the boxes, the panel's root and the editor's tabs; the body starts under it, so the mouse, the cursor and the pages of the panel, all of which count from `Geo::y0`, follow.

- **UI-2** [P2][S6] The first row of the panel is a header: the root, home-relative (`~/.local/share/chezmoi`; the path as it is when it is not below `$HOME`, `~` for `$HOME`), in bold, with an open folder icon; the editor's tab bar (UI-4) is on the same row. The rows below it show a Nerd Font icon before each name, in the secondary gray whatever the type (icons are monochrome, COLOR-1): a closed or an open folder, and a file icon by extension and well-known name (about forty entries: `rs md toml json yaml yml sh zsh bash lua py js ts tsx jsx go c h cpp hpp html css scss sql png jpg svg pdf`, `Dockerfile`, `Makefile`, `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes`, `LICENSE`, `README*`, `*.lock`, the default file and the default folder). `zi` turns the icons off and on for the session and the choice is remembered, as `zl`'s is: icons off shows today's `▸ ` and `▾ ` for directories and blanks for files. The icons are on at the first run — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the `zi` key is `zl`'s and `zh`'s (a `z` in Normal mode with nothing pending, then `i`, in the text; the App still gets both, and its parser drops `zi`). The choice is the file `$XDG_STATE_HOME/sim/icons` (`io::state_dir`'s directory) with the word `on` or `off`, written by the journal thread as the look is; a missing or other file is on. `Env.icons` says what `Sim` starts with (off in the tests, so that goldens stay ASCII unless a test says on); the status line says `icons off` or `icons on` until the next key.
  - **Decided** (P2): a row is a margin column (`•` for a likely file, MARK-2), a guide `│` and a blank for each level above it (the guide in the lines' color), the icon in the color of its type (an accent, or secondary for a type without one) and a blank, the name, and at the right edge two cells: a blank and the git letter or dot (UI-3). The terminal's cursor in the panel is on the first char of the name (`layout::name_col`). The name is cut to what is left (`…` is not used: the panel cuts, as before). A folder's icon is the open or the closed one by its state, and is blue; a name is looked up by its whole name (`README*` and `LICENSE*` by their start), then its extension, case ignored, after chezmoi's own decorations are taken off (`.tmpl`, and `dot_`, `private_`, `executable_`, `readonly_`, `exact_`, `empty_` at the front, so `dot_zshrc.tmpl` is `.zshrc`). The panel's marks of calm (`▸`, `~`) are not drawn when icons are on, and a directory has no slash after its name; with icons off the `▸ ` and `▾ ` marks and the slash stay as in calm, and the `~` is the dot of UI-3. A name that does not fit is cut at the room before the edge, as the panel always did (no `…`).
  - **Decided** (P2): the home directory is the shell's (`Env.home`, `$HOME`): the core reads no environment. A root below it is shown with its `~/`; a path that only starts with the same letters (`/home/alice2`, when home is `/home/alice`) is not below it.

- **UI-3** [P2,P3][S6] The tree shows git status per entry, from the `git status --porcelain=v1 -z` that MARK-1 already runs: a letter at the right edge of a file's row (`M` modified in amber, `A` added in green, `U` untracked in green with the name in italic, `D` deleted in red, `R` renamed in amber, `!` conflicted in red and bold) and the name in the same color; a directory shows the letter of the strongest status below it, in its color (conflict `!`, deleted `D`, modified `M`, renamed `R`, added `A`, untracked `U`: red, red, amber, amber, green, green; COLOR-3: a letter, not a dot, so that it needs no color). Names that start with a dot and entries that git ignores are in the secondary gray. The open file's row is on the surface (the 18% one), the indent guides are in the lines' color, and sim's own marks stay: a file that changed on disk since sim opened it is on green across the row, and likely files have their `•`, in the secondary gray — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the status of a path is its conflict if X or Y is `U`, or `AA` or `DD`; else its work tree's letter Y when it is `M` (or `T`, a type change) or `D`; else its index's letter X (`M`, `A`, `D`, `R`; a copy is added); `??` is untracked. So a file staged as added and modified after is `M`, and a renamed file that was also edited shows `M` (the rename is told by the directories it left, which have the blue dot). A deleted file is not in the tree (it is not on disk), so it has no row to carry `D`: the dot of its directories is red.
  - **Decided** (P2): a file row with a status keeps its color whatever the rest of the row is (a dotfile that changed is amber, not gray), and a row with a status never fades in depth (LOOK-7): it is news. The name of a file in conflict is bold, of an untracked one italic (the Painter's italic look of emphasis).
  - **Decided** (P2): ignored entries come from a second `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --ignored=traditional --untracked-files=normal --ignore-submodules=all` run by the same guard, of which only the `!!` lines are kept (so a directory such as `target/` is one entry, and its files are not listed), and appended to the status as `!! path` entries; a path is ignored when it, or a directory above it, is listed. `-uall` is not used for it: with it git lists every file of an ignored directory.
  - **Decided** (P3): in flat and depth the change color on a name (MARK-1) is for the files that changed on disk while sim ran; git's own changes show by letter and color as above, so a file is not marked twice. Calm is as MARK-1 says.
  - **Decided** (P2): the branch comes from the same status (`--branch`, the `## branch...upstream` entry, or `## No commits yet on branch`, or `## HEAD (no branch)` shown as `HEAD`) and is the status line's (UI-10).

- **UI-4** [P2][S6] The first row of the editor pane is a tab bar, one tab for each open file, in order: its icon and name, and the parent directory with the name (`src/mod.rs`, as many components as it takes) when two open files have the same name; `●` in bold when the file is modified (unsaved: weight, not a color); and `↯` in green when it changed on disk while it was open and the change is not yet looked at (whoever changed it: an editor, a build, a tool, an agent; COLOR-3: an arrow, not a second dot). The active tab is raised (the 18% surface, bold) with a bar `▎` in the accent at its left (the author's `indicator = { icon = '▎', style = 'icon' }`); the other tabs are on the bar's surface, and thin separators `▏` are between tabs (`separator_style = "thin"`). After a name there is a slot for a language server's counts of errors and warnings (`✘ 2 ▲ 1`, the shapes of the author's diagnostics), in the text's own gray, the shapes saying which, which the server's own code fills (`Buf::diag`). When the tabs do not fit, those around the active one are shown, with `‹` and `›` where tabs are hidden. A click on a tab shows that file, on `‹` or `›` the previous or the next; it is only a switch (PTR-2) — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the bar is always there while a file is open, one tab or many, as BufferLine's `always_show_bufferline` is. With no file open the row is the bar's surface, empty. A tab is the indicator (a blank in the others), a blank, the icon and a blank (with icons off, no icon), the name, the counts, the dots and a blank; between two tabs a thin separator. The `2/5` of BUF-2 is not added to the status line where the bar is (a screen of 10 rows or more in flat and depth): the bar says where you are.
  - **Decided** (P2): the indicator is a bar, not an underline as sim's first SPEC had it: the author's config says `▎` at the left, and a bar needs no SGR the terminals differ on. The window of tabs (`tabs::window`) keeps the active tab whole and adds its neighbors outwards, right first, then left, while they fit; a column is kept for each of `‹` and `›` where tabs are hidden, which are the hits `Prev` and `Next` (one file back or on), and a tab wider than the bar is cut at its edge. The bar is `tabs::layout`, which also says where each tab is, so that a click (`tabs::hit`) lands where it is drawn.
  - **Decided** (P1): a click on a tab ends what the file it leaves had pending (a count, an operator, a selection) the way a click in the text does, and does nothing while the command line is open (PTR-2); it never edits, saves or snapshots.
  - **Decided** (P2): the green `↯` is the file's marks of changes on disk (the same ones `changes on disk` counts in the status line, MARK-1's `disk` and the core's `marks`); it goes when they do (the core clears them when the cursor reaches them, DISK-6, and when a `:w!` or an `:e!` settles a conflict, SAFE-7).

- **UI-5** [P2][S6] The editor has a sign column of two cells to the left of the line numbers. The first shows the file's changes against `HEAD`, as gitsigns does: `▎` in green for added lines, `▎` in amber for changed lines, `▁` in red on the line above lines that were deleted (`▔` on the first line when they were at the top). The second is the core's change symbol (`~ + -`, LOOK-2) or, where there is none, the sign of a TODO comment (UI-7) and later an LSP diagnostic. The numbers are those of the author's `number` and `relativenumber`: the cursor's line shows its own number, left-aligned, in the primary gray and bold, and every other line its distance from the cursor's, right-aligned and secondary (in depth fading with the distance, like the text), in a column of at least four cells (`numberwidth=4`: three digits and a blank). In Normal mode only that number is lit (the author's `cursorlineopt=number`: a background on the line did not improve comprehension and can be noise, ICER'23); in Insert mode the whole line, every row of it, has a surface from the sign column to the right edge (`both`), behind the tints of changes (LOOK-2), the fence's band and the Visual selection. The sign column is always there (`signcolumn=yes`), and the view keeps eight lines around the cursor (`scrolloff=8`) — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P4): the signs come from `git diff --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-color --ignore-submodules=all -U0 HEAD -- <name>`, run in the file's directory in a thread of its own (`io::diff_thread`), through the same guard and environment as MARK-1's git (`io::git`: the config is read first, `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH`, `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT`, `core.fsmonitor=false`, `--no-optional-locks`), and the answer is `SimEvent::Diff(id, result, lines)`, `lines` being what the file had when it was asked for, since the signs are read against that count (`Diff::sign`). It is asked when a file is opened, after its save, when it changed on disk, and for the current file when git's status changed (a commit moves HEAD). Requests for one file that wait are one request (the newest). Until the answer comes the file has the signs it had, shifted as the highlighter's spans are for lines inserted or removed at the cursor; a file with no answer, outside a repository, with no commit yet, or whose diff fails, has no signs and says nothing (the status of git says what is wrong, MARK-1).
  - **Decided** (P1): a file's name is a path and never a pattern, and the diff never enters a submodule. Git reads the name after `--` as a pathspec, so a file named `*` (a glob) or `:(top)` (pathspec magic) would match the whole directory or repository, submodules included, and a file named `[ab].txt` beside `a.txt` would have the signs of both. Every git of sim's runs with `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1` (MARK-1), and this diff with `--ignore-submodules=all`, since a file is never a submodule. The reason for the second: a repository's own `diff.submodule = diff` is no key that holds a command, so MARK-1's guard lets it through, and with it git starts a child `git diff` in a submodule whose commit moved (and, if the submodule is dirty, one that diffs its work tree and runs its filters). `--no-ext-diff` and `--no-textconv` are flags, not config, so they do not reach that child, which reads the submodule's own config (`.git/modules/<name>/config`), a file the guard never read: its `diff.external` ran a script (measured with git 2.56.0, a witness file). Checked with a submodule whose own config names an external diff and a file system monitor, a superproject with `diff.submodule = diff`, and files named `*`, `:(top)` and `[ab].txt`: git as sim ran it before makes the witness (the control), sim does not, and the signs of `[ab].txt` are its own. The E2E case opens a file named `*` in such a repository.
  - **Decided** (P2): a hunk `@@ -a,b +c,d @@` is, as gitsigns reads it: `b = 0`, lines `c..c+d` added; `d = 0`, a deletion shown on line `c` (`▔` on line 1 when `c` is 0); else the first `min(b, d)` lines changed, and the rest, when `d > b`, added (when `b > d`, a deletion below the last line). The status line's `+ ~ -` (UI-10) counts them as lualine's diff does: added lines, changed lines, deleted lines. An untracked file has every line added, from the status of git; a file that is clean has no signs.
  - **Decided** (P2): the signs are of the saved file (the disk against `HEAD`), not of the unsaved buffer: sim does not diff text it has not got a file for. After an edit they are shifted as above and corrected by the next save.
  - **Decided** (P2): the numbers are rewritten in sim's composed row, in its own copy: the App's frame, which the mouse, the highlighter's looks and the tints read their lines from by the number in its gutter, is not touched. The cursor's line is the line of `buf.cursor` (the first match while a search is typed does not move it), found for every row by the number of the row that started its line (the rows after it are its wrapped rows; a row with no text is past the end). The first cell of a numbered row is still the App's change symbol; its number takes the next `digits` cells (at least 3), left-aligned for the cursor's line and right-aligned for the others.
