Outside the interface and in the preview of a content search there are no relative numbers and no accent: the core's own numbers, there being no cursor's line.
  - **Decided** (P2): the surface of the cursor's line is the 6% one (UI-1); on a wrapped line every row has it, the sign cell too, and the tints of changes and the fence's band lie over it (the first paint of a cell wins). The App's own `scrolloff` is its context of `min(8, (rows - 1) / 2)` rows (VIS-12): Neovim's 8 on a screen that has the room, so nothing changes there; a test keeps it.
  - **Decided** (P2): the sign column is `Geo::sign` (1 where the interface is drawn and the editor has 25 columns or more) cells ahead of the App's row, whose first cell is its own change symbol, so the column is two cells wide; the text starts at `Geo::tx()`, to which the cursor, the mouse and the pages count. The App is given the width that is left (`Geo::ew`), so no row is wider than the pane; a click on a sign does nothing (PTR-1).

- **UI-6** [P2][S6] Indent guides `│`, as indent-blankline draws them, in the lines' color, one at every indent level of a line's leading blanks, using the file's indent width (a tab is 4; spaces are 2 or 4 as the file shows); the guide of the scope around the cursor is brighter. Whitespace is drawn in a dim color: trailing blanks as `·`, a no-break space as `⍽`, a tab as `»` followed by blanks to its width. There is no mark at the end of a line, and no `-` or `%`: those are ordinary chars of code, and a mark must not look like one. Guides and marks take the place of the blank they are drawn on; they never change the text: the cursor, a click, the selection and the status line's columns are the file's — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the indent width is found for a file (`indent::detect`) when it is opened, saved or changes on disk, from its first 1,000 lines: more lines that start with a tab than with a blank means tabs; else 2 when the lines indented by 2 more than a multiple of 4 are at least a fifth of the indented lines, else 4. A guide is at column 0, w, 2w, ... of each level that the line's leading blanks cover (a line indented by 4 with width 4 has one, at column 0), not at the column where text starts.
  - **Decided** (P2): the scope around the cursor is, on the rows of the screen, the run of lines around the cursor's line (blank ones inside it too) whose indent is at least that of the cursor's line, and its guide is the innermost one of the cursor's line; a cursor's line with no indent has no scope, a blank cursor's line has that of the nearest line above that is not blank. A blank line draws the guides of the lines around it (the fewer of the two), in the cells past its end where the row has none of its own, from column 0 on. A line indented with a tab draws no guide: the tab is the level (the tab mark of `listchars` shows it).
  - **Decided** (P2): a guide is a glyph over the blank it stands on (`layout::Glyph`, found by the walk that finds the syntax looks, so it has the columns of a wide char before it right), in the lines' color, or the secondary gray for the scope's; a blank line's, past its end, pads the row with blanks to its column. Only the row a line starts in has guides, not its wrapped rows. The App's frame, which the mouse reads, is not touched: a click on a guide is a click on the blank under it. The scope is looked for up and down at most 300 lines from the cursor, a blank line's neighbors at most 50 lines away; none of this runs in calm or without the interface.
  - **Decided** (P1): indentation cannot overflow whatever the file holds: the columns that a line's blanks cover saturate at `u16::MAX` (a line of 70,000 blanks, or of 30,000 tabs), the product of a level and a width is checked (`checked_mul`, so a width near `u16::MAX` makes one guide and no wrapped column), and the guides are only made left of the pane's right edge (`indent::guides` takes the limit), so a line of 70,000 blanks makes no more guides than the pane has columns. A test runs those through sim.
  - **Decided** (P2): the colorcolumn is the author's `colorcolumn = 100` and the author's `ColorColumn`, the page times 1.18: a pale band, one cell wide, at the hundredth column of the text on every row of the pane (rows past the end of the file too), where the pane has 100 columns of text. It is made of the page and the secondary gray, as far from the page in OKLab lightness as `scale_hex(bg, 1.18)` is (and at least 0.02, so a black page has a step too), lighter on a dark page and darker on a light one (`color::stepped`): scaling a page that has a tint of its own, solarized's, would scale the tint into a hue, and a blue band would be the accent's (COLOR-1). It lies under every other surface of the cell (the cursor's line, a fence's band, a change's tint), and is not drawn in calm or in a content search's preview.
  - **Decided** (P2): the whitespace marks are glyphs of the same walk (`layout::colors`, UI-6's guides too), so a mark is where its char is after wide ones: a tab is `»` in the first column it is shown at (to the next multiple of 4) and blanks for the rest, a blank after the last char of the line (every blank of a line that is only blanks) is `·`, a no-break space is `⍽`, and a blank inside a line is a blank. All are dim (the lines' color) and drawn over the char, never typed: the buffer, the cursor and a click are the file's (a click on a mark is a click on its char). An indent guide wins a blank that is both. They are drawn only in the interface.
  - **Decided** (P2): the owner's call, 2026-10-17: the author's `listchars` (`tab:»-,trail:-,nbsp:%,eol:↲`) were matched exactly in Step D, and on seeing them in a file of code the owner found that `-` and `%` are ordinary chars there, so a mark that is one cannot be told from the text (and a `↲` at the end of every line is noise). `eol`, `extends` and `precedes` are not drawn (a line wraps), and the three marks that are are chars that no code has: `·`, `⍽` and `»`.
  - **Decided** (P1): the last row of an editor frame is the status line (or the command line) and no line of the text. A row's line is read from its gutter, the digits after the first cell, so that `:123`, `/123` and the status line of a file called `h264.rs` or `e1000.c` (`h264.rs  1:1  NORMAL`) read as the numbered row of line 123, 123 and 264, which a buffer of a few lines does not have, and the guides indexed the rope past its end: sim died when it opened `h264.rs` or when `123` was typed after a `:`, once the interface was drawn (found on 2026-10-18 by a test that started sim with a long `$TMPDIR`: its path made a command line that scrolled to digits). `layout::colors_with` leaves the last row out of the lines it reads (the other readers of a row's number already did, `changed`, or have nothing to index); `ui_6_the_status_line_and_the_command_line_are_no_lines_of_the_text`. The class, closed the day after (a review of that fix): a row of the frame that shows a number the buffer has no line for is no line of the text either (`text(l)` is none: a frame older than its buffer, a row that only looks numbered), so the guides, the window of lines that the highlighter is asked for and the spans are not asked about it (`ui_6_a_row_that_shows_a_number_the_buffer_has_no_line_for_is_no_line_of_the_text`), and `lead_of` of a line the buffer lacks is a blank one, not a panic of the rope (`ui_6_the_indent_of_a_line_the_buffer_does_not_have_is_blank`). The other places that put digits on the screen, the which-key window, the picker, the tree's names (`2024.md`) and the search's count, are drawn by sim beside the frame and not in its rows, so no row of the frame that reaches `colors_with` can show them; the test gives the function a frame that does.

- **UI-7** [P2][S6] A comment (the highlighter's comment spans) with `TODO`, `NOTE`, `FIX`, `FIXME`, `BUG`, `HACK`, `WARN`, `WARNING`, `PERF` or `TEST` in capitals, as a word, followed by `:` (and perhaps `(name)` before it) has that keyword as a badge: bold primary text on a surface, the colon included. There is one style for every keyword, since the word says which it is, and no sign: the sign column is for git and for diagnostics — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the colon is required, as todo-comments' default pattern has it (`\b(KEYWORDS):`), so prose such as `TODO list` is not a badge. Only the first keyword of a comment is a badge. The owner's call, 2026-10-17: todo-comments.nvim gives each keyword a hue (red, amber, blue, teal, purple), and in sim that would be five more meanings for the hues that already mean changes, the accent and the alert (COLOR-1); the badge is the card's neutral surface with primary bold text, and the rest of the comment keeps its comment look.

  - **Decided** (P2): the badge is found where the syntax looks are (`layout::todo_badge` over the highlighter's comment spans, in the walk of `layout::colors`, so it has the columns of a wrapped line right): the first keyword of a line that is in a comment, as a word (not after a letter, a digit or `_`), in capitals, the longest of those that start alike (`FIXME`, not `FIX`), with a colon or a `(name)` and a colon after it. Its cells are bold primary on the card's surface, for every keyword alike; nothing else of the comment, or of the row, changes, and the sign column's cells stay for git and the App's change symbols.

- **UI-8** [P2][S6] Markdown (`.md`, by the highlighter's syntax) is drawn as render-markdown draws it, with every char of the file still on the screen: headings in the heading hue (COLOR-1), bold for levels 1 to 3 and regular for 4 to 6, a first-level heading also on a band of that hue mixed into the page and a second-level one on a fainter band, with their `#`s dim; the markers of emphasis (`**`, `*`, `_`) and the backticks dim; inline code on a subtle surface; links underlined in the accent with their URL dim (calm: underlined, no color); fenced code blocks on a surface across the pane, highlighted with the syntax of their language, which is named at the right of the opening fence's row; bullets and the quote's `>`, drawn as `▌`, in the secondary gray; the pipes of a table drawn `│` and its delimiter row as `├ ─ ┼ ┤`; the text of a task that is done (`- [x]`) in the secondary gray; a thematic break as a rule of `─` across the pane — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P4): the looks of markdown are scopes of the highlighter, which already runs in a thread and caches per line (SYN-3): `Look` gains `H1`..`H6`, `Mark`, `Tick`, `Code`, `Link`, `Url`, `Bullet`, `Quote`, `Lang` and `Em`, and a line of a fenced block has a zero-width `Band` span at its start (a blank line in a block has no char to carry the surface). The main thread only turns each into a paint when a row is drawn, so the key costs no parse.
  - **Decided** (P1): nothing is concealed: a `#`, a `**`, a backtick, a URL are on the screen as in the file, only dimmer. (The read view, MD-1, hides the markers of emphasis and of inline code on the lines the cursor is not on, and `<Space>um` turns it off; what this line says is how the raw lines are drawn, and every line in `sim --plain`.) The `>` of a quote is the one char drawn as another (`▌`), in the same cell, the way a guide stands in a blank: the file's char is still `>` for the cursor, the clicks and the selection.
  - **Decided** (P2): bat's Markdown names the levels 1 and 2 only (`markup.heading.1`, `.2`) and calls the rest `markup.heading`, so `Syn::line` reads the level of the others from the line's `#`s (3 to 6). A heading's own look wins over emphasis, strong text and bullets inside it (the order of `Look`), and a link, inline code and a marker win over the heading's, so `## a `b` [c](d)` is a heading with code and a link in it. A look takes the colors of the syntax hues it stands for, so the Painter draws it with no help from `layout`, in calm too: bold for a heading, strong and level colors, italic for emphasis, underline for a link, the secondary gray for markers and URLs; the Painter now merges the paints of a cell field by field (the first range that has a color gives it) and fades a paint's own foreground, so a surface or a blue over a look still fades with the fog (LOOK-6).
  - **Decided** (P1): the Painter writes each color of a cell once, in one short SGR sequence (at most 19 parameters: attributes, one foreground, one background), the last that was meant (the style's, then the look's, then the paint's, then the fade). tmux 3.7c, measured with `-f /dev/null`, drops a whole sequence of more than 20 parameters and draws the cell as it was before it; the Painter wrote `0;38;2;primary;38;2;look;48;2;band;38;2;faded` (21) for the code of a fenced block far from the cursor in depth, so in the owner's tmux the commands of a ```bash block were plain and off the band while every other terminal drew them. A test counts the parameters of every sequence of a screen in the three looks, and an E2E case in tmux looks at the fence's code.
  - **Decided** (P2): in calm only those colors are drawn: no surface (the change is the only chromatic background there, P3), no bar and no language at the right, no blue. In flat and depth, with tones: inline code and its backticks are on the 14% surface, a fenced block (its fences, its blank lines and its code) on the 9% one from the first text column to the right edge, a link and level 5 are the blue accent, and a line past the end of the file has no band.
  - **Decided** (P2): the language at the right of the fence is the word the file has after the backticks, in the surface's row, right-aligned with a blank after it, dim; it is drawn only when it fits after the row's text. A fence with no language has none. Text in a fence is not further drawn as markdown (the highlighter's syntax for the language decides its colors).
  - **Decided** (P3): the owner's call, 2026-10-17 (COLOR-1): a heading is told from bold text by hue, its level by weight and band. In flat and depth every level is in the heading's text color (`Tones::head`, the cyan SYN-1 gives functions, |Lc| ≥ 75.5 against the stronger band, so against the page and the fainter one too): a paint over the heading's chars, laid after the link's and the code's so that a link or code in a heading keeps its own. Levels 1 to 3 are bold and 4 to 6 regular: the Painter draws every heading look bold (calm's, unchanged), so with the interface `layout::colors` gives the chars of levels 4 to 6 `Look::Sub`, which has the hue and no weight. A first-level heading's row is on `Tones::head1` and a second-level one's on `Tones::head2`, the page mixed 18% and 9% of the way (OKLab) toward the cyan (less where that would take the band farther from the page in lightness than the card is, 0.95 of it, so primary text keeps UI-12's floor on it), from the first text column to the right edge, like a fenced block's; levels 3 to 6 have no band. The `#`s stay dim (a marker, secondary). Calm is as it was: bold at every level, no hue, no band.
  - **Decided** (P2): a table is drawn as its text says: in a row whose first char (after blanks) is `|`, a `|` that the highlighter calls a marker (the scope of a table's cell separator, which only markdown has; a `|` in prose, or in a row with no leading pipe, is a pipe) is drawn `│` as a glyph over it (`layout::Glyph`, like a quote's bar; the file's char is still `|`), and the delimiter row (a row of only `|`, `-`, `:` and blanks, with at least one of each of `|` and `-`) has its `|` drawn `├` for the first, `┤` for the last and `┼` between, and its `-` drawn `─`, each the width of the char it stands for and the `:` kept, all in the secondary gray (the delimiter row's chars are markers too, `meta.table.header-separator`). Column alignment is not drawn (the read view's tables are, MD-3; this is how a raw row is drawn). Only with tones (flat and depth), as a quote's bar is.
  - **Decided** (P2): the text of a task that is done, `- [x] text`, `* [X] text` (a bullet or a number, a blank, the box and a blank, outside a fenced block), is in the secondary gray, as the highlighter's `Look::Done` over the chars of the text that no other look (inline code, a link, bold) has; `[ ]` is as it was. It is the owner's rank by lightness (COLOR-1: done or not), and it is a look, so calm has it too.
  - **Decided** (P2): a thematic break (`---`, `***`, `___`, or `- - -`, alone on its line: the highlighter's scope `meta.separator.thematic-break`, `Look::Rule`) is drawn as `─` across the pane, from the first text column to the right edge, in the secondary gray, over the chars it has. The first line of a file is never one: it opens YAML front matter (bat's markdown has no scope for that, and a `---` that closes it follows a paragraph and is read as a setext heading, which is not a break). A break inside a fenced block is code. Only with the interface; calm shows the chars, in the secondary gray.

- **UI-9** [P2][S6] sim wraps prose at the width of the editor pane in every look, as Neovim does, and not at 72 columns as the core does (`.md`, `.txt` and files with no extension), so the right half of a wide screen is no longer empty — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the one change in the core's `src/` is `App::wide: bool` (default false: the core wraps prose at 72, VIS-6 holds for the core), which view.rs reads where it had `72`; sim sets it for every App, the preview of a content search too. the core's cap was kept by folding two `let`s of `view::render` into one tuple, which changes nothing. The pane's width is what the App is given (`Geo::ew`), so no row is wider than the pane and a narrower pane wraps at its own width as before. The owner's first complaint on putting sim next to the author's Neovim was the right half of the screen left empty; the 72 of the core is the reading width of Bringhurst's finding (CONCEPT finding 6), kept in the core, where the screen is not the author's own.

- **UI-10** [P2][S6] The status line is one bar across the width with powerline separators ( and ) when icons are on, and none when they are off. At its left: a block of the mode (NORMAL a neutral gray block, INSERT, VISUAL, V-LINE and TREE the accent's blue: the word names the mode), the git branch with its icon, the file's changes against HEAD as `+18 ~3 -2` in green, amber and red, then the core's state words (`2 changes on disk`, `deleted on disk`, a filter's seconds). At its right: the security slot (the language server's state, ` rust-analyzer · sandboxed`, filled by the LSP's own code, and whether the root is trusted), then only what is out of the ordinary: the encoding when it is not `utf-8`, `CRLF` when the line ending is not `LF`, `BOM` when the file has one, and the file type when the file's name does not tell it (a script with no extension, whose first line says `sh` or `python`), and `line:col` in a block of the mode's color (the position follows the mode). Each fact is in one place (UI-18): the file's name is in the tab and in the breadcrumb, whether it is modified is the tab's `●`, and where you are in the file is the scrollbar's (UI-11), so none of the three is in the bar; where there is no tab bar (a screen of under 10 rows, or the tree's selected path) the bar keeps the file's icon, path and `●`, and where there is no scrollbar the progress (`Top`, `Bot` or `NN%`). A message, the command line or a search takes over the bar's text, as it did — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the sections are lualine's with its defaults: a (the mode) and z (the position) in the mode's block (neutral gray in Normal mode, the accent's in any other, UI-1), b (the branch and the changes) and y (the progress, where there is no scrollbar) on the 18% surface, c (the core's state words, and the file where there is no tab bar) and x (the slot, and what is out of the ordinary: the encoding, `CRLF`, `BOM`, the file type) on the bar's 10% one; a separator `` follows each section on the left and `` precedes each on the right, in the colors of the two sections it joins. The text of every block is `color::text_on` (|Lc| ≥ 75.5), the rest primary or an accent's text on the surface (UI-12); a test measures every run of text of the bar against the section it is on, on the eight backgrounds.
  - **Decided** (P2): the state words are the core's own, so that nothing the status line said is lost (P1): sim takes them from the tail of the core's status line (what follows `l:c  MODE`) and does not rebuild them, without `modified`, which the dot says. When sim has no bar to put in the row's place (a message, the command line, a search being typed, a content search, no file open, calm and LOOK-8's fallbacks) it shows the text as before, across the width. With more than one file open the bar says `2/5` before them (BUF-2; the tab bar of UI-4 is to say it later).
  - **Decided** (P2): what does not fit is dropped from the right, least important first: the slot, `CRLF` and `BOM`, the encoding, the file type, the progress, the changes, the branch, the core's state words; the mode block and the position stay, and so do the file's icon, path and dot where the bar has them, the path cut by `layout::short` to what is left. A screen under 24 columns, where that is all there is, shows the core's text.
  - **Decided** (P2): with the panel focused the bar has a TREE block in the accent, the branch, and the selected path (a directory's with its `/`), and at its right the row among the rows (`3/41`). Without a git repository there is no branch and no changes.
  - **Decided** (P2): the branch is the `## branch` entry `io::status` puts first in git's status (from `git symbolic-ref --short -q HEAD`, `HEAD` when detached; `git_status` itself is as it was) and `Marks::branch` reads it. The changes are those of UI-5's diff (a file git does not know has all its lines added). The file's type is what Neovim calls it (`rust`, `markdown`, `zsh`), by the same name rules as the icons (`icons::filetype`: chezmoi's names are read as what they stand for) and none for a file of no known type; the icon next to it stays where the type is shown. `CRLF` and `BOM` are the buffer's own format (`Buffer::format`), and the encoding is `utf-8` unless the file is not valid UTF-8 (the core reads every file as UTF-8, opens one that is not read-only and shows what is not as U+FFFD, SAFE-8, SAFE-4): then the bar says `not utf-8` at its right, and the core's `read-only: not valid UTF-8` in the middle is only `read-only` there (the state in one place, the format in the other; the core's own line, which has no right side, keeps the whole reason). No other word of the middle is said again at the right: `modified`, `CRLF` and `BOM` are taken out of what comes from the core, the file type and the encoding are not words of the core's, and the other reasons of a read-only file (a binary file, a directory, an unreadable one) are not an encoding. The core's own status line says `CRLF` and `BOM` among its state words (VIS-9), so the words that sim takes from it leave those two out, and the bar's slot says them once. A file's type is shown only when `icons::filetype` of its name has none and its first line is a shebang (`#!/bin/sh`, `#!/usr/bin/env python3`: `sh`, `python`; `icons::shebang`): a name with an extension or a known name already says what it is.
  - **Decided** (P2): the slot of the language server is `Buf.lsp`, which sim shows as it is (cut by the room it has), and whether the root is trusted is `Sim::trust` (none while nobody has said: nothing is shown; `trusted` in the text's gray, `untrusted` in the alert's magenta) and `Buf.lsp_alert` says that the server's text is an alert (a server that runs with no sandbox): all set by the code of the language servers (LSP-1, LSP-5) and never by sim. The server's own text wins the slot when there is one.
  - **Decided** (P2): the review under the owner's delegation (R4, R5, 2026-10-18): a fact is in one place. The bar had the file's name that the tab and now the breadcrumb have, `utf-8` and `LF` that are true of nearly every file (so they said nothing, and a `CRLF` among them was lost), the file type that the name and the highlighter's colors already say, `Top` and `NN%` that the scrollbar draws, and a `●` that the tab draws. What is left says what the other places cannot: the mode, the branch, the counts, the position, the core's state words, and the one thing that is not as it usually is. There is no pinned line of context above the text (treesitter-context's): the breadcrumb says which section or function the cursor is in, and a line that stays on top would take a row of the text from every file.

- **UI-11** [P2][S6] The right edge of the editor is a scrollbar, as the author's satellite.nvim draws one, in flat and in depth (in depth it is the box's right border, in flat a column reserved at the right end of the pane): each row stands for a range of the file's lines and shows the strongest mark in it, the git signs (green added, amber changed, red deleted: UI-5), the changes on disk (LOOK-4's marks) and the matches of a search (the accent, COLOR-1); a handle, a surface a step from the page (the author's `SatelliteBar`, the page times 1.8), behind the rows of the lines that are on screen; and a marker of where the cursor is in the file, one of `⎺⎻⎼⎽` for the quarter of its row it is in (the author's `SatelliteCursor`), in the secondary gray, or in the color of the mark it is on — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): row `r` of `rows` stands for the lines `l` with `l * rows / n == r` (LOOK-4's rule), and a row with several marks shows the strongest: an addition, then a change, then a deletion, then a match; a range of lines (a file git does not know: all of its lines) marks every row it touches. The cursor's marker is in row `l * rows / n` and its quarter is `(l * rows % n) * 4 / n`. A row with nothing in it is the box's border (depth) or blank (flat), a row of the handle with nothing in it a blank on the handle's surface. A click on the bar does nothing (PTR-1). In a content search's preview there is no bar's content: the file the editor shows is not the open one.
  - **Decided** (P2): the matches are those of the last `/` search, found only where that is cheap: a pattern that is a plain word (no regex syntax and no escape: a regex is the core's to run, and its marks are not worth a scan on the way of a key), a file of at most 5,000 lines, once for a pattern and a version of the text, and not while the text is being typed (the marks of the text as it was stay until Normal mode is back). The case is the core's rule for a pattern with no escape: smartcase, so one capital makes it exact, or exact when it was asked to be (`*` and `#`). The one change in the core is the visibility of `App::search` (it was `pub(super)`): the pattern is read, never written.
  - **Decided** (P2): flat reserves its column in `Geo::rule` (1 where the interface is drawn and the editor has 26 columns or more), taken from the text's width, so no row is wider than the pane. Without the interface, LOOK-4's ruler is what it was, in depth only.
  - **Decided** (P2): where there is no color (COLOR-2) the handle is not an inverse block. The block was the brightest thing on the screen, and brightest of all when the file fits and the handle is the whole bar, which says only that everything is shown. The handle is neutral structure (COLOR-1), so it is a shape at the weight of the text: a `║` in each of its rows, a double line beside the box's `│` and the marks' `┃`, with no inverse and no bold. The cursor's marker and a mark in a row of the handle stay as they are (the mark's `┃` is what that row says). The colors are unchanged: the handle is the surface a step from the page, as before.

- **UI-12** [P2][S6] Text keeps |Lc| ≥ 75 against every surface it sits on, on `#000000 #1e1e1e #282c34 #002b36 #ffffff #fafafa #fdf6e3 #eff1f5` (VIS-4's list): primary text on the cursor's line, the code surfaces, the tab bar, the active tab, the status line and the open file's row; the text of each role (a change's, the accent's, the alert's) on the card and so on all of them; the text of a block, the mode's gray block included, on its block — U
  - **Decided** (P2): the test prints the lowest |Lc| of each kind (`cargo test -p sim --test ui_color ui_12 -- --nocapture`). Secondary text (the numbers of the other lines, comments, icons) is the core's Lc 60 by design, and the dim of markers and URLs is secondary's; those are not held to 75.
  - **Decided** (P2): the heading's text (UI-8) is held to 75 on the page and on both of its bands, and a hidden char's alert text (UI-15) on the page, on the eight backgrounds; the test prints the lowest |Lc| of each.

- **UI-13** [P2,P5][S6] The feel of the author's Neovim, from the author's `init.lua`: the text a yank has just taken is lit for 150 ms (`on_yank({ timeout = 150 })`), in the accent's block in flat and depth (COLOR-1) and in the gray of a selection in calm; and `n`, `N`, `*`, `#`, Ctrl-d and Ctrl-u leave the line they land on in the middle of the screen (`nzz`, `<C-d>zz`) — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the lit text is what the App says it took: it reports a yank by its message (`3 lines yanked`, `5 chars yanked`) and by putting the text on the clipboard, and leaves the cursor at the start of what it took (at the start of the cursor's line for whole lines), so the chars are the cursor's offset and as many chars as the text has, in the buffer as it is. It is lit until the shell says 150 ms are over (`SimEffect::Flash(n)`: a thread sleeps `FLASH_MS` and answers `SimEvent::Unflash(n)`; an older flash's end does not put out a newer one) or until the text is edited, whichever is first; copying a path (`:copy`) is not a yank and is not lit. It is drawn in flat and depth, where there is an accent, over every other paint of the cell (a selection's gray, a badge, a tint); calm has no accent and lights it as a selection is (the next row).
  - **Decided** (P3): calm lights the yank as it lights a Visual selection (SYN-5): the neutral gray, lightness only, |Lc| >= 15 against the page, and the text on it |Lc| >= 75, over the syntax looks (the text of the lit cells is the neutral gray, plain, so it keeps its floor whatever the hue); where the selection is the core's underline (no truecolor, or no color, COLOR-2), the yank is underlined for the 150 ms, and in `NO_COLOR` that is all there is (no color is written; the cell has weight, underline, slant and inverse only). It is not the accent's block (P3: the change is the only chromatic background, and calm has no interface layer whose hue could say "here"), nor inverse (which calm gives to a char shown in another form, UI-15, and to the change where there is no color: a second meaning would make a yank look like a hidden char). The change style wins over the gray, as in SYN-5: a yank of words that came shows them as they are. It is the Painter's own `Look::Sel`, put in front of the row's looks by `layout::editor`, so there is no new color, no new constant and no paint (calm's `Screen.paints` stay empty).
  - **Decided** (P2): the step, and its grounds. The step is the selection's: on the eight pages of VIS-4 (`#000000 #1e1e1e #282c34 #002b36 #ffffff #fafafa #fdf6e3 #eff1f5`, measured 2026-10-18 with `Syntax::sel`) the gray is +0.450, +0.222, +0.189 and +0.211 of OKLab lightness above a dark page and -0.090, -0.091, -0.091 and -0.100 below a light one, |Lc| 15.1 to 16.6, with its text at |Lc| 75.0 to 76.5. (1) A floor in the field: APCA's |Lc| 15 is described as the absolute minimum for any non-text element that has to be discernible and differentiable (Steve Frenzel on the Washington Post design system's use of APCA, steve-frenzel.statichost.eu/posts/learning-about-perceptual-contrast; the University of Bath's blog on the new contrast measure, blogs.bath.ac.uk/digital-content-and-development/2024/11/08/the-times-they-are-a-changin-for-how-we-measure-colour-contrast; ServiceNow Horizon, horizon.servicenow.com/guidelines/accessibility/color-contrast; all secondary: the W3 draft itself, Myndex's apca-w3, gives the tables for text and none for patches). (2) A margin over the eye's own threshold: the cone system's Weber fraction, the smallest increment seen on a still uniform field, is 0.02 to 0.03 of the background's luminance (Davson 1990, quoted in Kalloniatis and Luu, "Light and Dark Adaptation", Webvision, webvision.pitt.edu/book/part-viii-gabac-receptors/light-and-dark-adaptation). The step is 25 to 28% of the page's luminance on the four light pages (eight to fourteen times the fraction) and 3.5 to 6.3 times the page's luminance on the three dark pages that are not black (a black page has no Weber contrast: its luminance is 0). That is the threshold of a still, uniform, lone patch; text on the patch and a transient make the practical one higher, which the margin is for. (3) Time: Bloch's law, that a flash is seen when luminance times duration reaches a constant, holds only within a critical duration, "of the order of 20-30 msec" at high photopic intensity, and longer for brighter backgrounds and smaller stimuli (Kalloniatis and Luu, "Temporal Resolution", Webvision, webvision.pitt.edu/book/part-viii-gabac-receptors/temporal-resolution). 150 ms is five to seven times that figure; the page gives no number for a bright background or a small patch, so how far above it 150 ms is on a white page is not known, and what is claimed is only that the duration is past the point where a shorter flash would have to be brighter to be seen. (4) A larger step does not fit everywhere: with UI-12's text (|Lc| >= 75 on every surface) the farthest neutral gray from the page that any text can still read on is at |Lc| 31.9, 30.1, 27.7 and 28.7 on the dark pages and 28.4, 25.4, 23.2 and 20.1 on the light ones (measured with black or white text, which no other surface uses; the selection's own gray text has less room), so over the selection's 15.1 to 16.6 there is 3.5 Lc of room on `#eff1f5` and 15.8 on black, and a step that is the same on every page cannot be much larger. (5) The yank does not carry the information alone: its message says what it took (VI-8), so the flash is the feel and not the only channel. What is not known: that it is seen in 150 ms by the owner was not tried on people, only looked at in the screenshots of the round (`ui-m-*`); the number is a floor from the literature and a margin from our own measure, not a result of ours.
  - **Decided** (P2): the centering needs nothing of sim's: the core already puts the match of `n N * #` and a half page's landing in the middle of the screen (the App's `center`, VIS-12, VI-14), also when the match is on the screen anyway. The tests pin it, since the author's keys rely on it; an explicit `zz` after each key, which sim first sent, changed nothing and was taken out.

- **UI-14** [P2,P5][S6] The matches of the last `/` search are lit in the text, as the author's `hlsearch = true` does: the matches in the rows on screen on the accent's block, the match the cursor is on (the current one) on the accent's solid color. `:noh` (and `:nohlsearch`) and Esc in Normal mode with nothing pending clear them and the scrollbar's marks of them (UI-11); a new `/` search, `n`, `N`, `*` or `#` light them again. Flat and depth only; calm draws none — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the matches are found by the same regex the core searches with (`regex-lite`, smartcase by the core's `folds`, which is made `pub`), compiled once for a pattern, in the lines on screen only (a few dozen on a pass of the frame: SPEED-1); a pattern that does not compile lights nothing. A match is found in the text of the line as the App wraps it, so a match across a wrap is lit on both rows; empty matches are not lit. The state is the buffer's: switching files shows each with its own.
  - **Decided** (P2): `:noh` is sim's (it closes the command line as `:qa` does, and the App is not given it: the core has no such command), and Esc clears the highlight when the App was in Normal mode with nothing pending, and still goes to the App. A search is `/` or `?` submitted, `n`, `N`, `*` or `#` in Normal mode with nothing pending, found by the App's `search` changing or one of those keys being pressed with a pattern set.

- **UI-15** [P1,P2][S6] A char that is shown in another form (SAFE-8's `^[` and `<U+202E>`) is drawn as the alert's block (magenta) in flat and depth and in reverse video in calm, in every state a cell can be in (selected, matched by a search, on the cursor's line, in a change from outside, in the fog of a far row, on a heading's or a fence's surface), so that a file which holds the literal text `<U+202E>` no longer looks like the real control char, which a reader could not tell from it — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P1): the core says which chars they are: `Style::Hidden` is a fourth style of a span (Deleted went with REVIEW), and `view::render` gives it to each char that `show` spells another way, whatever its style is (SAFE-8's Decided: an attacker's change around it must not hide it); `Term` draws it in reverse video, bold with no palette, in the primary color with one, which is calm's. The literal text `<U+202E>` in a file is eight ordinary chars: no span. The change added 3 lines to the core (view.rs 2, term.rs 1), and its cap went from 3,600 to 3,603 by exactly that, the owner having agreed on 2026-10-17 that caps move with a stated reason (SAFE-8).
  - **Decided** (P1): in flat and depth `layout::alerted` takes the spans of `Style::Hidden` out of a row of the editor and of the picker's preview and gives their columns a sealed paint of the alert's block (`Tones::alert_block`: the alert as the surface, with its own text color: a badge). A sealed paint (`Paint::sealed`) is the last word for its cells: the Painter draws them with it and nothing else, whatever look (the selection's gray, a syntax color), underline, fade or other paint they have (a search match's block, the cursor line's surface, a change's tint, a heading's or a fence's band, the fog). In calm the style stays and the Painter draws a `Hidden` cell as reverse video of the primary and nothing else, a selection included. The status and command lines, whose tokens are not a file's chars, are as the core draws them.
  - **Decided** (P1): the reason for "in every state" (a security review's finding on this item, 2026-10-17): a signal that holds in most states is one an attacker defeats by choosing the state where it fails (a right-to-left override written into a changed word, a long file whose far rows fade, a line that a search matches). So the test of each state is the exact sequence the Painter writes for the token, in all three looks: the plain text, a Visual selection (with the selection's gray and with only an underline), the current match and other matches of a search, the cursor's line in Insert mode, a word of an outside change (Style::Change around it), a row far from the cursor in depth's fog, a first- and a second-level heading's band, a fenced block, a quote and a done task; and in each the same text typed in the file is plain.

- **UI-16** [P2][S7] The boxes' titles are what the box is, with no number: the panel's top border says `files`, the editor's is the breadcrumb of the file and the place in it (UI-18); the badges of changes and of a conflict with the disk stay in it. A superscript number looked like a key and nothing happened when it was typed — U (golden), I
  - **Decided** (P2): this takes out `¹` and `²` and the editor's path from LOOK-3's border, which had them since the first depth (the editor's title came back as the breadcrumb of UI-18, with no number); the cut of the title to what the badges leave goes with it, and a pathological box shows the badges as it showed the title.
- **UI-17** [P2][S7] While the matches of a search are lit (UI-14), the status line says which one the cursor is at and how many there are, `[3/12]`, as Neovim's searchcount does: `[0/12]` before the first match, `[5/>99]` with more than 99, `[>99/>99]` from the hundredth on. In the interface it is a state word of the file's section, in calm it follows the core's status — U, I
  - **Decided** (P2): the matches are counted once for a pattern and a version of the text (up to 100 of them, `HIT_LINES` lines of text at the most, as the scrollbar's marks are), and the match under the cursor is the last one that starts at or before it: a key costs a binary search over at most 100 numbers. A file longer than `HIT_LINES` lines says nothing (a count that costs a scan on the way of every edit is not worth it).

- **UI-18** [P2][S10] The editor's title is a breadcrumb, `dir › file › section`: the file's path below the root, its components apart by ` › `, and the section the cursor is in: the nearest Markdown heading at or above it (its text, without the `#`s), or the definition it is inside (the function, `impl`, class, struct, trait, enum or module, by its name), where the file's kind has an outline. In depth it is the title in the editor box's top border, before the badges; in flat, which has no box, it is at the right of the tab bar where the tabs leave room; calm and `sim --plain` have none (the core's status line names the file). What does not fit is cut from the left, whole components first, with `…` (`… › src › md.rs › Read`), the section last of all; the directories and the separators are in the secondary gray, the file and the section in the text's own, bold for the file, with no color of their own and no number (UI-16). The tab bar and the breadcrumb both name the file (the tab says it is open, which others are, and whether it is modified) — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the section of a Markdown file is found going up from the cursor's line, at most 400 lines, through lines that are `#` to `######` and a blank and text: a fenced block's lines are not headings (a `#` there is a comment), which the scan knows by the fence lines it crosses, starting from the highlighter's own word on the cursor's line (the zero-width band of a fenced line, SYN-3) when it has one; for a cursor on a fence line itself it is the word on the line above that says which fence it is (a closing one has the block above it, an opening one has not). Further up there is none: a section longer than that has no name here, and says nothing rather than a wrong one.
  - **Decided** (P2): the section of code is the nearest line above the cursor (or on it) that starts a definition and is indented less than the cursor's own line (blank lines and the cursor's own line do not count), found by the indentation of the lines between, at most 400 lines up, and by the words the language starts a definition with: Rust `fn`, `impl`, `struct`, `enum`, `trait`, `mod`, Python and Ruby `def`, `class`, Ruby `module`, JavaScript and TypeScript `function`, `class`, `interface`, Go `func`, `type`, shell and Lua `function` and `name()`; after `pub`, `async`, `unsafe`, `export`, `const`, `default` and the like. The name is what follows the word (an `impl`'s is `impl Foo` or `impl Trait for Foo`, as the line says, up to the brace). A file of another kind, or a cursor that is in no definition, has no section: the breadcrumb is the path. It is a few string tests, not a parse: a definition the layout of the file hides from it (all on one line, or indented unlike its body) is not found, and none is made up.
  - **Decided** (P1): the parts of the breadcrumb hold anything a directory, a heading or a definition's name holds, so `crumb::parts` makes each fit to be drawn by the rule of `fit` (a control char, a bidirectional or a zero-width one is U+FFFD), which is what `cut` measures, and both drawings (the border's title and flat's tab bar) draw what `fit` gives and not what they were handed: neither trusts the other. A test draws both with parts that are not clean and one with a directory and a heading that hold escape sequences, and an E2E case checks that the terminal's title, which `OSC 0` would set, is untouched.
  - **Decided** (P4): one breadcrumb is made for a frame, kept for the file's version and the cursor's line, so a key that does not move the cursor to another line or change the text costs nothing, and one that does costs the lines it looks at (400 at the most), read on the rope without a copy: only a line that may start a definition or a heading is cut at 200 chars and read (measured in SPEED-1's Decided of 2026-10-18).
- **UI-19** [P2,P5][S10] A write from outside is seen when it arrives: the lines that another process has just written into the open file (a change from the disk that is taken in, DISK-2, or one that conflicts with what is typed, SAFE-7) are lit for 400 ms, the whole text of each line in the change's green as a solid block, bold (bold on inverse where there is no color, COLOR-2), over every other paint of their cells, and then are as they were, with the marks of DISK-6 (the tint, the symbol in the gutter, the tick in the scrollbar, the badge of the box), which are what stays. The marks are what remains once the eyes are back; the flash is what makes them come back. Flat and depth; calm draws none (the yank's, UI-13, is lit in calm as a selection is: the write has the marks that stay, DISK-6, and no gray of the Painter's own would say "changed") — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the lines are the ones the merge marked as written: the core lists them (`App::wrote`, the lines of the new text that got a mark of `+` or `~`, a line of a conflict included, since the disk changed it too), and the shell reads the list after every event and clears it. That field is the one change in the core (a line, not a feature). A deletion point has no text and is not lit. Only what the user did not do is lit: undo, redo, a filter's output (FILT-2) and a save's own echo (the same bytes: no marks) light nothing.
  - **Decided** (P2): 400 ms, not the yank's 150 (`WROTE_MS`, one constant). A yank comes from a key and the eyes are on the text; a write from outside comes from nowhere and the eyes may be anywhere, and a glance takes a few hundred milliseconds, so a flash as short as the yank's can fall between two of them. 400 ms is longer than a glance and short enough to read as an event and not as a state; the marks keep the rest. It is a reasoned figure, not one measured on a person.
  - **Decided** (P2): the whole text of each line is lit, not the words that changed: the words are on the solid green already (LOOK-3), and a flash of the same shape as the mark that stays would not be seen to change. Only rows on screen are lit (the scrollbar has the ticks for the rest). A yank's flash is over a write's, and an edit of the text ends both (the flash is of a version of the text, as the yank's is).
  - **Decided** (P2): the green is the change's text green as a background (`Tones::add_solid`, |Lc| >= 75.5 against the card, with the text on it), bold, and not the `add` block of LOOK-3: an added line is made of words that came, which are on that block already, so a flash on it would change nothing to look at. Where there is no color the words that came are inverse already, so the flash is bold on that inverse. The alert's hidden chars are inverse and bold too; they stay where a flash is gone in 400 ms and covers the text of a line, so they differ in time and in size.
  - **Decided** (P2): the paints of the yank, the write, the current match and the matches are put before every paint a row has, where they were put after: a changed row has its words and its tint first, and the first paint of a cell wins, so a flash would have been under both (the yank of a changed line was too, against UI-13's "over every other paint"). A hidden char's alert block (UI-15) is sealed and wins over them in the Painter whatever the order.

## Reading Markdown

- **MD-1** [P2][S9] The read view: in a Markdown file (UI-8) every line but the cursor's is drawn as it reads, in place and in the one pane: the markers of emphasis and of inline code are not drawn (MD-2) and a table is aligned (MD-3). The cursor's line is drawn raw, as UI-8 draws it, and so is every line that has a selected cell in Visual mode, so what is typed, moved over and selected is the file's own text, column for column. Nothing else changes: the file, the line numbers, the number of screen rows, the cursor and its column, a motion, a yank, a search and the status line are the same with the read view on or off; it is a drawing, made of the Frame's rows and the highlighter's spans by `md.rs` and `layout.rs`, and no key reaches it — U (the plan), I, E
  - **Decided** (P1): a line is read only when the highlighter's spans for it are known (the last ones, shifted for the lines inserted or removed at the cursor, as the colors are, SYN-3; spans that are a moment old cannot hide a char that is not markup, because MD-2 looks at the char too), and it is not a line of a fenced block (a `Band` span), of YAML front matter (a first line `---` up to the next `---` or `...` within the first 200 lines: the highlighter has no scope for it, and a `*` in a value is a value). Until they arrive the line is raw: the first frame of a file is raw and the next is read, and a line the highlighter does not know about is never guessed at.
  - **Decided** (P2): the cursor's line is raw whole, wrapped rows and all. The other rows of a table keep their columns while it is on one of them: the width of a column is that of all the rows, the cursor's included, so the columns do not shift as the cursor goes through a table (the raw row itself is as typed, and its pipes stand where it puts them).
  - **Decided** (P2): headings keep their `#`s, dim (UI-8); a link keeps its brackets, its text in the accent underlined and its URL dim; bullets, checkboxes and the quote's bar are as UI-8 draws them; a thematic break is the rule of `─` across the pane, and the first line of a file is not one (UI-8); a fenced block is as UI-8 draws it, fences and all. The read view takes away only what MD-2 lists.
- **MD-2** [P1][S9] The read view hides markup and never content. The chars that are not drawn are exactly: a `*` or a `_` in a run that the highlighter calls a marker of emphasis (the run is all one char), a backtick in a run that it calls the marker of inline code, what MD-3 replaces in a table (the blanks around a cell's text, and the delimiter row's `-` and `:`), and the chars of a callout's marker, `[!NOTE]`, which MD-11 replaces by the kind's word. Nothing else: not a heading's `#`s, a fence's backticks, a link's brackets or URL, an escape's backslash, a pipe, a bullet, a checkbox, a char of text, and no char that the highlighter calls something else. The check is on the char as well as on the look: a char that is not one of these is never left out, whatever a span says, so spans that are old or wrong cannot hide a char. A line that would be left with nothing but blanks is raw. A marker next to U+200D or U+FE0F is not hidden (the cluster's width must stay what the core gives it). A char that is shown in another form (SAFE-8: a control, a bidi control, a zero-width char) is drawn as the alert's block (UI-15) in every state of the read view, in its own cell: inside emphasis, a strong span, inline code, a link's text, a URL, a heading, a table's cell, beside or between hidden markers, and at the edge of a cell — U (a property test: no plan leaves out any other char), I (one case for each construct above)
  - **Decided** (P1): every char that a plan leaves out passes through one function (`md::may_hide`), which takes the char and why: markup (`*`, `_`, backtick), a blank of a table, a rule char of a delimiter row, or a char of a callout's marker (`[`, `!`, `]` and ASCII letters, in a marker that is exactly one of the five kinds, MD-11). A hostile char is none of them, so a plan cannot be made to hide one, and a test that tries each construct with each of SAFE-8's chars checks the screen (the token, the alert block in the same cell) and not only the plan.
- **MD-3** [P2][S9] A table is aligned by display width, as the core's `width` counts (a Japanese char is two columns, a SAFE-8 token the columns of its spelling): each column is as wide as its widest cell, in all of the table's rows, with a blank on each side of the text; the text is aligned as the delimiter row says (`:--` and no colon left, `:-:` center, `--:` right, in the header too); the pipes are `│` and the delimiter row is `├─┼─┤` of the widths of the cells (its colons are not drawn: the alignment is seen in the cells). A table is a run of lines that start with `|` (after at most three blanks), whose second line is a delimiter row of as many cells as the first has, that the highlighter calls a table (a marker in the delimiter row) and that is not in a fence; a cell ends at a `|` that a backslash does not escape; a row with more cells than the header has is not a table (a cell would be lost), and a row with fewer is shown with the cells it has — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): no top and bottom rule: a rule above and below the table would be rows that the file does not have, and the rows of the screen are the lines of the file (MD-1). The header is not bold: the delimiter row below it says it is one.
  - **Decided** (P2): a cell's text with its markers hidden is what is measured and aligned (`**a**` is one column). The cell of an empty `||` is blank. A tab in a table makes it raw (the width of a tab depends on the column it is in, which is what moves).
- **MD-4** [P1][S9] A table that cannot be drawn whole stays raw, drawn as UI-8 draws it, nothing cut and nothing hidden: one that is wider aligned than the pane has (the core wraps a long line, and a wrapped row cannot be aligned with the others), one that has a row which the core wraps, one with a row the highlighter has no spans for (raw until they come: the window that is asked for is made to hold the whole table, up to its 400 rows), one whose rows are not indented alike, one longer than 400 rows, and a run of lines that start with a pipe that is no table at all (no delimiter row): every line of such a run is as UI-8 draws it, markers and all — U, I, E
  - **Decided** (P2): the owner asked that a table wider than the window scroll with its line, as it does now. The editor has no horizontal scroll (the core wraps every line, VIS-7), so what the table does now is wrap, and a wrapped table is read raw. Horizontal scrolling would be a change of the core's view and of PTR-1, and is not made for this.
- **MD-5** [P5][S9] `<Space>um` turns the read view on and off for every Markdown file, and the status line says `markdown: read view` or `markdown: raw`. It is an entry of the key table (KEY-1), under `<Space>u` (the interface's toggles), so which-key and `:help` show it. The choice is remembered in the state directory (`md`, `on` or `off`, like the look's, LOOK-1); the first run is on — U, I, E
- **MD-6** [P1][S9] `sim --plain` has no read view, and says so: plain mode is the core alone (PLAIN-1), and the read view is the editor's (`sim/src/md.rs`, `layout.rs`), not the core's, so a file is drawn there as the core draws it. `<Space>um` says `plain mode: no read view` and changes nothing; the `md` file of the state directory is neither read nor written, in the terminal's plain mode and in a privileged context alike — I, E
- **MD-7** [P2][S9] A click or a drag in a line of the read view puts the cursor on the char of the file under the pointer: the point is taken back through the same plan the line was drawn with (a point in the padding of a cell is the char after it). The line then has the cursor and is raw — I
- **MD-8** [P4][S9] A key in a Markdown file whose screen holds tables takes at most 1 ms at p99 (SPEED-1): the tables on the screen are found once per frame, and a line that is not a table's costs one look at its first char; measured in SPEED-1's Decided of 2026-10-18 (p99 of 158 to 656 us) — B
- **MD-9** [P2][G1] The window draws the read view with the cells and roles the terminal has (GUI-1, GUI-2): a table is a scene like any other, with its aligned cells and the surfaces of its inline code, and has a golden scene and a golden picture, rendered offscreen — U (golden)
- **MD-10** [P1][S10] A link whose text names an address that its target is not is marked, in a line of the read view and in a raw one alike, in flat, depth and calm: the target's chars (and, for a reference link, its text with its brackets) are drawn as the alert's block (inverse and bold where there is no color; in calm as a hidden char is, inverse and sealed) and not in the dim of a URL, so that a link cannot say one place and go to another and be read as the first. A text names an address word by word: a URL (`https://…`), a name that starts with `www.`, a host (`example.com`, `docs.example.org/guide`, `paypal.com.mx`, `192.168.1.1`, `localhost:3000`, the host of a post box's address), or a path (`/etc/hosts`, `./a/b.rs`, `src/main.rs`). The link is deceptive when any such word names a host that the target's is not or a host that cannot be read, a path that the target's is not (only against a target that has no host: relative, or a file of the machine; against one that has, the browser shows the host and a path claims nothing of it, and a path whose first segment is a host, `/good.com/login`, is read as that host), or when the target runs code (`javascript:`, `data:`, `vbscript:`). What cannot be judged is not trusted: a link, a text, a target, a line or a frame that is past a limit is marked without judgment, and so is a use of a reference whose text names a host and that no definition resolves, when one may have been missed. Nothing is left out for it: the read view hides no bracket, text or URL of a link (MD-2), and code is judged like the rest — U (`lie::deceptive`, `lie::line_marks`, `lie::Refs`), I, E
  - **Decided** (P1): the class: every construct of CommonMark and GFM that makes a link, and what sim does with it. A row that is judged or fails closed has a test (`sim/tests/md_link_class.rs`, `md_link.rs`, E2E `md_10_*`):
    | construct | verdict |
    |---|---|
    | inline link `[t](d)`: d plain, `<…>` (blanks and tabs inside), with a title, after blanks, tabs or a line break, with parentheses, with entities and escapes, with a backslash or a `%` in the host | judged (`md_10_an_inline_link_is_judged_in_every_form_of_its_target`) |
    | reference links, full `[t][l]`, collapsed `[t][]` and shortcut `[t]`; the text over lines | judged by the definitions of the document; no definition: not trusted where one may have been missed (below) (`md_10_a_reference_link_is_judged_in_its_three_forms_and_wherever_its_definition_is`) |
    | definitions: on one line, the destination on the next, with a title, in `<…>`, indented, in block quotes and list items (`>`, `-`, `*`, `+`, `1.`, `1)`, nested) | judged |
    | a document not looked through whole (past `REF_LINES` lines or `REF_CHARS` chars), a line that could be a definition and is not read as one, a label with a letter that sim does not fold | fails closed: a use that no definition resolves and whose text names a host is marked (`md_10_a_document_that_is_longer_than_the_scan_marks_what_cannot_be_resolved`) |
    | the text over lines, the target on the next line, a `](` whose `[` is out of reach | judged as the paragraph around the line (three lines before, two after, none blank); out of reach: the target is marked |
    | raw HTML `<a href=…>text</a>`: inline, as a block over lines, any quoting, any order of attributes, tags in the text | judged; no `</a>` in reach, a tag that does not end where it is read, and an `href=` whose tag is out of reach: fail closed (`md_10_a_link_of_raw_html_is_judged_inline_and_as_a_block`); a tag that goes over lines (`<a`, `href=`, `"url"`, `>` each on a line) is known to each of its lines by the walk, which asks the paragraph of a line inside one, so that the line that has the target marks it (`md_10_a_tag_of_raw_html_that_goes_over_lines_has_its_target_marked_on_the_line_that_has_it`) |
    | links in tables, headings, emphasis, block quotes, list items | judged (a line is read whatever block it is in) (`md_10_a_link_is_judged_wherever_the_block_around_it_puts_it`) |
    | an image inside a link `[![alt](src)](t)`, `<img alt=…>` | judged by the alt text; the source is not text, and a badge says no address (`md_10_what_a_reader_does_not_see_in_a_link_text_is_not_judged`); the pixels are out of scope: they are not in the file |
    | inline code, fenced and indented blocks | judged like the rest, not exempt (`md_10_what_a_highlighter_calls_code_is_judged_all_the_same`) |
    | brackets in a comment, a tag, an autolink or a code span of the line | every reading of the pairs is judged (`md_10_the_brackets_pair_as_a_renderer_pairs_them_and_every_reading_is_judged`) |
    | an autolink `<https://…>`, a GFM bare URL or `www.` name | out of scope: its text is its target |
    | an image `![…](…)` or `![…][1]`, a footnote `[^1]`, a task `[x]` | out of scope: not a link whose text can differ (`md_10_what_is_no_link_with_a_text_that_can_differ_is_out_of_scope`) |
    | a text longer than 1,000 chars or 128 words, a target longer than 2,048, a line past 10,000 chars with a `]` in it, a frame past its budget | fail closed |
  - **Decided** (P1): the forms of a URL and of a host: every way that a target's destination or a text's host can be written, and what sim does with each. A row has a test, `md_10_every_form_of_a_url_and_of_a_host_is_judged_fails_closed_or_is_out_of_scope` (192 forms, each a text and a target and whether it is marked), and the forms are what the self-attack pass of the review's third round tried (two hand-written rounds, 192 forms in all, each in the table and the test; and a differential fuzz of the targets against Node's WHATWG URL parser, 14,900 targets of six seeds composed from schemes, slashes, userinfo, hosts, ports and paths, which found the `file:` row and nothing else once its oracle's own errors were taken out):
    | form | verdict |
    |---|---|
    | scheme: special with two or more slashes of either kind (`https://`, `HTTP:\\`, `ftp://`, `ws://`, `wss://`, `https:///x`) | judged: the host is read as a browser reads it |
    | scheme: special with fewer than two (`https:evil.com`, `https:/evil.com`, `https:\evil.com`) | fails closed: the host is the document's or the next one's, not known; odd, whatever the text says |
    | scheme: opaque and known (`mailto:`, `tel:`, `sms:`, `javascript:`, `data:`, `vbscript:`, `blob:`, `view-source:`, `about:`) | `mailto:` judged by the host of each box (address, `to`, `cc`, `bcc`); `javascript:`, `data:`, `vbscript:` always lie; the others lie for a text that names a host or a path; a text that names nothing is not judged |
    | scheme: opaque and unknown (`search-ms:`, `ms-msdt:`, `ms-excel:ofe\|u\|…`, `c:\`, `a:b`) | fails closed as the known ones do: it lies for a text that names a host or a path |
    | scheme: not special, followed by `//` (`zoommtg://`, `intent://`, `ssh://`) | judged: it has an authority |
    | scheme: `file:` (`file:///x`, `file:@good.com:1/x`, `file://evil.com/s`, `file:////evil.com/s`, `file://localhost/x`) | judged: an authority after exactly two slashes or a share after four or more is a host; the machine's own path is a local file (relative), which no text that names a host says; a path with an `@` and a port in it and no slashes is no authority (found by the differential fuzz against Node's URL parser, which the first version of this row read as the host after the `@`) |
    | scheme: none (a relative path, `./x`, `docs/good.com`) | judged: honest when it ends with the text's host or path; the only form that is relative |
    | scheme: none, scheme-relative with either slash (`//x`, `\\x`, `/\x`, `\/x`) | judged: the host after the slashes |
    | scheme with a tab or a newline in it, in capitals, written with an entity (`ht\ttps:`, `HTTPS:`, `https&colon;`, `java\nscript:`) | judged: the tab and the newline are dropped, the case is folded, the entity is decoded |
    | authority: userinfo and the last `@` (`good.com@evil.com`, `a@good.com@evil.com`, `evil.com@good.com`) | judged: the host is what follows the last `@` |
    | authority: a port (`:80`, `:`, `:80x`, `:0x50`) | judged: digits (or none) are no part of the host; any other port is odd |
    | authority: `\` in a special scheme (`https://good.com\@evil.com`), `#` and `?` (`good.com#@evil.com`) | judged: they end the authority; a Markdown target is read as a renderer writes it as well (`\` is `%5C`, userinfo) |
    | authority: `%` in a host, an at-sign or a full stop of another script (`good.com%2eevil.com`, `good.com＠evil.com`, `good.com。evil.com`), a zero-width char in it | fails closed: odd |
    | authority: a trailing dot, case, `www.` (`good.com.`, `GOOD.com`, `www.good.com`) | judged: the dot (one), the case and the `www.` are folded on both sides; two dots are another host |
    | authority: empty or userinfo only (`https:///`, `https://@good.com`) | `https:///` fails closed (odd); an empty userinfo is the host that follows |
    | IPv4: dotted (`127.0.0.1`, with a port, with a trailing dot) | judged, as a host; a text that names an address of numbers has no subdomains |
    | IPv4: a single number, hex, octal, short (`2130706433`, `0x7f000001`, `0177.0.0.1`, `127.1`) | fails closed in the target: a string that differs from the text's is another host, though a browser reads one address (nobody writes it, and a different address is the lie); out of scope in the text: a bare number or `127.1` is not read as an address |
    | IPv6 (`[::1]`, `[::ffff:127.0.0.1]`) | judged in the target as a host in brackets; in the text it is not an address (the brackets split it) |
    | names: IDN as written or as punycode (`例え.jp` and `xn--r8jz45g.jp`) | judged: honest as the same string or as the punycode of each label; any other lies |
    | names: fullwidth (`ｇｏｏｇｌｅ．ｃｏｍ`) | judged: mapped to ASCII before the words are split; in a target it is odd (a browser maps it, sim does not trust it) |
    | names: mixed scripts, a look-alike letter in a label or in the top-level domain (`gооgle.com`, `google.cоm`, `ex\u0430mple.com`) | judged: an address with letters that are not ASCII; the real ASCII host lies too |
    | names: a homograph the same on both sides (`gооgle.com` to `gооgle.com`) | out of scope: nothing in the file says which one the reader means |
    | names: a dot of another script (`google。com`, `google․com`, `google﹒com`) | judged: mapped to `.`; a katakana middle dot is out of scope (not a dot to a browser) |
    | names: ASCII look-alikes (`paypa1.com`, `paypaI.com`) | judged: another host |
    | names: shared suffixes (`github.io`, `herokuapp.com`, `co.uk`, `amazonaws.com`, `surge.sh`) | judged: a subdomain of a host on the list or of its shape is not trusted; any other subdomain is the owner's; the residual is in the rule above |
    | names: `www.` (`www.good.com` to `good.com`, `www.github.io`) | judged: folded on both sides, and the host that is left is judged by the rule of shared suffixes |
  - **Decided** (P1): the work is linear and bounded, and what is past a bound fails closed (an availability review: `[a]` many times, `[` then `]`, `](` many times and the like took minutes on a line of 450,000 chars, and opening such a file froze sim). The brackets of a line are paired with a stack, every look-up is a binary search or a hash, a target that is covered by an earlier link is not looked at again, a target that no `)` or title follows makes no link and is read again at the price of its length from the frame's budget, and nothing is cut without being marked: a line is read to 10,000 chars (`syn::MAX_LINE`, the highlighter's own limit), and a `]` past them marks all that is past them; a link text is judged to 1,000 chars and a target to 2,048, a longer one is marked; a frame judges 512 links and 200,000 chars (a judgment reads a text and a target a few times, and the paragraph of a line with a bracket that closes nothing is paid for by the chars it reads), and the rest are marked, a line whose paragraph the frame cannot pay for whole; a document is looked through for its first 2,000 lines and 120,000 chars, once for a version of the text; a label keeps eight definitions and the ninth makes every use of it lie. A link that is marked for being past a bound is marked with no judgment, so an honest link in a file made of them is marked: past a bound sim cannot say. Tests: `md_10_no_shape_of_a_line_makes_the_walk_hang`, `md_10_no_shape_of_a_frame_of_long_lines_makes_the_walk_hang` and `md_10_no_shape_of_a_document_makes_the_scan_for_references_hang` (24 shapes, each within four seconds in a debug build: the quadratic ones took 15 to 21; a first version read the paragraph of each line outside the budget and took 1.6 s a frame of 60 lines in a release build), `md_10_a_line_that_is_longer_than_it_is_read_has_what_is_past_the_cap_marked_if_it_has_a_bracket`, `md_10_a_frame_that_has_more_to_judge_than_its_budget_marks_the_rest_and_does_not_slow_down`, `md_10_a_paragraph_that_the_budget_cannot_read_marks_its_line`; the release bench (`md_10_bench_a_line_of_a_megabyte_and_a_document_of_long_lines`) has the numbers below.
  - **Decided** (P1): code is not exempt. The first version took what the highlighter calls inline code or a fenced block for code, and a differential fuzz against two renderers (markdown-it-py 4.0.0 in its CommonMark preset with HTML, and md4c's `md2html --gfm`) found at once what that costs: a stray backtick before a link, a single backtick on the line above that pairs with the first of the line, a fence in a block quote followed by a line outside it, a backtick fence with a backtick in its info string, and an escaped `\!` all leave a link live in the renderer that the highlighter calls code or an image. Whether a span of the line is code depends on the whole paragraph's backticks, and whether a fence is one on its block structure, and any rule of sim's that is not the renderer's exactly is a rule an attacker chooses to break. So a link-shaped text in code with an address that differs from its target is marked: the price is the documentation of Markdown itself (the README of sim has marks on its own examples), and an unresolved use in code (`a[pick.id]`) is marked only in a document where a definition may have been missed (below).
  - **Decided** (P1): the brackets pair as a renderer pairs them, and every reading is judged. A comment, a tag of raw HTML (`<span title="[">`), an autolink and a code span bind tighter than a link, so a bracket in one pairs with none (`[good<!-- [ -->.com](https://evil.com)`): the pairs are made as written, with the tags, comments and autolinks inert (the grammar of CommonMark's inline HTML, `lie::tag`), and with the code spans of the line inert too (a run of backticks and the next of its length), each reading once, and the links of all are judged. The line alone cannot say which backticks pair in a paragraph, so the reading as written is judged too: a reading that the renderer does not make costs a mark that is not needed, a reading that it makes and sim does not would cost a lie that is not marked.
  - **Decided** (P1): the text of a link is what a reader sees, before its escapes and entities are read: an image is its alt text, when a source or a label follows its brackets (`![x]` alone may be text, and is left as it is) (`[![good.com](logo.png)](https://evil.com)` is marked, a badge `[![Build](https://img.shields.io/x.svg)](https://github.com/o/r)` is not: the source of an image is not text, and in the corpus below it was most of the marks), a tag of raw HTML is nothing and an `<img>` is its alt, a comment is nothing, and what CommonMark does not read as a tag is text (`<good.com>`, an autolink). It is judged written, with the escapes and entities of CommonMark decoded, and without the markers of emphasis, strikethrough and code (`go**od**.com`, `_good_.com`, `~~good.com~~`, `` `good.com` ``; an underscore with a letter or a digit on both sides is a name's and stays, as it does to CommonMark: `no_alloc.rs`), with the full stops of other scripts as dots (`good。com`), and with the tags that a browser reads and CommonMark does not taken out (`<b title="]".>`), and any form that lies makes the link lie. A word is split from the text at blanks, at punctuation and symbols that are not ASCII (quotes, dashes, full-width brackets: `“good.com”`, `good.com—official`) and at ASCII that no address has (`| ( ) [ ] { } < > ^ * ; ,` and the backtick), and at an `&` in a word that is no URL or path (`good.com&x`, `AT&T`; a URL's query is the URL's), and what is not a letter, a digit, a slash or a dot at the edge of a word is trimmed; a word that follows a label is read as what it is (`site:good.com`, `url=good.com`, `site::good.com`, `mailto:a@good.com`), unless what follows is a path or a port (`https://good.com/?to=x.com`, `good.com:443`, `12:30`). An email names the host that follows its last `@`, a port is no part of a host, an IP address and `localhost` are hosts. A text that cannot be read is deceptive: an entity that sim has no table for, or a numeric entity that no `;` ends, next to a letter, a digit, a dot or a hyphen; a char that shows nothing (a soft hyphen, a zero-width or joining char, a variation selector, a tag char) with a piece of an address on both sides, where it breaks one that a reader sees whole (`good​.com`; an emoji and a full stop do not); a mark that turns text around (U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069) next to a word at all; more than 128 words are not judged and are deceptive. Blanks, tabs and a line break after the opening parenthesis are skipped, and a `<…>` target is read whole up to its `>` with the blanks and tabs inside it. Tests: `md_10_a_text_is_read_as_a_reader_reads_it_and_not_as_a_tokenizer_splits_it`, `md_10_a_word_that_follows_a_label_is_read_as_an_address_when_it_is_one`, `md_10_what_a_reader_does_not_see_in_a_link_text_is_not_judged`.
  - **Decided** (P1): the target is read as a browser reads it (WHATWG), so that sim compares the host the browser goes to and a parser of another reading cannot make it compare another. Tab and newline are dropped and the controls and blanks at the ends are trimmed first. For the special schemes (http, https, ws, wss, ftp) two or more `/` and `\` follow the colon (`https:/\evil.com`), and the authority ends at `/`, `\`, `?` or `#`; fewer than two (`https:evil.com`, `https:/evil.com`) is resolved against a base, the document's own host, or the next one where there is none, so it is not known, and is odd whatever the text says (`https:good.com` is not honest for `good.com`); `file:` has an authority after exactly two slashes (`file://evil.com/share`), is a path of the machine with none, one or three (`file:///etc/hosts`, an empty authority), and is a share of a host with four or more, as Windows reads them (`file:////evil.com/share`); a target with no scheme that starts with two slashes of either kind is scheme-relative (`//evil.com`, `\\evil.com`); the user part is what precedes the last `@`; the port is digits. The host is lower case, without a trailing dot or `www.`. Anything else in a host is odd and is never taken for another: a `%`, a `\`, a control char, a blank, an entity, a char that is not ASCII (nothing is normalized: punycode, a fullwidth dot, a Cyrillic letter), a port that is not digits. A text that names a host is deceptive when the target's host is odd, as when it is another. The target is also read after the backslash escapes and the entities that CommonMark decodes (`\@` is `@`, so `https://good.com\@evil.com/` goes to evil.com and `https://evil.com\@good.com/` is judged deceptive too): the written and the decoded forms of the text and of the target are all judged, and any that lies makes the link lie. A target that has an entity before its first `/` (`javascript&colon;`) is deceptive whatever the text says: sim has no table of entities and a browser has. The target of a Markdown link is also read as a renderer writes it: cmark-gfm and markdown-it write a `\` of a destination as `%5C`, so `https://good.com\@evil.com/` is the userinfo `good.com%5C` and the host evil.com to a browser (found by the third fuzz), while in raw HTML it is a slash; both are judged for a Markdown link. A host that ends with `.` and the host of the text is the owner's of that host (`[nordaccount.com/manual](https://my.nordaccount.com/…)`), and `good.com.evil.com` is not.
  - **Decided** (P1): a target that has a scheme is never read as a relative path (the review of e1ee30b: `[good.com](search-ms:query=x&crumb=location:\\evil.com@SSL\DavWWWRoot\good.com)` was relative, ended with the text and passed, and Explorer opens a WebDAV share of evil.com for `search-ms:`, as in real phishing). Only a target with no scheme is relative. A scheme that is not special and is not followed by `//` is opaque, and goes to no host that a text can name: for a text that names a host or a path it lies, whatever follows the colon (`tel:`, `sms:`, `search-ms:`, `ms-msdt:`, `c:\`, a scheme that nobody knows); the text that names nothing is not judged (`[Open in VS Code](vscode:…)`). A scheme followed by `//` has an authority, as it had. `mailto:` is judged by host: the recipients of the address (split at `,`) and the `to`, `cc` and `bcc` of its query are read, percent-decoded, and the domain after each one's last `@` must be the host of the text, or every one of them lies (`mailto:support@good.com,x@evil.com`, `?cc=x@evil.com`, `support%40evil.com`, no recipient, none with a domain, and a header other than the body whose value has a line break, which starts a header of its own: `?subject=x%0d%0aBcc:b@evil.com`); the box of the text is honest (`[support@good.com](mailto:support@good.com?subject=x)`). Tests: `md_10_a_target_with_a_scheme_is_never_a_relative_path`.
  - **Decided** (P1): a subdomain of the host in the text is trusted only when that host is not a suffix that many share (the review: `[github.io](https://evil.github.io)` passed, and the owner of github.io does not own evil.github.io). A host is such a suffix when it is on a compact list of shared-hosting suffixes (`SHARED` in `lie.rs`: github.io, githubusercontent.com, gitlab.io, vercel.app, netlify.app, herokuapp.com, pages.dev, workers.dev, web.app, firebaseapp.com, appspot.com, blogspot.com, azurewebsites.net, cloudfront.net, readthedocs.io, wordpress.com, ngrok.io, ngrok-free.app, onrender.com, fly.dev, glitch.me, surge.sh, bitbucket.io, translate.goog, myshopify.com, wixsite.com, notion.site, substack.com, and a few more), has the ICANN shape `<generic>.<country>` (`co.uk`, `com.au`, `or.jp`, `ac.jp`, … with `co com net org edu gov ac ad or ne go gr lg ed mil nom ltd plc sch gob gouv`), is under `amazonaws.com`, or is made of numbers; it matches for itself or it lies. The list was chosen from the private section of the Public Suffix List, by how often a platform's name is linked to (a README names its host, a page of its docs, a project's page); the list is some 9,000 names and has no place in the editor, and no crate is added. The names of the list are hosts though their last two letters end a source file (`surge.sh`). A subdomain of any other host is its owner's (`[python.org](https://docs.python.org/3/)`, `[github.com](https://docs.github.com/x)`), and one with an empty label is not (`https://.good.com`). Residual: a shared suffix that is not on the list (`duckdns.org`, `herokudns.com`, `cloudapp.azure.com`, the suffixes of a country that are not of the shape above such as `nhs.uk`, and the rest of the some 9,000) has its subdomains trusted. Tests: `md_10_a_subdomain_is_trusted_unless_the_host_in_the_text_is_a_suffix_that_many_share`.
