The p99 of a key in depth at 240x60: 877, 722 and 936 µs for motions, insert and lit against 881, 717 and 940 µs before; at 80x24 282, 264 and 336 against 281, 261 and 338; MD-8's in depth at 240x60 657 µs against 640, whose worst key is 1.2 ms against 750 µs: the one key that looks through the document. Nothing moved beyond the noise; the worst p99 is 936 µs. MD-8's bench now also types in that file (`md_typing`: 150 times a word, with a definition and the use of an address on the screen, so that each key that changes the text looks through the document's first lines again): the p99 of those keys in depth at 240x60 is 390 µs, at 80x24 146 µs (calm 262 and 113), the p50 275 µs; the lines of the bench's text that have a bracket are fewer than the micro-bench's, whose scan of the 2,000 lines costs 250 µs (`md_10_bench_the_scan_for_reference_links_takes_well_under_a_key`), so a file whose every line has a bracket pays up to the micro-bench's 250 µs for each key that changes its text, and was not benched with typing.
  - **Decided** (P4): measured again with the second review's closing of MD-10's class (the document's scan, the readings of a line, the tags, and the text as a reader sees it; 2026-10-18, release build, AMD Ryzen 9 9950X, a machine at load average 8 to 11 the whole time, the commit before (e8af3bb) and this one run back to back, twice each, `SPEED_1_ONLY=motions,insert,lit` and MD-8's bench). The p99 of a key in depth at 240x60 for motions, insert and lit: 871, 750 and 951 µs and 857, 708 and 943 µs for e8af3bb in the two runs against 872, 719 and 937 µs and 873, 717 and 943 µs for this one; at 80x24 in depth 275, 248 and 332 µs against 275, 248 and 333; nothing moved beyond the noise (these files are Rust: the walk looks for a link only where the highlighter has one). MD-8's bench, a Markdown file with tables in which every line is looked at for links: the p99 of a key at 240x60 in depth is 688 and 678 µs against 670 and 698, in calm 414 and 412 against 399 and 397, at 80x24 in calm 173 and 172 against 166 and 167, in depth 225 and 224 against 218 and 215: about 3% more, which is what `line_marks` costs a line (the passes that find the readings of a line's brackets are skipped for a line with no bracket, and those for tags for one with no `<`). Typing in that file (`md_typing`), where every key that changes the text looks through the document's first 2,000 lines again for its references, is 9 to 12% more at the p99: calm at 80x24 999 and 975 µs against 890 and 869, depth at 80x24 978 and 989 against 897 and 898, and at 240x60 1.2 to 1.3 ms against 1.1 to 1.3 ms; the scan itself is 400 µs against 304 to 324 for the same 2,000 lines. The typing bench was above the 1 ms at 240x60 for both commits on this machine in these runs, and the 390 µs that e8af3bb's own SPEC recorded for it did not reproduce (the same commit measured 1.1 to 1.2 ms in the same minutes in which this one measured 1.2 to 1.3 ms); I did not find out why, and the bench's assertion (p99 at most 1 ms) does not hold for either on this machine at this load. What the scan costs a key is what the cap of 2,000 lines bounds, and a file with no definition and no use of an address on the screen pays nothing.
- **SPEED-2** [P4][S2] In a repository of 100,000 files, `sim` shows its first frame within 20 ms; the file list arrives later — B
  - **Decided** (P4): the first frame is drawn before the file list, git, the history, the highlighter and the ANSI colors are asked for anything: `Sim::new` answers with the tree's first read and the requests, and the shell draws, then starts the threads' work. Since the core's M5 the terminal's background is asked for first, by `term::enter`, and the first frame waits for the answer at most 3 ms from the query (the wait overlaps the work of opening the first file and ends at once when the answer is in; what has arrived by then is taken even when the 3 ms are over). Measured on a release build (AMD Ryzen 9 9950X) in a repository of 100,000 files (1,000 directories of 100): `Sim::new` and drawing into memory take 35 us, and the real binary started under a pseudo-terminal (`script`) writes its first frame 1.7 ms after the start, wrapper included (the wrapper alone, with `printf` in place of sim, takes 1.3 ms). The list arrives 30 ms later and git's status 150 ms after the start, in their threads. Measured again on the core's M3 (2026-10-16): `Sim::new` and drawing into memory take 24 us, the real binary's first frame comes 1.75 ms after the start (1.28 ms for the wrapper alone), the list arrives 31 ms later and git's status 32 and 70 ms after the start (two runs; the repository was in the file cache). Measured again on the core's M5 with S4 (2026-10-16, a busy machine): `Sim::new` and drawing into memory take 54 µs; under `script`, which answers nothing, so the 3 ms wait is in it, the real binary's first frame comes 7.05 ms after the start (1.28 ms for the wrapper alone); the list arrives 34 ms and git's status 32 ms after the start. With the core's `scripts/pty_bench.py startup` playing the terminal against sim's binary (a file argument, 50 runs): the first frame comes 1.82 ms after the start when the terminal answers everything, 3.67 ms when it answers all but OSC 11 and 3.71 ms when it answers nothing; the quit after `:q` takes 0.38, 0.64 and 100.7 ms (the bound). The startup keys (`+cmd`, `file:line`) are typed once the first file is read, so when there are any the terminal is asked for its colors after they have run, and not at all if they quit (`sim +q file`): that quit leaves nothing for the shell (VIS-4, E2E `vis_4_a_quit_at_startup_leaves_no_answer_in_the_shell`). `cargo test --release -p sim --test speed_2 -- --ignored bench --nocapture` makes the repository and prints them; the E2E case `speed_2_the_first_frame_comes_before_the_list_of_files` checks with a git that takes a second that the tree and the help show at once.
  - **Decided** (P4): measured again as one program (2026-10-17, release build, AMD Ryzen 9 9950X, load average about 12): in a repository of 100,000 files `Sim::new` and drawing into memory take 60 µs, the real binary under `script` (which answers nothing, so the 3 ms wait is in it) writes its first frame 7.65 ms after the start (1.39 ms for `script`, `sh` and `stty` alone), the list arrives 39 ms and git's status 77 ms after the start, in their threads.

# Part three — the window

The window (`sim-gui`) draws the editor's depth look for real: boxes raised from a floor, bands and chips on the page, windows that float over a dimmed scrim, with soft shadows. It is called depth, not 3D, because nothing is meant to look out of the screen: depth is there to make what is hidden visible (a char shown in another form is a rounded pill with a glow of its own color, a write from outside a green patch, everything behind the picker dimmed), and it is never decoration alone. It is a renderer and an input adapter. The editor, its keys, modes and model are sim's, and every cell's color is derived by sim's own APCA code from the six colors the window hands it (GUI-6, GUI-11); the window chooses none of its own for the text, the changes or the alert. Its principles, in CONCEPT: P1 the window can do less than the terminal it replaces, P2 it is faithful to the editor (the terminal does not change and the window keeps no rule of sim's), P3 depth shows what is hidden, P4 a key never waits for a frame, P5 it stays small. Tests of the seams are in `sim/tests/gui_N.rs`, of the window in `gui/tests` and in the modules.

## The editor's seams

- **GUI-1** [P2][G1] A `Screen` says its layers, in cells and from the lowest to the highest (`Screen::layers`, `Layer { kind, rect }`): the boxes of depth (`Box { focused }`: the tree's and the text's, which end where the status line begins), the text of the open file (`Text { sign, gutter }`: the sign column and the digits of the numbers with a blank on each side, 4 + 2 for a file of a thousand lines or more), its scrollbar (`Scroll`), the tree's cursor row (`Cursor`, only when the tree has the focus), the status line (`Status`) and the windows over the rest (`Float { modal }`: the picker is modal and which-key is not, and each has the rect of PICK-1's window). Calm has no layer but its windows, flat has no boxes, and on a screen too small for the boxes there are none; with no file there is no text and no scrollbar. The changes view (DIFF-1) is the tree's panel and has the tree's layers (its box, its cursor row), and HEAD's version of a deleted file (DIFF-3) is a tab and a text like any other. A window draws a surface for each and infers none from a geometry or from the identity of a color — I
  - **Decided** (P2): the layers are made in `Sim::frame` from the same `Geo` and `pick_win()` the cells are laid out with, so they cannot disagree with the screen; the terminal never reads them, and the Painter's bytes are unchanged by their being there (`tests/gui_1.rs`).
- **GUI-2** [P2][G1] A `Paint` says what its surface is (`Paint::role`), where `layout` makes it: `Heading` and `Fence` (UI-8's bands), `Chip` (the shown tab, a badge, the open file's row in the tree, a block of the status line), `Patch` (a change, a match, a yank, inline code, the letter on a tab, the picker's selected row), `Tint` (a changed row, the cursor's line in Insert mode), `Bar` (the tree's header, the tab bar, the status line), `Alert` (a char shown in another form and text in the alert's ink, UI-15), `Thumb` (the scrollbar's handle), `Column` (the colorcolumn), `Fill` (what a window fills with) and `Shadow` (the one-cell strip the terminal draws beside a window, which a window replaces with a shadow of its own). Two paints that differ in role alone are equal and hash alike, so the role never makes a row look changed to the Painter, and the bytes of the Painter are the same with every role taken away. A role is for a surface: what is only text has none (the changes view's `+n −n` in the colors of an addition and a deletion, its letters, a name cut with a `…`, the `HEAD:` of a tab's name), and in depth no background is without one — I
- **GUI-3** [P2][G1] What a cell finally is has one place: `paint::resolve` (style, underline, look and paint, with the palette and the syntax colors) makes a `Resolved` (the text color before the fog, SGR 31-36 where there is no palette, the background, the ordered attributes, the fade and whether it stops at |Lc| 40, the role, whether the char is hidden or selected), `Resolved::ink(page, fade)` is the text color a given fade reaches (never past |Lc| 75, or 40 for the numbers, and the secondary gray does not fade, LOOK-6), `Look::attrs` is the table of weights, slants and underlines and which look takes whose hue, and `Resolver` makes the cells of a row of a `Screen` with the same merging as the Painter's (the first range wins, paints merge field by field, a hidden char is itself and nothing else). `sgr` is `resolve` written as bytes, so the terminal and a window cannot disagree — I
  - **Decided** (P2): the rewrite of `sgr` on `resolve` was proved to write the same bytes as the code it replaced on every combination of style, underline, look, palette and paint's field (96,768 of them), and the goldens did not change; `tests/gui_3.rs` reads the Painter's bytes back, cell by cell, and compares them with the Resolver's on depth, flat and calm, on a light and a dark page, with and without a palette, in Visual mode, with the tree and with the picker.
- **GUI-4** [P2][G1] The start of sim and what is done with the Effects `Sim::update` returns (read a directory, open, save and watch a file, ask git for the status, the changes view's numbers and HEAD's version of a file, highlight, search, snapshot, run a filter, put a yank on the clipboard) is `sim::shell::Shell`, and the terminal's loop in `main.rs` runs it, so a window cannot do these things differently. `Shell::threads` runs each job on a thread of its own, as the binary always has; `Shell::inline` runs each at once on the calling thread and queues its answer (a pause on a prefix is over at once, a flash gone, a spinner stays, a filter is waited for 5 s), so the same input gives the same screen. What is the terminal's or the window's own comes back in `Out` (the last frame, the text for the clipboard, a quit). The start is `shell::arguments` and `shell::begin` for both, which apply the rules of plain mode (PLAIN-1), of the state directory (SAFE-6, STATE-1) and of git's hooks (GIT-1) in one place from a `World` the system says (`World::here()`) and a `Front` of what the front end brings, and `shell::signals` is the one thread that turns a hangup or a termination into the event that snapshots what is unsaved (SAFE-6) — I
- **GUI-5** [P2][G1] `Sim::wants_text` says whether the next key is text, and not a command: typed in Insert mode, on the command line, in the picker's query, in the tree's search, or the char after `f F t T r`. A window turns its input method on for it and off where a letter is a command (an input method that is on in Normal mode turns `j` into a composition) — I
- **GUI-6** [P2][G1] `SimEvent::Colors(background, ansi)` tells sim the background and the ANSI colors it would have asked a terminal for (red, green, yellow, magenta, cyan: 1, 2, 3, 5, 6), as they are, because a window has no terminal to answer OSC 11 and OSC 4. It does what the answers do with the same result: the screen after it is the screen after them, `Sim::asking` is false once all five are told, every open file is told the background, and a later one replaces the earlier (a change of theme at run time) — I
- **GUI-7** [P2][G1] `Input::event` is what a terminal event is to sim when no reply of a terminal is waited for (a window has none): the mouse, Shift-Tab and F1 as sim's own events, and the rest as the core's `convert_all` makes it (Alt-x is Esc, then x). `Input::feed` takes the same path after it has held back what could be a reply, so a window and the terminal decide what a key is in one place — I

## The window

- **GUI-8** [P2][G1] The window reaches sim through `gui/src/api.rs` and nowhere else, and keeps none of sim's rules of its own: no table of looks, no order of SGR parameters, no floors of the fog, no inference of a layer from `Geo` or of a surface from the identity of a color against `Tones`. A renamed or moved item of sim is one edit. Nor does it decide how sim starts: it builds no `Env`, no `Config` and no sim of its own, runs no `forbid_programs`, looks for no `rg`, names no state directory and catches no signal, and a world made by hand is for the offscreen path alone (a render or a test is not a privileged context), so that `sudo sim-gui` is plain as `sudo sim` is (PLAIN-1) — I (`gui/tests/api_seam.rs` scans the sources)
- **GUI-9** [P3][G1] Depth maps sim's layers to elevations, and every text lies on the page color sim's palette is made for. The floor is the window, darker than the page (`mix(bg, black, 0.30)` on a dark page, `0.08` on a light one), so that what is on the page is raised from it. A box is a rounded surface (radius 10 dp) filled with exactly the page, a rim brighter on the box that has the focus, a shadow 4 dp down and 16 dp soft; its border chars are not drawn. A heading's band, a fence's band, a chip and the alert's pill are on the page, one level up (radius 6 dp, shadow 1 / 4 dp; consecutive rows of a fence or a heading's colors are one surface), the status line is a bar of its own, raised, the tree's cursor row a rounded highlight in the accent at low alpha, the scrollbar a faint groove and a round, raised thumb. A window is a surface of its own at the highest level (radius 14 dp, shadow 16 / 40 dp) over everything under it; a modal one (the picker) has the content behind it dimmed by a 50% black layer (30% on a light page), which-key does not dim and the keys still go on; the terminal's one-cell shadow beside a window is not drawn. A char shown in another form is a fully rounded pill in sim's alert block color with a glow of its own color and sim's alert text color on it, and the same text typed in a file stays plain text. Guides, table pipes, rules, signs and the quote's bar are shapes to the cell's edge — I (`gui/tests/golden.rs`, `depth.rs`)
  - **Decided** (P3): every text lies on the page color and the floor is darker, instead of the boxes being lifted a few percent: sim proves its contrast guarantees (UI-12, LOOK-9, the fog's floors) against `Palette::bg`, and a syntax color at exactly |Lc| 75 would be 74 on a lifted box. The same cue of elevation, and the proofs hold on the pixels.
  - **Decided** (P3): the window is depth only; flat and calm are the terminal's looks. The layers of calm and flat are still said, so the window would draw whatever it is given, but it asks for depth (`Env.skin`).
- **GUI-10** [P2][G1] The input is the terminal's, from the same events. The window makes the `Event`s crossterm's parser would have made of the bytes a terminal sends and joins the binary's path at `Input::event` (GUI-7), so what a key means (Ctrl-h is Backspace, Ctrl-[ is Esc, Alt-x is Esc then x) stays sim's. Text from a key is the text the layout made (a dead key, a capital: `SHIFT`); Ctrl + a letter is the bare key; Alt + a key is Esc then the key; named keys are the named keys; Shift-Tab is `BackTab`; Cmd-V, Ctrl-Shift-V and Shift-Insert ask the clipboard and send one paste event; an input method's commit is the text typed a char at a time (a line break is Enter, a tab is Tab, other controls are dropped), and its preedit is kept, sends nothing, is drawn at the cursor as a chip with the text underlined, and puts the candidate window under it; the input method is on where sim wants text (GUI-5). The pointer is in cells (`Metrics::cell_at`, at any scale, clamped to the grid as a report would be): press and release at the cell under it, a drag only on entering another cell, no motion without a button; a wheel notch is one event and a trackpad one for three lines of travel; the size in cells is `Event::Resize`. Release events, a bare modifier, a dead key, Cmd + anything else and function keys but F1 are dropped. Closing the window is a hangup to sim, so what is unsaved goes to the journal (SAFE-6) — I (`gui/tests/input_to_sim.rs`, `input.rs`)
  - **Decided** (P2): one step short of bytes: a byte encoder in front of a second decoder would be two parsers to keep in step with crossterm for nothing. The two state machines of `Input::feed` that wait for a terminal's replies are not on the window's path.
  - **Decided** (P1): the clipboard goes through the system's own tools (`wl-copy` and `wl-paste`, `xclip`, `pbcopy` and `pbpaste`, found on `PATH`), each run with 500 ms to answer, so no clipboard owner can hold the window; a native one would remove the `PATH` lookup and is in NOTES.
- **GUI-11** [P2][G1] The palette is two themes, handed to sim as the one `Colors` event (GUI-6): dark is One Dark's (`#282c34`) and light Catppuccin Latte's (`#eff1f5`), both on VIS-4's list of eight backgrounds. `SIM_GUI_THEME` (`dark`, `light`) wins, then the system's, then dark. Everything else (the palette, the tones, the syntax colors at |Lc| 75, the change green and red, the alert) is sim's. A theme answers every query, so sim is not left asking — I (`theme.rs`)
- **GUI-12** [P3][G1] The fog (LOOK-6) is smooth in time as well as in space and keeps sim's floors. Each cell's fade glides from its old value to its new one (time constant 70 ms) and is applied with `Resolved::ink`, so for every value in between the text stays at |Lc| 75 or more against the page (40 for the line numbers), the secondary gray never fades, and the change style never does. The fade a cell is drawn at is sim's own `fade_to`, at the percent the animation is at — I (`gui/tests/golden.rs`, `anim.rs`)
- **GUI-13** [P4][G1] Motion never runs behind the keys and is off on request. The cursor glides from cell to cell (time constant 35 ms) and takes the shape of the mode (a block in Normal, a bar in Insert, morphing), with the char under a block drawn again in the page's color; the text's rows are compared before and after each screen, and a shift of k rows is animated over 80 to 160 ms with an ease-out, clipped to the text's box, the cursor glued to the text. With `SIM_GUI_REDUCED_MOTION` set to anything but empty or `0`, there is no glide, no scroll and no fog in between: every change is at once and a frame is a settled one — I (`anim.rs`)
- **GUI-14** [P2][G1] The window renders offscreen with the code it draws a window with: a studio (fonts, a GPU, an offscreen target, the animator, the scene builder and the renderer) draws the screen of a headless sim (the shell inline, keys from a script, a neutral demo project of invented files in a directory of its own) to a PNG, so the same input gives the same picture. Goldens keep the display list as text (`gui/tests/golden/scene_*.txt`: every surface with its rectangle, radius, color, shadow and rim, every run of text with its color; no GPU, no font) and the pixels (`pixels_*.png`) through the GPU with the bundled font on the software adapter, compared with a tolerance since GPUs round differently; `SIM_GUI_BLESS=1` rewrites them. A pixel test draws on llvmpipe only and skips, saying so, when there is none. `gui-shots OUT_DIR` makes the pictures of the scenes (the tree and Markdown, the picker, which-key, `:help`, the changes view and HEAD's tab, a hidden char, a light page, Japanese text with the input method's preedit, a dense screen, a frame in the middle of a scroll) and, with `--time`, the frame times of each on the GPU asked for — I (`gui/tests/golden.rs`, `demo.rs`, `bin/gui-shots.rs`)
  - **Decided** (P1): the GPU that draws the screenshots may also drive the desktop, and a hang resets it and ends the session. So: shaders without loops or compute (GUI-17), one render pass a frame, frames waited for one at a time, nothing run for stress, and the tests on llvmpipe.
- **GUI-15** [P1][G1] The files the window makes are not at names another user can prepare. The demo project and the offscreen renders' journal are in directories made by `mkdir` with a name of chance at mode 0700, checked to be real directories (not links) of the user's own, and removed when done; a link planted at a name an earlier version used is not followed. `gui-shots` has no default output directory (`OUT_DIR` is required), makes it at 0700 if it is not there, refuses one that is not the user's or that others can write to, and writes every file by creating a new one next to its target and renaming it over, so a link left at the target is replaced and never written through — U (`safe_io.rs`, `gui/tests/private_paths.rs`)
- **GUI-16** [P3][G1] Text is drawn from sim's cells. One char is drawn at the origin of its cell with its own color, a wide glyph centered in its two columns, bold a face of its own (a fake bold where a family has none), glyphs from fonts of the platform found by locale for the chars a family lacks; the glyphs are packed in an atlas of our own that is flushed and filled again when it is full, and the same glyph is in it once. The box-drawing and block chars sim writes its guides, signs and bars with (including `┆`, the changed line's sign) are shapes, not glyphs, so that lines join at the cell's edge in every font. The family the text is set in is, unless one is asked for, the first the system has of a short list of common coding fonts, in this order: JetBrains Mono, Fira Code, Cascadia Mono, Source Code Pro, Noto Sans Mono, DejaVu Sans Mono, Liberation Mono; on macOS then Menlo and Monaco; then the generic `monospace` (the first family its alias lists in the fontconfig files, as `fontdb` reads them, which is not fontconfig's own matching: `fc-match monospace` can name another); then the bundled DejaVu Sans Mono, so that no glyph is a missing box (which also makes the tests hermetic). `SIM_GUI_FONT` overrides all of it with a family or a comma-separated list of them (the first one installed is used, and none installed is the default), and `sim-gui --help` lists this and every other word the window reads from the environment — U (`font.rs`, `blocks.rs`, `metrics.rs`, `gui/tests/help.rs`)
  - **Decided** (P3): the default is a short list of common coding fonts, and the generic monospace after it, not the generic one first. `fontdb` reads `monospace` from the fontconfig files as the first family its alias names, and that can be a Courier clone (Nimbus Mono PS on the machine this was found on, where `fc-match monospace` says Noto Sans Mono CJK KR): a poor first impression for a code editor, and a choice no user made. The list is neutral (no one's own font, none that needs a license, every one of them a monospace font made for code or for a terminal), the first one installed wins, and the CJK fallback by locale (`SIM_GUI_LOCALE`) is cosmic-text's and is kept. Anyone's own font is set with `SIM_GUI_FONT`.
- **GUI-17** [P1][G1] The window can do less than the terminal it replaces. No webview and no browser engine, ever; its direct dependencies are a closed list (`sim-editor`, `sim-editor-core`, `crossterm` for the event types only, `winit`, `wgpu` with Vulkan and Metal and WGSL only, `cosmic-text`, `bytemuck`, `pollster`, `png`), no crate that can speak to a network is in the build, no `unsafe` (`#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, and clippy denies `unwrap`, `expect` and `panic`), the crate is not published, and only the binary `sim-gui` opens a window. `sim-editor` and `sim-editor-core` do not depend on any of it: their dependency trees are what they were, and `scripts/check.sh` fails if they change or if a window crate leaks into them — U (`gui/tests/limits.rs`), and `scripts/check.sh`
  - **Decided** (P1): a webview is the opposite of this product's attack surface (millions of lines of C++, a JavaScript engine, a network stack); the window must be less able to do things than the terminal, not more. egui and iced would be a second UI on top of the first, owning layout, text and widgets, where this needs a glyph atlas under our control, per-cell fog and bold, a dimming layer between two layers, scissored scrolling and shadows; Skia is the best 2D renderer and is C++ and a huge unsafe surface for rounded rectangles; glyphon is wgpu and cosmic-text glue for lines of text where a grid needs each glyph at the origin of its cell. wgpu is one safe API over Vulkan and Metal with validated WGSL (a malformed shader is an error, not a hang) and first-class offscreen rendering; cosmic-text is the pure-Rust font discovery, shaping, fallback and rasterization that a grid needs most; winit has the window, the keyboard with the text a layout made and the bare key, the input method and the pointer.
  - **Decided** (P1): the window has no config file (CONCEPT: no config files); what it can be told is in the environment, and each word has one effect. `SIM_GUI_FONT` (families, comma separated; unset, the default list of coding fonts, GUI-16), `SIM_GUI_FONT_SIZE` (dp, 15 by default), `SIM_GUI_LOCALE` (the locale the fallback fonts for CJK are looked for by, `ja-JP`; else the environment's), `SIM_GUI_THEME` (`dark` or `light`; GUI-11), `SIM_GUI_REDUCED_MOTION` (GUI-13), `SIM_GUI_ADAPTER` (a part of an adapter's name, for the offscreen renders and the window), `SIM_GUI_ICONS=0` (no icons even where the font has them) and `SIM_GUI_NO_STATE=1` (no history, journal or places on disk, SAFE-6). `SIM_GUI_BLESS` rewrites the goldens (GUI-14).
  - **Decided** (P1): the network-capable crates searched for in the resolved tree (`reqwest hyper ureq curl openssl rustls native-tls tokio async-std h2 http socket2 isahc surf attohttpc webkit wry tauri quinn tungstenite websocket smol`) are none; `mio` (the poll primitive under crossterm and notify, which are sim's own dependencies) and `smol_str` (winit's keys) are what the search finds. `tokio` is in `Cargo.lock` only as an inert target dependency of wasm32 (`wasm-bindgen-futures`, reached through winit's web backend) and is not in the tree of the host.
